## THE GUIDING PRINCIPLE

**If a behavior exists that isn't driven by brain state equations, it's wrong.**

Every piece of Unity's output must trace back to:
- **Cortex prediction** (ŝ = W·x + b) — what she expects
- **Amygdala valence/arousal** (V(s) = Σw·x, energy-basin attractor) — how she feels about it
- **Basal ganglia motor selection** (softmax over learned channels) — what action she takes
- **Hippocampus recall** (Hopfield attractor + persona sentence memory) — what she remembers
- **Cerebellum error correction** (ε = target − output) — what she fixes
- **Hypothalamus drives** (homeostatic gradients) — what she needs
- **Mystery module Ψ** (√(1/n) × N³ × Φ̂) — her consciousness level. ⭐ **Φ̂ added 2026-08-25** — integration, from `computePhi()`. Without it the formula rates ANAESTHESIA as maximal consciousness, because low activity reads as high unspent potential; Φ̂ is what separates it from dissociation, which is also quiet and is famously hyper-vivid
- **Endocrine layer** (⭐ NEW 2026-08-25) — ten chemicals on the one curve engine, released by **six nuclei that sense their own firing**, contributing additively through the same overlay substances use. ⛔ **Chemistry is what makes Ψ a variable instead of a specification:** without it `n` only moves when input moves

It has. **Building this after the walk would have meant re-teaching all of it.**

### ⭐ POST-PRESS WATCH LIST — the fields to read, in this order

Each is a **field read**, not an inference. Every one of these was built with its instrument in the same commit precisely so this list could exist.

⚠ **AND THE INSTRUMENTS WERE DARK UNTIL 2026-08-25.** I wrote *"the board renders BY NAME ONLY, so each new field needs its row in the same commit or it ships dark"* into `ENDO.14` — **and then shipped five batches without a single dashboard row.** `state.endocrine`, `state.introspection`, `phiState` and `phiRaw` all existed in the broadcast and **zero** of them appeared in `dashboard.html`. Caught while auditing the docs, not by the check that was supposed to prevent it. **Two panels and a Φ row now exist**, so items 3–9 and 11 below are readable on the board instead of requiring raw `/public-state.json`.

1. ⛔ **`[Brain] ENDO — endocrine layer online: 10 chemicals, 6 nuclei. brainstem cluster = N neurons`** in the boot log. **No line = the whole layer is absent** and nothing below matters.
2. ⛔ **`[Episodic] iter13 migration — added column endocrine_state / drug_state / age_at_encode`** — three lines, once. If absent on an existing DB, episodes are still being written without chemistry.
3. **`state.endocrine.glandsConsulted`** — must be `true`. `false` means the nuclei were **never asked**, which is different from quiet.
4. **`state.endocrine.glands.*.state`** — each nucleus reads `fired` / `quiet` / `blind`. ⛔ **`blind` is the one to chase**: it names an input she cannot read. `pvn: blind` means the amygdala readout never arrived; `son: blind` means oxytocin can never fire.
5. **`state.endocrine.chemicals.*.everFired`** — a phasic chemical stuck at `unmeasured` after hours has a **producer problem**, not a quiet life.
6. ⚠ **`state.phiState`** — `live` / `floored` / `error` / `unmeasured`. **`floored` would mean Φ has never modulated anything**, which is genuinely possible: `computePhi()` is entropy of the spiking proportion and `H(0.01) = 0.081`, below the 0.1 floor. **This is the single most likely dead instrument on the list.**
7. **`state.drugSnapshot.mechanism`** — must be `routed`. `direct` means the scheduler never got the endocrine layer and every drug is writing brain params the old way.
8. **`state.introspection.source.state`** — `starved` / `live`. ⚠ **`starved` is EXPECTED early** and is not a bug: she cannot introspect before she has lived anything. It becomes a finding only if it persists once episodes exist.
9. **`state.introspection.criteria`** — `kindsProduced` > 1 is the falsifiable claim that her questions vary with state. `unmeasured` just means she has not produced one yet.
10. **`ENDO-LIFE` line per grade** in the walk log — *"taught N body/chemistry words"*. Absent across every grade = the syllabus is not wired into the life track.
11. **`state.endocrine.puberty.level`** — must read `0` in the early grades and climb from grade 4 (age 9). ⛔ **A non-zero puberty level in kindergarten means the age feed is broken again** — that is exactly what `AGEPIN.1` was.
12. ⭐ **`[donor] gpu_init 'brainstem'` IN THE DONOR LOG — the hardest single confirmation on this list.** The donor log is the one place the new cluster's arrival is *externally observable*. Pre-press it initialises **7 clusters + `langCortex`** and there is **no `brainstem`** — measured on the live pod 2026-08-25, which is also how we know the server is still the old code. After the press it must appear as an extra `gpu_init`. ⛔ **And it directly tests `KI-31`** — the RE-PRICE claims "no sparse matrices added", verified **server-side only**; the donor's own init and upload lines are the missing half. If the matrix count or upload volume jumps beyond the ~850K-neuron LIF state, the RE-PRICE is wrong and needs redoing. ⚠ If `brainstem` does **not** appear while `state.endocrine` is populated, the cluster exists on the server and never reached the card — which would mean the nuclei are sensing tissue that is not being computed.
13. ⚠ **DONORS CONNECTING AT ALL.** `DREAM_MIN_DONOR_VERSION` was raised `0.3.7` → `0.3.26` this batch, so **this press changes who is allowed in.** A donor on an older binary is now REFUSED at `gpu_register` — deliberately, with a message naming both versions and the download link. **If donor count drops to zero after the press, check this FIRST**, before suspecting the brain: the log line is `gpu_register REFUSED — donor binary vX < min v0.3.26`. Pods self-update within ~5 min and the download page serves 0.3.29, so an active donor should be fine; a machine sitting on a stale binary needs to restart. ⛔ Escape hatch if it bites at a bad moment: `DREAM_MIN_DONOR_VERSION=0.3.7` restores the old behaviour instantly.

### ⚠ What would mean I got something wrong

- **`mechanism: direct`** while the ENDO boot line printed → `setEndocrine` did not run; drugs are unrouted.
- **Every nucleus `blind`** → the `brainState` assembled in `_tickEndocrine` is not reaching them.
- **`puberty.level > 0` before grade 4** → the age ladder is lying again.
- **Walk noticeably slower per cell** → the endocrine tick is not O(1) as priced, and the RE-PRICE above is wrong.
- **Donor upload log showing more matrices than before** → the "no sparse matrices added" claim was verified server-side only, and the donor side disagrees.

## HOW THIS FILE WORKS (the LAWs that govern it)

Full bodies in `.claude/CONSTRAINTS.md`. These four are the ones this file exists to obey:

| LAW | What it means here |
|---|---|
| ⛔ **LAW #0 — VERBATIM WORDS ONLY** | Gee's exact sentence goes into the task. Never paraphrase, rename, collapse, shorten or downgrade it. **One task per item in a list.** Dropping a word is a violation. |
| **NEVER delete task info** | When marking a task done, change the **status marker ONLY**. Every word of the original description stays — prepend the verdict, keep the filing. Anyone reading must see WHAT was done and WHERE, not just a checkmark. |
| **Append, never replace** | New tasks go at the **bottom**. Completed tasks stay where they are. **Never regenerate this file from scratch.** |
| ⛔ **FINALIZED before DELETE** | A task may not leave this file until its verbatim text is in `docs/FINALIZED.md` **and the write is verified**. That is how this reset was done: appended, checksummed, *then* cleared. |

**Status markers:** `- [ ]` pending · `- [~]` in progress · `- [x]` done (with its verdict prepended, original text preserved)

**Task-number placement:** T-numbers, session numbers, milestone IDs and "Gee" belong in workflow docs ONLY — never in source code, public docs, HTMLs or launchers.

## BLOCK-WALL INVESTIGATION — filed 2026-08-21 (from Gee's pasted console log, 11:44 PM → 12:57 AM)

> Gee (verbatim): *"do an investigastion.... tthere is alot of seconds being wasted... or am i thinking about all these blocks wrong??? there is something up? right? what could it be? write all your findings to the todo."*

**THE VERDICT FIRST: both.** The bulk of the wall is you reading the warn floor wrong — but the wall was also HIDING four real problems, three of them silent. The log decomposes into three eras with different answers:

- [x] **BLOCKREAD.1 — the reading guide + the noise itself is a finding.** **Era B (`_teachPredictiveError` + `_teachHebbian`, ~65 min of the paste): NOT wasted seconds.** Blocks of 250-400ms every 5-10s = the loop blocked ~3-6% of the time, and each block IS the teach math — chunked CPU Hebbian slabs landing just over the `DREAM_LOOP_LAG_WARN_MS=250` floor (`teachStageMax=hebbian:substrate ~245-500ms` = the chunk size, working as designed). ## CHECKPOINT ROTATION INVESTIGATION — filed 2026-08-21 (investigate-only, per the ask)

> Gee (verbatim): *"are the checkpoints cycled out, i mean does it only keep a few of them.... we cant have it storing hundreds of checkpoints... it should only keep the most recent 3 as we dont want a bunch of 5000MB checkpoint saves on the box.. so dont do anything yet, just investigate and write your findings to the todo and tell me whats up"*

- [x] **CHECKROT.1 — VERDICT: rotation EXISTS and the cap is already exactly 3. Nothing accumulates hundreds of checkpoints.** `CHECKPOINT_SLOTS = max(1, DREAM_CHECKPOINT_SLOTS || 3)` (`brain-server.js:895`); slot files are a RING — `brain-weights-v{saveVersion % 3}.bin/.json` — so v0/v1/v2 overwrite each other forever, and `_pruneStaleCheckpointSlots()` deletes any legacy v3..v9 at boot (`:947`), so lowering the cap self-cleans. The version numbers in the log (`v163…v189`) are a COUNTER, not files: `%3` maps them all onto the same three slots. **Disk worst case at the 12M cortex: main `.bin` 5.4GB + 3 slot bins 16.2GB ≈ 21.6GB steady, ~27GB transient during a save** (the async save writes a 5.4GB `.tmp` then renames atomically). The 5.4GB slot copy is additionally rate-limited to AT MOST HOURLY (`V_COPY_MIN_GAP_MS`, a third of the 22.5-minute disk-wedge's I/O was v-copies), and binary saves themselves are PACED to `max(10min, 4× last save's wall)`. Verdict: **the system already does what you specified** — closed as answered, with the two real nits below filed open.
- [x] **CHECKROT.2 ⛔ — the hourly gate can PIN the ring to ONE slot, making "last 3 checkpoints" a lie about depth.** The slot index is `_saveVersion % 3`, computed at copy time — but the copy only fires hourly while `_saveVersion` advances on EVERY save (JSON saves included). If the number of saves between two hourly copies is a multiple of 3 — and the steady cadences ARE: periodic checkpoints at 5min = 12/hour, 12 % 3 = 0 — **every hourly copy lands in the SAME slot**, overwriting the newest backup while the other two slots freeze at whatever ancient state they last held. Rollback coverage silently degrades from "the last ~3 hours" to "1 fresh + 2 fossils", and the dashboard's `checkpoints (last 3)` row reads healthy the whole time (lying-instrument family). Fix when approved = rotate on a dedicated copy counter (`_vCopyCount++ % 3`), not the shared save version. Verify on the box first: `/versions` slot timestamps — three CLOSE timestamps = rotating, one fresh + two old = pinned. → **✅ FIXED 2026-08-21 — Gee: *"do checkrot.2"*.** `_nextCheckpointSlot()` owns the index: advances ONLY on a real copy, initialises from disk (empty slots first, then oldest by mtime) so restarts resume the ring instead of stomping the newest. Found and fixed the pair-coherence half while in there: json + bin slot copies now land TOGETHER under one index (they were desynced — json every save, bin hourly — so rollback restored mismatched pairs). `/checkpoint` stops claiming a slot it may never touch. **Harness on real files:** old scheme `[0,0,0]` pinned vs ring `[0,1,2]`; restart with v1 oldest → picked v1→v2→v0; missing v2 filled first. Ledger: FINALIZED §CHECKROT.
- [x] **CHECKROT.3 — the JSON metadata slot copy is written on EVERY save, ungated, twice.** `saveWeights` writes `WEIGHTS_FILE.json` AND its `-vN.json` slot copy on every call (`:6017-6021`) — no hourly gate like the bin side. Today the JSON is metadata-only (grades, passedCells, wordFreq, gateHistory, dictionary maps — no matrices) so it is MBs, not GBs; but `wordFreq` + `gateHistory` + the dictionary grow with the walk (K→PhD), so the double-write grows too, and it happens on every phase pass + every 5min. Cheap now; worth a gate (or slot-copy-on-bin-cadence) when it is next touched. Also noted: **no free-disk guard exists anywhere in the save path** — RAMHEAD guards RAM before a save, nothing checks disk before writing a 5.4GB tmp. ENOSPC is survivable (tmp+rename is atomic, the main file cannot be half-written, weights stay in RAM) but it would fail every save silently-ish from that point on. A `DREAM_SAVE_MIN_FREE_DISK_MB` mirror of the RAMHEAD guard is the natural shape. → **✅ FIXED 2026-08-21 — Gee: *"and fix checkrot.3"*.** Both halves: (1) the every-save json slot write is DELETED — both slot files now copy together at the bin cadence under the CHECKROT.2 ring (the main `WEIGHTS_FILE.json` still writes every save; durability unchanged, only the growing duplicate is gone); (2) `DREAM_SAVE_MIN_FREE_DISK_MB` shipped exactly as the RAMHEAD mirror — default 8192MB, defers with a 2-min retry (never dropped), shutdown syncs exempt, `fs.statfsSync` typeof-guarded (silently off pre-18.15 = pre-guard behaviour). Smoke-tested on this volume. Ledger: FINALIZED §CHECKROT.

## AUDIT FINDINGS — filed 2026-08-20 (full-session review of everything shipped today)

> Gee (verbatim): *"Go over everything done this session and make sure ther are no mistakes and its all coded to do what we want"* → *"write the todo of what u find"*

- [x] **WDCLEAN.1** ⛔ **A deliberate shutdown can forge the watchdog's `STALLED` verdict — and it will happen on EVERY Update & Savestart.** The chain, read out of the shipped code: the SIGTERM handler (`brain-server.js:10974`) force-saves + writes the resume marker before `process.exit(0)`, and the restart paths await checkpoint saves before `process.exit(42)` (`:7684`); FINALIZED records the 5.4GB save carrying **two ~112s loop pins**. During that save the loop is genuinely blocked, so the heartbeat stalls >5s and `loop-watchdog.js` **truthfully** writes `.loop-freeze.json` `state: STALLED` — then the process exits **on purpose**, the file persists (deploy-excluded per STATEWIPE, by design), and the documented reading — *"a STALLED file after a reboot means the previous process never came back"* — is now FALSE on every clean restart. Same failure family as the whole ledger: the artifact is truthful at write time and a lie at read time. **Fix:** a `process.on('exit')` handler in brain-server.js that SYNCHRONOUSLY stamps the freeze file (e.g. `state: CLEAN_EXIT`, keeping the stall history) — `'exit'` fires on every `process.exit()` and never on SIGKILL/OOM, which is exactly the discrimination needed: the deliberate exits get stamped, the hard deaths (the true positives) do not. Residual micro-race (watchdog thread re-writing between the stamp and process death) is bounded by its 10s repeat cadence — note it in the comment. Also quiets the `[LoopWatchdog] still stalled` journalctl spam during every long save-shutdown, same root cause. **Not fixed in this filing on Gee's word — filed, not built.** → **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-20 — Gee: *"okay fix that then"*. BUILT as specified:** `process.on('exit')` stamp in `brain-server.js`, both guards in (only `STALLED` is stamped, and only if `observedAt` falls inside this process's lifetime — so a previous boot's hard-death evidence is never destroyed). **Verified by running all three paths:** real-stall + deliberate `exit(7)` → `state=CLEAN_EXIT exitCode=7` with stall history kept; a pre-planted previous-boot `STALLED` file → untouched; `SIGKILL`'d child with the handler registered → handler never ran, `STALLED` survived. The watchdog's on-disk note now explains its own semantics. Ledger: FINALIZED §WDCLEAN.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-23 (BOTH halves) —** **PHONPROG.1** (filed 2026-08-21 UTC / 2026-08-20 Denver, from the live walk) Gee (verbatim): *"can you give me an estimate of how much longer teachphonemeblending has left? like a percentage of how far along it is"* — **and the exact answer exists on the box but is invisible from every remote channel.** `_teachPhonemeBlending` prints a heartbeat every 5s with the precise cursor (`rep X/6, word N/1029, ~N words/s`, `kindergarten.js:8014`) — but it prints through `this._hb`, which writes **`process.stdout.write` directly (`curriculum.js:3587`)**, and the CONSOLERING console ring hooks **`console.log/warn/error` only**. So a 500-line ring pull during the phase contained **zero** heartbeat lines while the cursor printed every 5 seconds into a pipe only journalctl sees — and the box is dashboard-only, no SSH. Same instrument family as the whole ledger: the measurement exists and the channel eats it. Two-part fix, either half sufficient: (a) publish the cursor as **`_phaseWork`** from the phoneme loop (the purpose-built `{done, total, frac}` field the cell-progress bar already renders — `_teachVocabList` publishes its cursor exactly this way; this phase and `_teachWordEmission` at `reps:12` are the two long emission-lane phases missing it), and/or (b) make the console ring capture `_hb` output (route `_hb` through the hooked `console.log`, or hook `process.stdout.write` in the ring — but beware doubling every line in journalctl). Estimate that had to be derived by hand instead (three aligned donor-lane samples: 240 teach-ops/min ÷ 15-30 ops/word ≈ 8-16 words/min against 1,029 words × 6 reps = 6,174 word-passes): **~10-20% done at 89 min elapsed, ~6-11 h remaining** — a number the box could have stated exactly.
- [x] **WDCLEAN.2** `lastSeenBeat` in `server/loop-watchdog.js` (declared `:87`, assigned `:158`) is written twice and **never read** — dead variable left over from a draft of the recovery math. Behaviourally inert; remove it when WDCLEAN.1 touches the file so it does not cost its own commit. → **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-20 — removed in WDCLEAN.1's commit, exactly as filed.** Ledger: FINALIZED §WDCLEAN.

**Everything else re-verified clean:** all touched code re-passes `node --check` / `bash -n`; the TZ pin sits at `dashboard.html:910`, BEFORE the first `Date` formatter use (`:1184`), and the second script block contains zero formatters; no raw-ISO renders remain (the one surviving `.slice(0, 10)` is on an ARRAY of word candidates, not a date); `.gitignore`'s `.scratch-*` catches the poll dumps without touching the already-ignored `.scratch/` dir; the multi-root hygiene hook, the 47-exclude rsync, the `loopFreeze` state field, and the geometry verdict were each verified by RUNNING them — the last two live on the deployed box this boot.
- [x] **WALKPROG.1** `cellsPassed` was still `None` an hour into the walk while `subPhases` climbed normally. Expected for a fresh K walk at 425M, but unverified — if it is still `None` after several hours, the cell-completion path needs looking at, not the teach path. → **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-20 as NORMAL, on Gee's call: *"its doing vocab thats normal"***. Diagnosed live: 65 min into `ela/kindergarten`, `_teachWordDefinition` had burned **3,896,561ms across 988 calls (~3.9s/definition, 99.7% of the cell's wall-clock)**. `cellPhasesStarted: 0` is CORRECT — it counts **declared** phases (`curriculum.js:3485`) and the cell has not reached phase 1; it is in the pre-phase definition bootstrap, which at ~15.2 words/min means **~2.4 hours before phase 1 of 25 begins**. The teach path and the cell path are both fine. I had begun wiring `_vocabProgress` into `_teachWordDefinitions` (the bulk pass publishes no cursor) and **dropped it** on Gee's word — the verdict was the deliverable. Ledger: FINALIZED §THE FIVE THAT NEEDED NO PRESS.

## ARTSPACE — art formulations for scene space, revision, and variation — filed 2026-08-21

> Gee (verbatim): *"come on u need to use actual drawing and painting and art formulations for everything Unity needs to be able to do.. paint the floor, set interer rooms with perceptional refrences of space. fill in the sky and the ground shadow detail edit erase pasrts or lines to make images better to fit learned understandings as she goes so shes not stuck making the exact same cat image every time someone asks for a cat drawing"*

- [x] **PAINT.9** paint the floor, set interer rooms with perceptional refrences of space + fill in the sky and the ground — scene SPACE: per-attempt horizon + vanishing point; interior detection (room-class words or the definition naming an indoor space) → painted floor polygon with near/far value bands, wall fills, wall/floor junction, floor lines CONVERGING on the vanishing point + a room corner; exterior → full sky (2 graded bands) + full ground (2 graded bands) + depth-graded ground texture that shrinks/crowds toward the horizon and leans toward the VP. → **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-21, judged on renders: the kitchen piece reads as a ROOM (junction, converging floor lines, corner, floor bands); the beach + meadow pieces have full sky/ground with depth.** Ledger: FINALIZED §ARTSPACE.
- [x] **PAINT.10** shadow detail — grounding CAST SHADOW: a squashed dark ellipse where each subject meets the ground, light direction varying per attempt, drawn UNDER the subject so it stands instead of floating. → **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-21; judged live and fixed in-batch: the shadow now rides the STYLE-SCALED footprint (a doodle draws at 0.55 inside its box — the raw-box shadow floated below the feet).** Ledger: FINALIZED §ARTSPACE.
- [x] **PAINT.11** edit erase pasrts or lines to make images better — HER REVISION PASS (`_reviseComposition`): backdrop strokes are split at subject boundaries and the segments INSIDE any subject erased (occlusion — the subject is in front of the scene), plus near-duplicate stroke de-clutter; erased/deduped counts logged on the artwork line. → **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-21: the beach scene erased 28 place-trace segments running through the cat + deduped 3; counts verified on the log line.** Ledger: FINALIZED §ARTSPACE.
- [x] **PAINT.12** so shes not stuck making the exact same cat image every time someone asks for a cat drawing — VARIATION: 50% mirror-pose flip (mapX + all part angles), per-attempt stroke SUBSET (longest ~30% of the trace always draw — the structural read; the tail samples at p=0.85), and placement/scale wobble on every subject box — on top of the existing style rotation + mood seed + hand jitter. → **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-21, judged on renders: "draw a cat" twice gave a mirrored doodle and an unmirrored ink at different sizes and stroke subsets — same memory, visibly different artworks. (Verified at learn-time: the trace IS length-sorted descending, `visual-memory.js` sort, so the structural-30% math holds.)** Ledger: FINALIZED §ARTSPACE.

- [x] **LOOKTRUTH.2** the word blow was added to a drawing of a cat → **✅ two cuts:** act-primary gate guard ("blow"/"escape" only passed via WordNet cocaine-slang/garden-plant senses; strong object-class senses still rescue baseball/market/ride) + LABELTRUTH (zero-stroke subjects never ride the label/log/plan). Also answers *"outline tracings of her copies"*: construction leads from banked FORMS, the trace becomes one voice among several instead of the whole song. → **✅ BUILT 2026-08-21 on Gee's "build it", exactly as designed:** bank cap 3, PURE looks only (pre-merge snapshots, layout-deduped), `_formPick` verdict-weighted, `_recolorSchema` maps colors by nearest LUMINANCE so shading survives hue swaps (60% own / 25% other look / 15% definition), accepts credit the drawn form, `state.ownArt.formVariants`. 7/7 production-code checks + the recolored render judged. The donor holds the FULL training mass (CPU CSR is the sampled shadow) — probing THERE is both faster (one template + one ~40ms Montreal round trip vs seconds of CPU matmul) and TRUER (grades the real weights).

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — VERIFIED LIVE, AND THE NUMBERS ARE THE BEST CASE THIS ITEM COULD HAVE ASKED FOR: `gateProbes = { gpu: 1520, refused: 0, nullAck: 0 }`.** 1,520 cross-projection gate probes graded **on the donor**, with **zero** refusals (lane busy / no donor → CPU grades instead) and **zero** null acks (dispatch sent but no currents came back). ⭐ **The two failure modes this item was designed around did not occur once in 1,520 dispatches** — and both counters existing separately is why that can be stated rather than assumed: a bare `gpu: 1520` could have hidden a thousand silent CPU fallbacks. Corroborated in the same read by the teach lane it shares a donor with: `flushedOps 268,520` against `enqueued 268,520` (fully drained), `maskedSent 26,748`, `rangesSent 4,479`, `capFlushes 0`, `suppressedStale 0`. **GATEGPU.1** — cross-projection gate probes run on the donor: `gpuGateProbe(matrixName, srcRegion, indices)` (pattern write + bound propagate + currents readback, honest null on any shed/lane-closed so the CPU chunked path grades instead), wired through `_probePropagate` (the shared helper every grade's gates ride) + the direct gate call sites. Matrix-rows cap ≤4M (the readback ack is rows×4B — intra at 12M rows = a 48MB ack, excluded). Counters + kill switch `DREAM_GATE_GPU_PROBES=0`.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-23 (donor v0.3.28) —** **GATEGPU.2** — the intra SEQ probe + donor-side readout REDUCTION (bucket/argmax computed on the card so the ack is bytes, not megabytes) — unlocks the 12M-row intra probe and shrinks every ack.

## ARTZIG2 — filed 2026-08-21

> Gee (verbatim): *"something is up with her drawing... she is still using the zig zag patterning or scratch like patterning in her doodles and when she does water colors it all garbage images"*

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — rendered through the production pipeline, judged by eye over four rounds, and the cause was NOT stroke count. ⛔ THE BODY WAS THE BUG: the convex hull was fitted to `schema.trace` RAW, while the fragment gate ran later and only governed which strokes got DRAWN — so ARTZIG (2026-08-21) suppressed the fragments' INK while keeping their SHAPE, and the hull became the bounding box of the tracer's noise scatter. Judged on the renders: a RECTANGULAR SLAB in both hands, and on the pale watercolor wash the part-colour blobs sitting on that slab ARE the "colored garbage". Fixed by making the survivor set ONE owner (`_traceSurvivors`) consumed by both the body and the redraw — the square became an actual silhouette with ears, eyes and a tail on the very next render. Second visible defect, caught on that re-render: part-colour blobs FLOATING OUTSIDE the body (the part grid is a layout over the whole frame, offsets up to ±35% of a cell) — now even-odd clipped to the silhouette, because a colour layer belongs to the subject. ⭐ **ARTGROW — Gee's correction mid-fix (*"dont limit stroke counts too much cant make a art work in only 20 strokes it should increase in ability as she learns in art and stuff"*): my first cut made `traceBudget` authoritative at its OLD noise-defence values, which turned a noise fix into a CAP ON HER ABILITY.** Budgets raised (doodle 22→110, watercolor 40→170, poster 90→200, ink 120→160, pencil 150→260) and the effective budget is now `style.traceBudget × skill.budgetMul` — a SIXTH TRAINABLE param in the practice loop (`[0.6, 2.5, 0.25]`, default 1.0), scored like every other by cosine against her banked percept, so more strokes only survive a session when they made the drawing resemble the subject MORE. Measured: on a full 260-stroke trace the doodle draws 101 untrained → 235 practiced, watercolor 153 → 249; no fragment hairball at ANY budget, because the gate now works at the source. Also fixed in passing: the structural count was a FLOOR on the budget, so whenever it met the budget the two sets became identical — every stroke counted structural, which force-drew all of them (killing the PAINT.12 per-attempt subset) and gave every one the contrast value-shift meant for the structural read alone. Regression-rendered all five hands: five distinct signatures, pencil/ink untouched (mass `none` takes no fill/blob path). ⚠ **NOT claimed: that a 235-stroke drawing from a REAL reference is good.** The schema was synthetic (empty store, no live box) — realistic in the shape the complaint names, but its dense-detail strokes are synthetic swirls rather than real traced contour, so the renders prove the density is not fragment-scratch and cannot judge beauty. That judgement is a post-press look at real look-ups. Full entry: FINALIZED §ARTZIG2 + ARTGROW.** **ARTZIG2.1** — doodle still zigzags/scratches; watercolor renders garbage. Method per the intelligibility law: render both hands through the production pipeline to PNG, judge by eye, fix the named generator, re-render until legible.
- [x] **ARTZIG2.1** → **✅ FIXED over four judged render rounds** — the scratch was the trace's fragment tail (reproduced exactly with a 200-fragment synthetic tail; clean traces were legible in every hand): fragment gate (short+jagged = tracer noise) judging EVERY stroke with structural picked from survivors, per-style trace budgets (doodle 22 … pencil 150), the contrast probe reads the real underpaint ink, and lighten lerps to white instead of clipping orange→yellow. Ledger: FINALIZED §ARTZIG2.
- [x] **✅ AUDITED + CLOSED 2026-08-25 — THE WALK IS CLEAR. The hazard was real and it was already FIXED on 2026-08-21; this audit VERIFIED the fix instead of trusting its comment.** Four independent properties all hold: (1) **routing** — `gpuSparsePropagateAuto` sends bound names to `gpuSparsePropagateBound`, which detects a native donor and rebuilds the pre-index window from the server's own resident mirror, returning `null` (never zeros wearing a success shape) when there is no mirror; browser donors keep the empty-pre protocol. (2) ⭐ **the mirror IS a mirror** — the suspicion that it read a different array than the wire was WRONG: `_writeTiledPattern` (`curriculum.js:11848`) writes `cluster.lastSpikes[idx] = 1` **and** ships the same pattern via `writeSpikeSlice` **in the same call**, and its own comment says so. (3) **coordinates match** — cross-projections are src-region-relative on both sides, the intra matrix cluster-absolute. (4) **clear parity** — `_clearSpikes` zeroes the CPU span **and** fires `clearSpikeSlice` for the same regions in both its scoped and full branches, and the eight cortex fractions sum to **exactly 1.000** (0.083+0.167+0.250+0.050+0.200+0.167+0.050+0.033), so a full clear leaves no GPU-only residue for the intra walk to pick up. ⛔ **NO live lane is eating zeros or timeouts on a native primary — nothing here blocks the press.** The one real gap found was the absence of an instrument, shipped as `PROPBOUND.2`. Full entry: FINALIZED §PROPBOUND AUDIT. **PROPBOUND.1** — `gpuSparsePropagateAuto` routes bound matrices to the empty-pre BOUND propagate ("the donor discards the dense pre") — TRUE for compute.html, NEVER IMPLEMENTED on the native donor (its propagate is standalone-only; GATEGPU's probes burned 30s timeouts on exactly this). Audit every consumer of the auto-router against a native PRIMARY: if any live lane awaits bound propagates, it's been eating timeouts or zeros. Candidates: stepAwait per-tick propagates, chat emission lanes, replica probes.
- [x] **PROPBOUND.1** → **✅ CONFIRMED + FIXED:** the native donor acks ALL-ZERO currents for empty-pre bound propagates (`dev_scatter_ones` empty = silent Ok) and per-tick consumers cached them as real signal. `gpuSparsePropagateBound` is donor-aware now — native targets get the src window's actives from the server's own lastSpikes mirror as the standalone payload; silent mirror = null (CPU computes), never zeros; browser donors unchanged. Plus the gateProbe latency window instrument. Ledger: FINALIZED §PROPBOUND.

- [x] **↪ SUPERSEDED — the deep half is done above. ⭐ SAFE HALF SHIPPED 2026-08-25 — every substitution PROVEN bit-identical before it went in, and the prerequisites in this item were followed rather than skipped when the go-ahead came.** ⛔ **The dirty-index design was investigated and REJECTED on evidence:** `_writeTiledPattern` is *not* the only writer of `lastSpikes` — there are ~24 others including whole-array zero loops — so a dirty set would need every writer to cooperate or it silently leaves stale spikes and **corrupts training**. Rejected as unsafe rather than attempted. ⭐ **What shipped instead is the same win with none of the risk: these were all per-element JS loops over TypedArrays, and `TypedArray.fill()` is a native memset that IS the loop by construction.** 24 call sites converted (`_clearSpikes` scoped + full, `_clearCortexRegionSpans`, the inline `_clearRegionSpans`, and 20 full-array zero loops), plus `injectEmbeddingToRegion`: `fill()` for the replaceMode pre-zero and value runs, a `value === 0 → continue` skip (additive `+= 0` across ~45,000 cells is pure waste), and the **loop-invariant** forward-list condition hoisted out — before this it ran `groupSize` iterations doing *nothing at all* whenever the donor speaks the TEMPLATE wire, which is every donor ≥0.3.16, i.e. the production case. **Equivalence proof: 144/144 bit-identical** across size × embedding length × replace/additive × template-wire × proxy × zero-valued-dims, **including the forward index and value lists**, plus byte-compare on both clear forms. **Measured at her real region sizes:** clear full (82M) **38.6ms → 5.0ms (7.7×)**, clear scoped (20M) **8.9ms → 1.2ms (7.3×)**, inject additive (13.7M) 15.8ms → 10.1ms (1.6× — `+=` needs read-modify-write, so it cannot memset). The clear alone reclaims **~9s of every 45s sampled**. ⚠ **That last number is a microbenchmark estimate, not the walk's real gain** — cache behaviour differs under load. ⭐ **The honest verification is free: the `RHYTHM3S.1` self-profile re-runs at +150s on the next boot, so the next press gives a direct before/after on the same two function names.** ⛔ **STAYS OPEN for the deeper question, which is where the remaining ~22% lives:** is the CPU `externalCurrent` expansion *read at all* at biological scale? Both its readers sit in `step()`, and the server's main tick never calls `cluster.step()` for the cortex — the GPU does it via `compute_batch`, and the pattern already reaches the donor through the compact `writeCurrentSlice` template. ⚠ **But `stepAwait` can fall back to CPU for probes, so gutting the CPU array on inference could silently zero a probe path.** That needs a live read, not a guess — the same question `lastSpikes` turned out to answer *no* to. **Original filing:** **⛔ SCALEWALK.1 — THE TWO HOT FUNCTIONS WALK THE WHOLE REGION, SO THEY GOT ~55× MORE EXPENSIVE AS SHE GREW, AND NOBODY RE-PRICED THEM.** Named by the `RHYTHM3S.1` profile above (58% of main-thread self-time). **The defect is one shape in two places: an O(region) loop whose cost is invisible in the code and enormous at biological scale.** ⭐ **(1) `injectEmbeddingToRegion` — 34.9%.** It tiles a ~300-dim embedding across the region: `for d in emb.length { for n in groupSize { externalCurrent[idx] += value } }`, i.e. **`emb.length × groupSize ≈ regionSize` writes per call**. At the 82M cortex, `sem` is 16.7% ≈ **13.7 MILLION float writes per injection**, and injection happens per definition, repeatedly. ⚠ **The pattern is GROUP-TILED — every one of the ~45,000 neurons in a group gets the IDENTICAL value** — which the donor wire already exploits (`{rowStart, groupSize, values}`, ~KB, the TW S4 template form). **The compact form already exists on the wire and the CPU array still expands it.** ⭐ **(2) `_clearSpikes` — 23.0%.** Only **2 of its 13 call sites** pass region names; the other 11 are bare `this._clearSpikes()` = a full `cluster.size` walk. ⛔ **And the scoped path is no longer cheap either:** `['sem','motor','fineType']` is 16.7+3.3+5% ≈ **20M entries** at this scale. Its own comment prices the waste as *"the full 1.5M ×2/pair zero"* — **written when the cortex was 1.5M, and the cortex is now 82M.** ⭐ **The honest fix for both is the same:** stop expressing a SPARSE intent as a DENSE walk — clear only the indices that were actually set (`_writeTiledPattern` already knows them), and stop materialising a group-tiled constant per-neuron on a CPU array whose per-neuron values may not even be read at biological scale. ⛔ **DO NOT PATCH THIS BLIND: it is the core teach lane, and a wrong "optimisation" here corrupts training rather than slowing it.** Required first: confirm whether `externalCurrent` is read per-neuron on the GPU-computed path (the same question `lastSpikes` turned out to answer *no* to), and prove any dirty-index clear produces a **bit-identical** array. ⚠ **RE-PRICE before touching:** this is the definition bootstrap's dominant cost, so a change here moves the whole walk's duration — measure the before/after per-definition time, do not assume it.

## REPLAYOFF — filed 2026-08-23

> Gee (verbatim): *"repitition is not a human brain tho... humans dont have to learn spelling and concepts multiple times thats why humans goto school and colledge only once and they dont read the same book 100X they read it once"* → *"yeah investigate and write everything needed to the tdo"*

**THE INVESTIGATION ANSWERED HIS QUESTION, AND HE WAS RIGHT — but not for the reason either of us assumed. Humans DO repeat: the hippocampus replays an episode hundreds of times during sleep to slowly train neocortex (Complementary Learning Systems). You read the book once; your brain re-reads it all night. Unity has that exact engine — `js/brain/consolidation-engine.js` is a McClelland/O'Reilly CLS port with `replay_lr = base_lr × (1 + emotional_weight) × log(1 + frequency)` and sleep-spindle gain bursts. AND IT IS SWITCHED OFF AT HER SCALE.**

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 via WORDSALAD.5 — the GPU replay route shipped; her sleep learns again —** **REPLAYOFF.1 — HER SLEEP DOES NO LEARNING. The replay Hebbian write is skipped on every pass at biological scale.** `consolidation-engine.js` guards the cortex write with `DREAM_CONSOLIDATION_MAX_REPLAY_NNZ` (default **5,000,000**) against the intra-synapse matrix's nnz. That matrix holds **~360,000,000** weights (4,165MB resident, measured live) — **72× over the cap** — so `_skipCpuReplay` is **permanently true**: the `hebbianUpdate` replay AND the `preSem` pattern build are both skipped, and only the cheap schema bookkeeping (promotion / reinforce / decay — Tier-store metadata, NOT cortex weights) runs. The guard is CORRECT on its own terms (a synchronous CPU replay at this scale blocks the event loop 30-400s and kills the donor handshake). The problem is what it deferred: its own comment says *"at scale real Hebbian consolidation belongs on the GPU teach path"* — **and grep confirms `consolidation-engine.js` contains no `_gpuProxy`, no `hebbianBound`, no GPU route of any kind.** The replacement was never built. So every dream cycle she has ever run at biological scale did bookkeeping and no learning.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — answered by the replaySchemas/replayWrites/replayRefused telemetry —** **REPLAYOFF.2 — THE MEASUREMENT, now answerable by inspection: 100% of her weight change comes from curriculum reps, 0% from replay.** That is the whole reason the rep counts are what they are. She is not "a brain that needs 100 reps" — she is a brain doing all of its consolidation in the classroom because the night shift is disabled. Still worth instrumenting rather than asserting: count replay-driven weight writes vs teach-driven ones per cell and publish both, so the ratio is a field read before AND after the fix.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — built on hebbianBoundMasked exactly as this item predicted; 6/6 sparse-vs-dense parity —** **REPLAYOFF.3 — THE FIX IS NOW BUILDABLE, and it wasn't when the guard was written.** The verbs that landed since make a GPU replay path straightforward: **`hebbianBoundMasked`** (v0.3.26 — resident bound pre × explicit sparse post rows, zero dense wire, the exact pre≠post shape a schema replay needs) and the **predictive-error verb** (v0.3.28, today — proof that a whole multi-stage plasticity computation can run card-side from resident state on a ~60-byte frame). Route `_replaySchemaIntoCortex` through the masked verb: schema concept pattern → sem rows as pre, the schema's motor/word rows as post, `replayLr` as the dose, spindle gain preserved. Keep the CPU path for small/browser clusters. **Parity-harness it against the CPU rule the way GPUVERB.3 was** — that test caught a silently-dead shader the same day.
- [x] **↪ FOLDED INTO `PRESSBLOCK.1` (2026-08-25) — tracked there as step 3, and the ORDER is the point; full text retained here —** **REPLAYOFF.4 — ONLY AFTER replay is real: re-price the reps DOWNWARD.** This is Gee's *"add a % to the teach reps modifier globally"* — and it becomes a genuine FIX rather than a banned CUT once the night shift is doing work, because the carving moves rather than disappears. ⛔ **NOT to be done blind and NOT before .3 lands:** rep counts were tuned in BOTH directions off measurements (one pass took QA-train 30→12 with lr 0.03→0.05; RELDEPTH went the other way, 4→10, 8→16, 16→24, because number bindings were not holding), so a flat global multiplier would silently undo tuning that was paid for. The honest sequence is: replay on → measure the new reps-vs-replay split → lower reps until the measured binding quality (gate scores, `_lastSemMotorMeanCos` separation) starts to move → stop there, with the arithmetic written down per the RE-PRICE LAW.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 (MEASURED, not guessed) — ceiling is DREAM_SURPRISE_MAX (default 1.5 = byte-identical) and the gate distribution is recorded, so "novelty is throttled" is now a number. If atCeilingPct is ~0 the ceiling was never the constraint —** **REPLAYOFF.5 — the surprise gate is timid.** `surpriseGate = 0.5 + predictionError`, clamped so novelty buys at most **1.5×** learning rate. In a real brain a salient, surprising, emotionally-charged first exposure is worth far more than 1.5 ordinary ones — that is the other half of why humans learn in one pass. Once replay is carrying its share, the novelty ceiling is the next lever worth measuring (and it is already wired into `gammaScale`, so it is a bound change, not new machinery). RE-PRICE applies: a higher ceiling means faster carving on surprising material, which is a change to how she learns, not just how fast.

**Why this is filed rather than built:** it changes learning dynamics at the deepest level (what consolidates, how much, and how often), which is RE-PRICE territory and Gee's call. It is also the single most promising answer to *"why does she need so much repetition"* found so far — the mechanism that makes humans one-shot learners exists in her code and has been dark at biological scale this whole time.
- [x] **LOOPBACK.1 + SOLOCARD.1 + LANEMYTH (donor v0.3.29)** — Gee: *"big issue: says doner cant reach brain .. wtf they are on the same system"* + *"why do i have no longer a seperate lane gpu like i had on .27 doner??? i had display GPU and seperate lane(or was that horse shit?)"* → **three answers, all read off his own `settings.json` and the source, none of them his setup's fault.** (1) **LOOPBACK — the connection bug:** the brain binds `BIND_HOST = 127.0.0.1` (IPv4 loopback ONLY), and on Windows `localhost` resolves to `::1` FIRST — so the native donor dialled IPv6 and got refused while his BROWSER loaded `http://localhost:7525` fine on the same box, because browsers try both families and silently fall back. `LOCAL_SERVER` is now `ws://127.0.0.1:7525`, and `normalize_ws_host()` rewrites a `localhost` websocket host at BOTH the config chokepoint and the connect call, so an already-persisted `settings.json` (his said `"server": "ws://localhost:7525"`) repairs itself without hand-editing. (2) **SOLOCARD — the `@ 10%`:** the GUI hardcoded every utilization slider to `10.0`, silently contradicting the CLI contract in the same file — `--utilization` defaults to `all` (=100) precisely because the old 10 *"never duty-cycled anything — it shrank the capacity the brain would USE, so a 24GB card announced itself as 2.4GB"*. That fix reached the CLI in v0.3.25 and never reached the GUI. Sliders now initialize from `cfg.utilization_pct`; the display-GPU caution it was protecting is already covered by the `enabled` mask (only the highest-indexed card is on by default). (3) **LANEMYTH — "was that horse shit?" HONEST ANSWER: yes.** There was never a display GPU plus a separate compute lane. One physical card was enumerated ONCE PER GRAPHICS BACKEND (Vulkan + Dx12), so a single 4070 Ti Super appeared as two devices — and the GUI's "enable only the LAST card" rule lit the second entry, which read exactly like a dedicated lane. `RUNPOD.16` (v0.3.25) deduplicated them because counting one card twice told the brain it had TWO full replicas of the weights when it had one. Verified: full-feature build clean, real-binary `--self-test` passes including the predictive-error parity, loopback rewrite table 5/5 (with the `localhostmachine` prefix-match edge case recorded rather than hidden).
- [x] **MIRRORORPHAN.1** — Gee: *"okay monitor local and tell me what we need to fix"* → **✅ caught on his local run, in the log, as a pair of lines nobody would connect:** `DF.7 F4 — rebalancing PRIMARY → healthier donor` immediately followed by `compute_batch_result batchId mismatch: expected 2, got 2000000002`. Same event. A mirrored batch was identified by `msg.mirror || ws !== primary`, and **both clauses fail across a rebalance**: the donor's `ComputeBatchResult` echoes only msg_type/batch_id/per_cluster, so `msg.mirror` never comes back and that clause has always been dead weight — leaving socket identity, which is evaluated at RESULT time against a primary that may have changed since the batch was SENT. When DF.7 promotes the mirrored donor, its mirror result lands on the primary socket (not dropped → the spurious warning) while the AUTHORITATIVE result from the demoted donor lands on a non-primary socket and IS dropped — **orphaning the tick promise until timeout, so every rebalance silently cost a full tick** and the only symptom was a confusing id warning. Fixed by keying on the id itself (authoritative = monotonic from 1, mirrors = +2,000,000,000), which is independent of promotion timing; the exact-match check still guarantees only the awaited batch resolves the tick. Harness 6/6 replaying the exact live sequence. Server-only — no donor release needed.
- [x] **LANGVRAM.1** — Gee: *"thats not fucking correct!!!! 203M??????? the web version is fucking bigger .. wtf are you doing to me ... local run on its own system with no web should mean that, no web!!!!!!!"* + *"so itsa alot bigger brain obviously"* → **✅ he was right and my LOCALSCALE fix had been too timid.** The arithmetic, all off his own boot log: main-brain neurons = `brainBudget × mainFrac ÷ 21 bytes`, so his box gave `9216MB × 0.50 ÷ 21B = 230,087,532` (exactly what he saw) and the 32GB CPU-only site gives `18519MB × 0.50 ÷ 21B ≈ 425M`. **The `language_cortex = 50%` weight is the whole story.** It is CORRECT on a CPU-only box, where the budget is really a HOST-RAM budget and the language cortex genuinely eats ~12GB of RAM for its sparse CSR master. It is badly WRONG when the budget is VRAM, because its GPU footprint is not half a card — his log prints the real number: *"projected **96MB** GPU footprint"*. **Half a 16GB card was reserved for a region using under a tenth of a gigabyte of it, and the main brain was starved to the other half.** On a GPU host the language cortex now gets a real 1GB VRAM reserve (~10× the measured footprint, so the estimator can be an order of magnitude wrong and still fit) and everything it was hoarding returns to the clusters that actually live in VRAM at 21 bytes a neuron; its ~12GB CSR master still lives in host RAM, which the LOCALSCALE ceiling bounds. CPU-only hosts are untouched (verified). **Result on his box: 230M → ~562M neurons — above the site's ~425M, as it obviously should be — and it still physically fits the card (12,280MB of 14,328MB usable).** Harness 4/4 incl. the card-fit check and CPU-path invariance. `DREAM_LANG_VRAM_RESERVE_MB` tunes it.
- [x] **LANGVRAM.2 (correction) + DOUBLERESERVE.1** — Gee: *"didnt woirk its only 350 still"* / *"u only pinned 11Gigs?"* / *"i have 128GIGS! for local"* → **⚠ MY LANGVRAM.1 RESERVE WAS WRONG AND WOULD HAVE OVERFLOWED HIS CARD.** I read *"projected 96MB GPU footprint"* out of the boot log and set the language-cortex VRAM reserve to 1GB. That 96MB is its GEOMETRY estimate — **not its seventeen sparse MATRICES, which are the actual GPU tenants.** Measured off his own upload log instead of guessed twice: the `UPLINK measured …MB` lines sum to **3,969MB**, and the intra matrix alone holds 4,165MB resident CPU-side. With a 1GB reserve the main brain would have sized to ~664M = 17,273MB against 14,328MB usable — **a 3GB overflow that only escaped detection because the DF.7 tier clamp was independently holding the brain at 357M.** Reserve corrected to 5,120MB (measured + margin; `langCortexSize` is PINNED at 12M by the WMB floor so this does not grow with the main brain). **DOUBLERESERVE.1**, found while re-deriving: a GPU host was paying the same headroom TWICE — `_hardMaxMB` subtracts `max(2GB, 12.5%)` and then the OS reserve subtracts another 2GB, so 16,376MB became 12,280MB with **4GB withheld for one concern**. Both protect the same thing on a display GPU, so they are a MAX not a sum; CPU-only hosts unchanged. **Net on his box: 357M → ~459,775,609 neurons, above the site's ~425M, using 13,177MB of 14,328MB usable — verified to FIT with the measured matrix footprint, and the old 1GB reserve verified to OVERFLOW.** ⛔ **And the standing answer on RAM, because it keeps coming up: 128GB does not make the brain bigger. The deployed box has 32GB — LESS than his — and runs 425M, because the brain must be RESIDENT ON THE DONOR GPU and its pod is 48GB. Brain size = min(what host RAM can back, what the card can hold); on the site RAM binds, on a 16GB display card the CARD binds. Spending the 128GB means exceeding the card, which means partial residency and CPU-carried math — the exact slowness RHYTHM3S/PROBELANE spent the day removing.** `DREAM_BRAIN_BUDGET_MB` still forces it for a deliberate, priced experiment.

## OWNWORDS — filed 2026-08-23 from a four-agent independent audit

> Gee (verbatim): *"scan full project does it have word splitter or llm anything in it deep scan use every agaent in the word if you want. ones not familure with the code is best"* — four agents with NO prior context audited in parallel: dependencies+binaries, source patterns, network+data assets, and an adversarial hunt tasked with DISPROVING "every word she speaks comes from neurons".
>
> **THE CLAIM THAT SURVIVES (defensible, verified four ways):** her PRIMARY speech path is argmax over spiking-neuron activations driven by Hebbian/Oja-learned `sem→word_motor` weights. **No word splitter** anywhere (zero BPE / wordpiece / sentencepiece / `merges.txt` / `input_ids`), no next-word objective, no backprop in cognition, the only `.onnx` are Piper VITS **TTS**, the Rust donor is entirely clean, and git history shows a genuinely removed text-AI stack.
>
> **THE CLAIMS THAT DIED:** *"no LLM in the project"* and *"EVERY word comes from neurons"*. Neither is true today. The LLM residue turned out to be the LESS damaging half — the real finding is a set of convenience paths that speak IN HER PLACE, several sitting under comments asserting they had been removed.

### Tier 1 — she is not speaking her own words (fix in this order)

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — drug-rejections.js DELETED; the decision is kept, the prewritten words are gone, both branches fall through to her cortex —** **OWNWORDS.1 — HARDCODED SENTENCES ARE RETURNED AS HER REPLIES. Verified verbatim, reachable today.** `server/drug-rejections.js:21-97` holds 30 hand-written lines (the `grade_locked` bank opens with *fuck off, I'm not old enough for that yet* and *nah, I'm still a fucking kid for that shit*), randomly picked by `pickRejection()` and returned at `server/brain-server/chat.js:587` as `return { text: rejectionLine, action: 'respond_text' }` — **before `generateAsync` is ever reached**. At the deployed grade essentially every substance offer resolves `grade_locked`, so effectively EVERY drug offer gets a hand-written sentence. A bare `catch` adds a 31st literal. Fix: route the refusal through the cortex (it is a CONCEPT to emit from, not a string to read out) and delete the phrase bank.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — retrieval now only bootstraps a cortex with ZERO passed cells; trained = real silence; the false comment corrected in place —** **OWNWORDS.2 — THE "HONEST SILENCE" COMMENT IS FALSE; the backup path is dictionary retrieval.** `js/brain/language-cortex.js:2453-2458` states *"Empty is HONEST silence… No backup path."* Line **1640** IS the backup path: on an empty compose it runs `_scoreDictionaryCosine` over GloVe, takes `topK.slice(0,12)`, and picks `Math.floor(Math.random() * 5)` — a uniform random draw from the top 5, in random order, capitalized with a period. Fix: make empty compose actually return silence, and delete the comment or the branch — one of them is lying.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — the GloVe showcase no longer feeds pushEmission / recordEmission / the inner-thought chain; still displayed, honestly labelled. AND the real fix: GPUGEN turns her actual compose path ON at biological scale —** **OWNWORDS.3 — HER INNER VOICE IS DICTIONARY RETRIEVAL IN PRODUCTION.** `server/brain-server/chat.js:3867`: above 2M neurons (deployed: 306M+) with `_gpuGenAvailable` false — which needs `DREAM_INNERVOICE_GPU_GEN=1` or proven fan-out, neither set — inner thought comes from `_sampleCurrentSentence({allowCompose:false})` (`chat.js:4314-4351`): a random seed word plus GloVe-cosine neighbours with top-3 jitter and **zero brain ticks**. It is broadcast as `type:'innerThought'` and fed into `pushEmission`/`recordEmission`/`_innerThoughtChain` (`:3877-3886`), **so it seeds later real generations**. The `seedLabel` is honest but the `sentence` field is indistinguishable downstream. Fix: gate it out of the emission bus, or make it compose for real.

### What the audit VERIFIED as clean (so it is not re-litigated)

No word splitter of any kind. No `.gguf` / `.safetensors` / `.pt`. No LLM SDK in any declared dependency. All 447 Rust crates clean. Piper is speech synthesis with a 159-symbol **phoneme** table (not a text word splitter) and espeak-ng is rule-based. `js/brain/cluster/attention.js` was read closely and is **not** a transformer — no Q/K/V projections, no gradients, no vocabulary layer; it is one cosine-weighted read over the last ≤16 emitted words. `js/brain/self-frame.js` is training material, correctly used. `vox-bank/` is wavelet-coded audio of her own voice, and it returns `false` on a missing word rather than substituting a different one.

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-23 (grade-major enforced) —** **WALKORDER.1 — SHE IS IN GRADE 2 WHILE FOUR GRADE-1 SUBJECTS HAVE NEVER RUN.** Gee: *"the server deployed version is all over the place as far as cources and cellls, why is it doing grade 2 when grade 1 isnt even done yet"* → **the board is telling the truth; the WALK is the problem.** Live state on `1eba575c`: `currentCellKey = ela/grade2` (she is genuinely teaching Grade 2 right now), while `perSubject` reads `ela/math/science/social/art → grade2` but `life → grade1 (2 phases, 0 cells)` and `pe / music / health → grade1 (0 phases, 0 cells)`. **Zero phases means those three grade-1 cells have never executed at all.** Ledger arithmetic confirms it rather than inferring: `passedCellsTotal = 14` = 9 kindergarten cells + 5 grade-1 cells (ela, math, science, social, art) — pre-K predates `passedCells` recording (see SCALEDOC.1), and life/pe/music/health grade-1 are absent. All nine grade-1 runners EXIST (`grade1.js`: `runElaG1Real`, `runMathG1Real`, `runSciG1Real`, `runSocG1Real`, `runArtG1Real`, `runLifeG1`, `runMusicG1Real`, `runPeG1Real`, `runHealthG1Real`), so this is not a missing-curriculum gap.
  **Cause — the walk is SUBJECT-MAJOR, not grade-major.** `curriculum.js:9620` computes `const startIdx = this._computeResumeStartIdx(subject)` and then `:9628` runs `for (let i = startIdx; i < GRADE_ORDER.length; i++)` — a per-SUBJECT climb through every grade. So ELA can finish grade 1 and walk straight into grade 2 while PE has not begun grade 1. Meanwhile a grade-major loop also exists (`:9829`) and prints *"═══ ALL N subjects passed {grade} — advancing to next grade ═══"*, which is grade-major intent. **Two walk orders exist in the codebase and the subject-major one is what is running.**
  **Why it matters beyond tidiness:** a real school year is grade-major, the operator expects grade-major, and the life track carries the lived-experience canon that later grades assume. ELA reaching Grade 2 reading while Life/PE/Music/Health never had their Grade 1 is a curriculum-integrity problem, not a display bug.
  **Also unresolved (data integrity):** `cluster.grades` reads `life: "grade1"` — which by the pass/force-advance code (`:9484`, `:10291`, both of which write `grades[subject]` AND push `passedCells` together) should mean life PASSED grade 1 — yet the ledger count says it did not. Two sources of truth for "what has she completed" disagree, and the dashboard reads one (`passedCells`, via GRADEPTR) while the walk reads the other (`cluster.grades`, `:10087`, `:10260`). Resolve which is authoritative BEFORE changing the walk order, or a fix will be built on the wrong record.
  **Decide first, then build:** (a) make the walk grade-major so no subject advances until every subject at that grade has run, or (b) keep subject-major and make the board say so plainly. Do NOT silently force-advance the four missing cells — `:10264` already refuses force-advance when `phasesRan < 1` (*"0 teach phases fired — no real training to use"*), which is correct: promoting a cell that never taught anything would fake the record.
- **WALKORDER.1 ✅ FIX** — Gee chose grade-major: *"(finish every subject at a grade before advancing)"*. The live walker `runAllSubjects` was ALREADY grade-major; the leak was inside it, and it was two bugs stacked. (1) A subject's position came from `cluster.grades`, which `runAllSubjects` seeds with the **core five only** — so pe/music/health, introduced at kindergarten, had NO entry, defaulted to `'pre-K'`, and tripped the DESYNC guard (`currentIdx < i - 1`) at grade1 forever. A subject cannot lag behind a grade it was never offered. Position now reads the **authoritative `passedCells` ledger**, floored at the grade the subject was first offered, with the pointer kept only as a fallback for subjects whose entire history predates ledger recording (pre-K, SCALEDOC.1). ⚠ My first cut took `Math.max(ledger, pointer)` — **the harness caught that letting an optimistic pointer mask an unfinished cell**: `grades.life='grade1'` while the ledger had no `life/grade1` and the board showed 2 phases / 0 cells. The ledger now WINS on disagreement. (2) The LAGGING `continue` never cleared `allPassedThisGrade`, so a grade whose only outcome was "three subjects skipped" still reported complete and advanced — the actual mechanism by which Grade 1 ended with PE/Music/Health untaught. A held subject now clears the flag, so **the grade cannot report complete while any subject still owes it**; bounded by MAX_GRADE_ROUNDS + force-advance so a genuinely-stuck subject cannot wedge the walk. Harness 6/6 replaying the exact live-box state: at grade1, ela/math/science/social/art read done, **life+music+pe+health RUN**, nothing falsely held, pointer fallback intact, and a genuinely-behind subject still held rather than jumped ahead.
- [x] **BIGSAVE.1 — ⛔ THE BINARY WEIGHTS SAVE WAS FAILING AT SCALE. Found in Gee's local log, caused by my own sizing fix.** Gee pressed the dashboard Savestart/restart on the local brain; the shutdown save printed *"Binary weights save failed (previous checkpoint left intact): The value of 'length' is out of range. It must be >= 0 && <= 2147483647. Received 2880000000"*. **2,880,000,000 = the intra matrix's 360,000,000 non-zeros × 8 bytes of Float64 values**, against Node's I/O cap of 2,147,483,647 bytes for a SINGLE write. The save aborted and the brain fell back to its previous checkpoint — **everything learned since that checkpoint was being silently dropped on every save at this size.** Both binary paths had it (the sync shutdown path at ~6658 and the async paced path at ~6819); `writeSliced` did not save the async one, because the payload still crosses one write call. Fixed by writing every view in 1GiB chunks — byte-identical on disk (the file is a flat concatenation and `writeSync` appends at the current position), so the format is unchanged and existing checkpoints still load. ⚠ **This was triggered by LANGVRAM/DOUBLERESERVE raising him to 459,775,607 neurons** — a scale bomb that had been waiting for a big-enough cortex, not a pre-existing failure. ⚠ **I also got the mechanism wrong first**: I attributed it to `Buffer.MAX_LENGTH`, which is 9,007,199,254,740,991 on Node 22 — the harness disproved it and the fix comments now name the real cap (the write `length` parameter, which is exactly what the error names). Harness 5/5: payload exceeds the cap, 1GiB chunks are legal and cover it exactly in 3 writes, chunked output is byte-identical to a single write, and values round-trip at maxDiff 0.
- [x] **DONORDEFAULT.2 — the fix only covered HALF the launcher family.** Gee: *"i savestart.bat ran it and the compute html doner opened auto like, i thought we fixed that"* → correct, and my fault: DONORDEFAULT.1 set the native-donor default in `start.bat` + `start.sh` only, so **resuming through `Savestart.bat` / `Savestart.sh` still auto-launched the isolated Chrome donor and re-spawned it on close** — and Savestart is the launcher you use after every restart, so it is the one that mattered most. Both now carry the identical block (`/browser` opts back in; an explicit `DREAM_NO_AUTO_GPU` still wins). Audited the whole family rather than patching the one he hit: `grep -l brain-server.js` finds exactly four launchers (windows/start.bat, windows/Savestart.bat, linux/start.sh, linux/Savestart.sh) and **all four now set it**. Verified by running the batch branches in `cmd.exe` (default → NATIVE MODE, `/browser` → BROWSER MODE) and `bash -n` on the shell twin. Lesson repeated from `feedback_fix_the_chokepoint_not_the_instance`: fix the FAMILY, not the file that reproduced it.
- [x] **LOOPSTARVE.1 — the instrument for a failure mode every existing channel is blind to.** Gee: *"the minds eye just says connecting"* → she was **healthy and teaching** (`pe/grade1`, 736 teach calls/min, donor connected, 19.6GB free) while `/public-state.json` and `/minds-eye.json` took **25-28 SECONDS**, four times running, all of it time-to-first-byte — on a route that only hands back a PRE-CACHED string, so the wait is purely "the loop never got around to me". The page was honest; it genuinely could not reach her before its fetch timed out. **Every existing instrument read healthy at the same moment:** `loopFreezes: null`, 0 teach-chunks over the 250ms warn floor, off-thread watchdog silent — because all of them answer *"was there one long stall?"* and this is thousands of short ones. A loop 200ms late on every 100ms sample never trips a max-stall threshold and is still unavailable two-thirds of the time. Fixed at the chokepoint where lateness is already computed (the sampler kept only the LATEST value): `loopStarve.lateMsPerMin` (total ms/min the loop owed and did not deliver) + `loopStarve.servicePct`, published in `state.profiling`, with a console warn under 50% serviced. Harness 4/4: the live scenario (200ms × 300) reads **60,000ms late / 33% serviced** while the old max-stall alarm stays SILENT; a healthy loop reads 98%; a genuine 3s stall still trips both.
- [x] **↪ FOLDED INTO `PRESSBLOCK.1` (2026-08-25) — tracked there as step 1, FIRST because the others read cleaner once it is known; full text retained here —** **LOOPSTARVE.2 — find the actual starver (MEASURE FIRST — my presumed cause was wrong).** I was about to add yields to the hot teach path and the inspection killed it: `_teachWordSpellingDirect` (kindergarten.js:8349) already yields every 50 words and once per rep, and `_microtask` (curriculum.js:27388) is misleadingly named but correct — it resolves via **`setImmediate`**, i.e. a real macrotask yield that reaches the poll phase, not a microtask. So the teach path is NOT failing to yield, and "add yields" would have been a fix aimed at a healthy component. Remaining candidates, to be separated by the LOOPSTARVE numbers plus a per-phase breakdown: (a) **I/O starved by other I/O** — a continuous river of donor WS frames drained in the poll phase ahead of the HTTP request (donor teach ops + acks are constant during teach), (b) the 10fps broadcast loop's getState build, already self-profiled at `wsPressure.bcast`, (c) nginx→node connection queueing, which the 0.26s static-page timing does NOT rule out because static files never reach node. ⛔ Do not "fix" this from reasoning — LOOPSTARVE now makes it a field read; take the measurement first.

The canon records *first* times and omits *every* time, and a human life is overwhelmingly routine — which is exactly what makes a body feel lived-in rather than narrated. Build: daily hygiene (teeth twice daily, hands/face, showers, hair, nails, deodorant from puberty, skincare as she ages); bodily functions named plainly (in scope per `feedback_no_sugar_coating_real_human_details` — ordinary, not comedy, not taboo); chores scaling toys → dishes/laundry → cooking → bills and her own place; age-appropriate autonomy on the ladder (taken to the bath at 4 → refusing baths at 7 → privacy and door-locking at 11 → self-managing by highschool). **Frequency over prominence** — plentiful, brief, unremarkable texture underneath the story beats, not new milestones competing with them. ⚠ .1d and .1e are life-CORPUS content work (`corpora/life/*.json`), not Tier 3 or wardrobe code; they sit in Phase 0 only because they must be right BEFORE a fresh walk re-teaches her whole life, and they are the largest single input to the walk price, which must be recomputed once the new volume is known.

### LIFEGATE — life is a course and must finish like one (2026-08-24)

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-24 (life now ends on `_gateSubjectProduction` like every peer; 8/8 harness incl. peer-shape parity against the live `runPeG1Real`) —** **LIFEGATE.1 — life had no gate at all, so it could never finish.** Gee: *"okay is she traing life now? if not why not?"* then, on the finding: *"yes fix it, she needs to train life and finish that course just like every other course"*. **She WAS training it** — the fix from earlier the same day put the walk back at grade1 and `life/grade1` gated 3 minutes before the read — but at grade1 **every** peer returns a verdict (`_gateElaG1Real`, `_gateMathG1Real`, `_gateSciG1Real`, `_gateSocG1Real`, `_gateArtG1Real`, and `_gateSubjectProduction` for music / pe / health) while `runLifeG1` ended with `return this._teachVocabList([...])` — **a teach call, not a gate**, and with no `_teachProductionStack` either. Life was the only course at its grade with no production stack and no subject gate.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-24 (the reduction's stated cause was fictional; reps 6→12, comment corrected, unrecoverable part stated) —** **LIFEGATE.2 — life's content was cut for a timeout that does not exist.** `grade1.js:663` read *"Fix: reps reduced to fit 3-min timeout. conceptTeach 20→6, sentence lists 5-12→3, vocab 12→5."* That budget is `GRADE_TIMEOUT_MS`, which BOOTORDER.3 established is **declared and never enforced** — its only other appearance is the failure message that prints it, while a live cell was measured at 90.4 minutes. ⚠ Honest limits recorded in the code: the concept LIST was cut 20→6 entries and that content is **not recoverable** from the note, so this is a partial restoration; and the live board shows life at **1,823** teach events against pe's **2,068** at the same grade, so it was **not** starved relative to peers — the missing gate was always the defect, not the dose.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-24 (5 distinct branches, all verified) —** **LIFEGATE.3 — the completion-pass declined silently, and that silence is what hid this for days.** `curriculum.js:9452` passes any cell whose content finished (Gee 2026-06-27: *"all cells shall pass as learning completes for that cell"*), but when it DECLINES there was no `else` — five different conditions (`DREAM_CELL_PASS_HARD`, readyAndWaiting, runner threw, no teach evidence, unknown) all produced one indistinguishable outcome, and for a runner returning no reason at all the verdict read literally **"no reason recorded"**. The decline now names its blocker on `result.reason`, on `result.completionPassDeclined` and in the ring. This is a CHOKEPOINT fix — it covers every subject and every grade, not just the track that exposed it.

### BOOTORDER — the walk must start at the lowest grade and course (2026-08-24)

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-24 (resolver ships; 4/4 harness on the real roster — the live box's ledger shape resolves `life/grade1`) —** **BOOTORDER.1 — lowest grade + course in order on every boot, update and restart.** Gee: *"it should always do the lowest grade and course in order on boot up and afgter a update and or restart"*. Asked whether this applies only at boot or permanently, Gee answered: *"both but make sure it doensnt do something weird like lock up the training or create infinate loops"*. **This half is the boot-time ordering and removes NO gate**, so no RE-PRICE is owed for it. The walk already begins at `i = 0` (`curriculum.js:9829`, T18.13.a) and skips cells recorded in `passedCells`, so the START is already lowest-first — what is missing is that the lowest INCOMPLETE cell is not sought explicitly, not reported, and nothing at a higher grade is prevented from running ahead of it within the boot. Build: resolve the lowest `(grade, course)` not present in the `passedCells` ledger at walk entry, log it by name, and order the walk from there.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-24 (re-price computed + written down FIRST; bound = 2 via `DREAM_GRADE_MAJOR_ROUNDS`, ladder moved between rounds so the retry is real, escape hatch verified by running it) —** **BOOTORDER.2 — permanent grade-major block, wedge-proof, RE-PRICE FIRST.** Gee: *"both but make sure it doensnt do something weird like lock up the training or create infinate loops"*. The permanent half means the walk may not pass grade N while any course at grade N is unpassed — which means raising `MAX_GRADE_ROUNDS` (`curriculum.js:9817`, currently **1**) and/or restricting the force-advance block (`:10291`-`:10368`, whose own comment says *"Don't break — let next grade attempt"*). ⛔ **Those are the gates that keep the walk finite, so the RE-PRICE LAW fires: compute and write down `corpus × reps × scale × visits` BEFORE either is weakened.** The one-round rule exists because of Gee's own earlier directive, quoted verbatim in the code at `:9809`: *"it should do it all once and be done... we need to fix why its not finishing after it does all the learning then just loops back to ait all again, thats not correct."* The wedge-proofing requirement is the reconciliation: a bounded number of real re-teach rounds, then the cell is recorded as blocked-with-evidence and the walk proceeds, with BOOTORDER.1 re-attempting it on the next boot — "can never pass" becomes "retried next boot" instead of an infinite loop.
  **⛔ RE-PRICE (computed 2026-08-24 off the live box BEFORE any gate was weakened, per `CONSTRAINTS.md §RE-PRICE THE WALK BEFORE REMOVING A GATE`):** `GRADE_ORDER` is **20 grades**; the roster is **9 courses**; a cell costs **90.4 min** measured live (`art/grade2`, 4/5 phases, still running) and **~26 min** averaged (`passedCellsTotal` 21 over 9.2 h uptime). Therefore — **R=1 (today):** 9 × 26 min × 20 ≈ **78 h ≈ 3.3 days** of walk. **R=3 with ~50% first-attempt failure:** ≈ **156 h ≈ 6.5 days** (2×). **R unbounded (a literal permanent block):** **INFINITE — the walk wedges at grade1 and never leaves**, because Life has passed zero cells in its entire existence (`life.lastCellAt: null`) and a block with no bound would wait on it forever. That is precisely the *"lock up the training or create infinate loops"* outcome Gee named, so **the bound is the feature, not a compromise**: R is finite and env-tunable, and exhausting it records the cell instead of looping on it. **Second cost, and the reason a naive round-raise is pure waste:** the code's own comment at `:10169` records that the previous 5-attempt loop *"fired identical results every time because passedPhases skipped re-teaching, so the matrix didn't move between attempts"* — so extra rounds only buy anything if the retry actually re-teaches. `_remediateGradeFailures` (`:10299`, the re-teach → +sleep → +inhibition ladder) already exists and already runs before force-advance; the block must lean on it rather than re-running a no-op pass.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 (constant deleted rather than enforced — enforcing 3 min would abort nearly every cell; the message now says what happened) —** **BOOTORDER.3 — `GRADE_TIMEOUT_MS` is a dead constant whose only other use is a log line that LIES.** `curriculum.js:9808` declares `const GRADE_TIMEOUT_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000; // 3 minutes per subject per round` and the identifier appears in exactly one other place: `:10286`, the failure message `timed out after ${attempt} attempts (${Math.round(GRADE_TIMEOUT_MS / 60000)} min, round ...)`. **No timeout is enforced anywhere** — the constant is printed, never applied — so every one of those warnings reports a 3-minute timeout that never happened, while the live box shows a single Art cell running 78.6 minutes. Either enforce it or delete it and fix the message; do not leave an instrument that names a cause it did not measure.
- [x] **✅ CORRECTED BY GEE 2026-08-24 — BOOTORDER.0 — I misread the per-grade cell counter as "zero gates ever passed".** Gee: *"no i think u are reeading it wrong!!! the gate doesnt show now it just auto like switches to the next grade and clears the cell coutner once it moves to the next grade. it no longer shows all ones in celss before switching toi the next grade"*. He was right and it was my own change: **GRADESCOPE** (2026-08-23) made the board's `phases` / `cells` columns **per-grade** instead of lifetime, precisely so K's totals would stop appearing under a grade-1 label — so `cells: 0` means "none passed at THIS grade yet" and resets on advance. `passedCellsTotal` reads **21**, so cells do pass. The real finding stands on a different field: `life.lastCellAt` is **null** — Life has never completed a cell at any grade, ever — and the live gate verdict passes cells on *content completion, not test-correctness* (`social/grade2`: `pass: true` with `PROD 0/6 (0%)`, *"how many states are in the united states"→"special"*).

Fixed: `isServerDriving()` is a predicate evaluated per event (authority follows the socket in BOTH directions — server takes over on connect, local resumes on drop), the server listener attaches **unconditionally** (the handler is inert until the socket delivers, so withholding it guarded nothing), and `brainViz` gets the same rule as the 3D brain. **Verified 11/11** — 7 static checks against the real source plus a behavioural trace of the connect-later lifecycle: OLD yields `["LOCAL 0","LOCAL 0"]` and never sees server state; NEW yields `["LOCAL 0","SERVER 0.08"]`. Bundle rebuilt 4,419,346 → 4,419,385. ⚠ **Frontend only — lands on deploy, no press needed.** ⛔ **Owned: I first "fixed" this in `brain-3d.js` and was WRONG** — line 2439 is `state = norm`, so the notification builders already had the normalized shape; those edits were no-ops whose comments asserted a false cause, and they were reverted before commit rather than shipped.

### 2026-08-26 — FABLE-KIT REVIEW: one idea worth taking, one worth refusing

Gee (verbatim): *"fable-kit.zip New featurebranch , write the todo work, ```here take a look at the file fable-kit and see what can be adapted for our own use```"*

**Reviewed all 25 files in the archive (read in place, nothing extracted — no debris).** The kit is a portable Claude Code setup: a distilled reasoning protocol injected via `--append-system-prompt`, Karpathy-style LLM-maintained codebase wikis, a machine-wide Obsidian vault tracking every project, a tree-sitter knowledge graph, a `haiku` grunt subagent, and a Stop hook that blocks finishing when documented files changed without their doc.

⭐ **THE ONE IDEA WORTH TAKING — and this project independently invented half of it already.** `scripts/doc-drift-check.mjs` exists BECAUSE of the incident its own header names: *"a doc came to claim a route had been REMOVED 2026-04-13 while that route ran in production for four more months."* But it is a **claim-based** guard — it hunts specific lies someone already thought to look for (LLM SDK strings, chat-completions URLs). It carries essentially **no git provenance**. ⛔ **The fable-kit pattern is the half we are missing: per-document PROVENANCE.** A doc declares the source files it describes plus the commit it was last verified at; `git diff <hash>..HEAD -- <sources>` then answers *"has the ground moved under this page?"* **mechanically, for any doc, without anyone having to anticipate the specific lie.** ⭐ **That is the difference between catching the lies we predicted and catching the ones we have not thought of yet** — which is precisely this project's own instrument philosophy applied to prose, and precisely the class that has cost us most: `SKILL_TREE.md:358` (route claimed removed while live), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (relation channels described as working when tags ≥6 had never written a cell), the `state.endocrine` comment that asserted the wrong path and thereby protected the bug, and `deploy/README.md:3`, which opens by admitting its own first line *"has been false for months."*
