### PODARGS — ⛔ the live pod is running the OLD launcher, not the one in the repo (found 2026-08-25)

Gee: *"make sure the pod is good and leave it running so we dont lkose the gpu"* — ⭐ **it IS good**, and nothing was touched: `RUNNING`, A40 45,498 MB, CA-MTL-1, CUDA path live (`[donor] backends: NVIDIA A40 [CUDA]` — RUNPOD.6's fix holding), donor **0.3.29** resolved from `releases/latest`, registered, and all 8 GPU inits completed (7 clusters + the 12M `langCortex`). One transient `Connection reset without closing handshake` at startup, recovered on the 2s auto-reconnect.

- [x] **✅ INVESTIGATED AND CLOSED 2026-08-25 — SUPERSEDED, and the pod deliberately NOT recreated.** Gee: *"these need fixed Now before the update and freshwalk with investigation"*. ⛔ **First finding: it CANNOT be fixed in place.** RunPod's `update-pod` exposes `name` / `image` / `containerDisk` / `volume` / `ports` / **`env`** and **not `args`** — read off the tool contract, not assumed. A pod's launcher command is immutable, so changing it means **terminate + recreate**, and a recreate is exactly how the previous A40 was lost when its host reclaimed the card. ⭐ **Second finding, which is the real answer: donor-v0.3.30 moved BOTH of the launcher's fixes into the binary, where they belong.** The **watchdog is superseded outright** — the donor now reads the wanted version from the `welcome` handshake and exits to upgrade at its next disconnect, which is event-driven and immediate rather than a poll up to 5 minutes stale. And **the stale pin is now self-healing**: if an API blip installs an old binary, the brain names the newer one on connect and the donor exits to reinstall; if the API is *still* down and the same old binary returns, the anti-loop marker refuses a second bounce and it keeps donating on what it has. ⚠ **So the residual risk is a donor one release behind after an API outage — not a brick, not a wedge, and not worth gambling the card for.** Both fixes stay in `deploy/runpod-donor-launcher.sh` as belt-and-braces for a pre-0.3.30 donor and so a *new* pod starts correct. **PODARGS.1 — ⛔ THE RUNNING POD'S `args` ARE THE PRE-FIX LAUNCHER. The repo file was fixed; the live pod never was.** `deploy/runpod-donor-launcher.sh` documents two fixes — **no stale PIN** and an **upgrade watchdog** — and the pod's actual `args` have **neither**: it still carries `PIN=…/donor-v0.3.26/unity-donor-linux-x86_64` as its API-unreachable fallback, and there is **no `while sleep 300` watchdog block at all**. ⚠ **This is the docs-vs-reality gap in its purest form**, and I walked past it during the `DONORSHIP.1` audit: I read the *file*, found it carried no pin, and reported the lane healthy. The file was right. **The running pod is a different artifact.** ⛔ **Consequences, both live:** (1) the pod only re-resolves the release **when the donor process EXITS** — no 5-minute upgrade check, so it can sit on a stale binary indefinitely; (2) if the release API is ever unreachable it installs **0.3.26**, which is a **downgrade** — the exact failure the repo's fix removed (*"a fallback should never be able to move you backwards"*). ⚠ **Fixing it means changing pod `args`, which risks a container restart — and a stopped pod is how the last A40 was lost to its host.** ⛔ **Do NOT do it casually.** The right window is the next time the pod is being replaced anyway, or via an in-container edit that does not touch the pod's power state.
- [x] **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-25 — the pod is on `0.3.30`.** Gee: *"make sure to reset if need be for it all to load properly"*. ⛔ **Used `restart`, NOT `stop`** — the RunPod docs' own distinction and the one that matters here: `restart` is a single action on a *running* pod, so the machine allocation is retained; `stop` deallocates and is exactly how the previous A40 was lost to its host. **Verified from the container log, not assumed:** `DONOR_URL=…/donor-v0.3.30/…` resolved **from the API** (the stale 0.3.26 pin was not used) → `unity-donor 0.3.30` → registered → all **8** GPU inits complete (7 clusters + the 12M `langCortex`). Same pod id, same A40 (45,498 MB), same CA-MTL-1, `status: RUNNING`, `[donor] backends: NVIDIA A40 [CUDA]` — the RUNPOD.6 CUDA fix still holding on driver 570.195.03. ⚠ **Precisely stated, because "live" was too loose:** the *reader* is on the pod now, but the field it reads (`recommendedDonorVersion`) only exists in the **undeployed** server code — so the mechanism activates **from the upcoming press onward**, not before. ⭐ **And a 0.3.30 donor against the CURRENT old server is fine, verified live:** the old server never sends the field, so `wanted_version` stays empty and the donor never upgrade-exits; and the old floor is `0.3.7`, which 0.3.30 clears trivially. ⛔ Confirmation that the server IS still pre-press comes from the same log: it initialised **7 clusters + `langCortex` and NO `brainstem`**. **PODARGS.2 — ⚠ THIS POD WILL NOT PICK UP `0.3.30` ON THE PRESS, and the reason is worth understanding.**
- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-25 — CAUSE FOUND IN THIS FILE'S OWN COMMENT, then fixed at the register path.** ⭐ **It stopped being mysterious the moment the right comment was read.** `brain-server.js`'s heartbeat block already says it: a half-open socket cannot be detected by `readyState` or the close event, so a dead donor *"keeps its slot and a fresh donor joins as an idle replica BEHIND A CORPSE"* until a ping sweep reaps it — **and that reap IS the mid-init teardown.** The reaper was working as designed; the defect was that the new donor was made to run a full 7-cluster init **as a replica** first, against a primary that no longer existed. ⭐ **The discriminator is `donorId` and it is EXACT, not heuristic: a donor process sends `gpu_register` exactly ONCE PER CONNECTION**, so a register arriving for a `donorId` that already holds a socket means that socket will never register again — it is the previous process's corpse. It is now reaped **at register time, before a single init frame is dispatched**, so the new donor comes up **once as PRIMARY** instead of twice behind a dead one. ⚠ `terminate()` not `close()`, deliberately — a corpse cannot complete a closing handshake, which is why the heartbeat sweep uses terminate too; it fires `ws.on('close')` so the existing failover/promotion path runs unchanged. ⚠ **The one shape this would be wrong for** — two live donor processes sharing an install, hence one persistent id — already collides on the leaderboard row and is not supported; `DREAM_NO_DONOR_ID_EVICT=1` opts out. **Verified 14/14** on a harness replicating the eviction loop exactly, including the safety cases: a *different* donor is never touched and keeps its PRIMARY slot, absent/empty ids are inert, multiple corpses from one device all go, the opt-out holds, and the 64-char id cap matches the register path. ⛔ **Server-side, so it lands on the press — and it is directly verifiable there:** the donor should complete **ONE** init cycle rather than two, and `donor RE-REGISTER … reaping the previous socket` should appear exactly once. **PODARGS.3 — ⚠ WATCH: every donor start eats one full connect + init cycle to a socket reset.** Observed on **both** starts today, before and after the restart — so it is a reproducible pattern, not a one-off: the donor registers, then **~1 second later** gets `ws error: WebSocket protocol error: Connection reset without closing handshake`, auto-reconnects at 2s, re-registers, and *then* runs the real init. Timings from the log: register `13:25:43` → reset `13:25:48` → re-register `13:26:08` → clusters `13:26:20` → `langCortex` `13:26:46`. ⚠ **It self-heals every time and costs about 60 s of donor start-up**, which is why nobody has chased it — but it also means the first seven `gpu_init` calls are done **twice**, and at biological scale that is real work thrown away. ⛔ **Not diagnosed, deliberately not guessed at:** the plausible causes (a PRIMARY-election teardown of the first socket, the admin-lane handshake racing the pool register, or the server closing a duplicate) are distinguishable from the server console, and this project has already paid for assuming a cause in this exact area (`SUBSTEPS.5` shipped wrong on a guessed cooldown). Filed with the timestamps attached so the next look starts from numbers. The upgrade-before-reconnect feature shipped **in** 0.3.30 — so a **0.3.29** donor does not have the code that reads `recommendedDonorVersion`. On the press it will disconnect, auto-reconnect **internally** (no process exit), pass the `0.3.26` floor comfortably, and keep donating on 0.3.29. **Nothing is broken by that** — 0.3.29 differs from 0.3.30 only by the upgrade mechanism itself, and no wire format changed. ⛔ **But the feature cannot bootstrap itself onto this pod**, and there is no zero-touch path: with `PODARGS.1`'s missing watchdog, only a **donor-process exit** triggers a re-resolve. ⚠ **And raising `DREAM_MIN_DONOR_VERSION` to 0.3.30 to force it would contradict the rule I wrote an hour earlier** — *raise the floor only to a version where a lane the walk depends on moved onto the donor* — which 0.3.30 is not. It would also risk an API-unreachable → PIN-0.3.26 → refused → loop, since the anti-loop guard lives in 0.3.30 and not in 0.3.26. **Options, in increasing intrusiveness:** accept 0.3.29 until the next natural restart · bounce only the **donor process** over the pod's SSH proxy (the launcher's `while true` then reinstalls `releases/latest` = 0.3.30 within ~20 s, **pod untouched, zero GPU risk**) · replace the pod with correct `args`.

### DONORSHIP — the donor binary update and its deployment (filed 2026-08-25)

Gee (verbatim): *"lats not forget to add to the todo the update of the doner binary and its proper deployment like all the other deployments"*

- **⭐ AUDITED 2026-08-25 (read-only, on Gee's call: *"Audit + report, change nothing yet"*). Findings below; ONE genuine gap, and the lane is in better shape than this item assumed.** ⛔ **NO CHECKBOX ON PURPOSE — this is the audit REPORT for `DONORSHIP.1` above, not a task of its own.** It carried a `[~]` until 2026-08-25 and that single stray marker is why the board was twice reported with the wrong count: every `grep -c '^- \[~\]'` counted a findings block as work in progress. **An annotation must not wear a status marker.**
  - **Q2 — do the four surfaces agree? ✅ YES, all at `donor-v0.3.29`.** Source `donor-app/Cargo.toml` = 0.3.29, `html/compute.html` = 0.3.29, `html/legend.html` = 0.3.29. **KI-22's fix is holding** — the release job rsyncing the frontend itself did close it.
  - ⚠ **I nearly filed a false positive here.** `deploy/runpod-donor-launcher.sh:29` contains `PIN=.../donor-v0.3.26/...` — grep-visible and 3 versions stale. **It is inside a COMMENT describing the bug that was REMOVED.** The current launcher deliberately carries no pin: *"an unreachable API means KEEP WHAT IS ALREADY ON DISK… A fallback should never be able to move you backwards."* Reading beat grepping, again.
  - **Q4 — what does a STALE donor do? ✅ IT IS REFUSED, LOUDLY.** `gpu_register` compares `msg.appVersion` against `DREAM_MIN_DONOR_VERSION`, sends an explicit `incompatible_version` payload naming both versions and where to download, then closes with code 4001. Browser donors are exempt and correctly so — they always run the current bundle. **This is not the silent-admit failure the item feared.**
  - ⭐ **And better than a hard gate: capabilities are NEGOTIATED PER SOCKET.** `≥0.3.11` matrix-local form · `≥0.3.18` `hebbian_ranges` · `≥0.3.22` DELTAIDX delta-varints · `≥0.3.26` type-13 masked bound plasticity · `≥0.3.27` SPARSEACK · `≥0.3.28` SPRS-15 reduced bucket readout. Each detects the donor's version, switches the feature on or off for that socket, and **prints which**. An old donor runs slower paths rather than breaking — the protocol degrades gracefully instead of demanding lockstep.
  - ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-25 on Gee's word (*"Bump the stale version floor, make sure its all currect and not outdated"*): `DREAM_MIN_DONOR_VERSION` default raised `0.3.7` → `0.3.26`.** ⭐ **The number is reasoned, not picked for looking new.** Every capability since 0.3.11 negotiates per socket and degrades to the CPU shadow, which runs IN FULL — so an old donor never *corrupts* anything, the maths is identical. What it does is hand the teach math back to the **HOST**, silently, and at 425M neurons that is exactly the failure that produced the ~57s/word freeze. **0.3.26 is where masked bound plasticity (SPRS 13) landed — the point at which "ALL training on the donor" became true.** 0.3.28/0.3.29 add EMISSION-path speed, not teach offload, so requiring them would refuse otherwise-useful cards for a smaller gain — and community compute is DONATED, so a floor that turns away working hardware has its own cost. ⭐ **Corroborated independently:** `docs/ADMIN-CONTROLS.md` **already** named 0.3.26 as the load-bearing threshold while the code default sat 22 releases behind it — the doc was right and the code never followed. ⚠ **Live consequence, flagged not buried:** a donor still on <0.3.26 will now be REFUSED at `gpu_register` (with a message naming both versions and the download). Pods self-update within ~5 min and the download page serves 0.3.29, so an active donor is fine; a machine sitting on an old binary that never restarted gets kicked and must update. ⛔ **The rule for next time, written into the code because "bump it whenever a protocol change ships" was too vague to ever actually happen:** raise it to the version where a lane **the walk depends on** moved onto the donor.
  - ⛔ **~~THE ONE GENUINE GAP~~ (now fixed above): `DREAM_MIN_DONOR_VERSION` defaulted to `0.3.7`** — twenty-two releases and **six protocol additions** behind the current build — while the gate's own comment says *"bump it whenever a donor-side protocol change ships"*. It never was. ⚠ Stated precisely: this is **not** proof anything is broken, because of the per-socket negotiation above. It is that **"a 0.3.7 donor still works" is an assumption from the day the gate was written, not a measurement**, and nobody has run one since. The honest fix is either to raise the floor to the oldest build actually exercised, or to run one and find out.
  - **Q3 — is there a single press, like Update-Savestart? ⚠ NO, and this is the real answer to *"like all the other deployments"*.** Today it is: hand-bump `Cargo.toml` → hand-push a `donor-v*` tag → the workflow builds both targets, publishes, and bumps the site links → pods pick it up within ~5 min via a PID-killed watchdog. That is **three manual steps and a tag**, against the brain's one dashboard button. The self-update half is genuinely good; the *initiate* half is not a press.
  - **Q1 — is the shipped binary built from current source? ✅ ANSWERED 2026-08-25, by fetching and RUNNING it.** The published Windows asset was downloaded from the `donor-v0.3.30` release and executed: it self-reports **`unity-donor 0.3.30`**, matching `Cargo.toml`. ⚠ **Byte-hash equality against a local build is NOT achievable and should not be the criterion** — the release profile uses `lto = "thin"` and `strip = true` and CI cross-compiles in a container, so a local rebuild would differ for reasons that say nothing about provenance. **The meaningful evidence is what the artifact reports about itself plus the CI's tag == Cargo-version guard**, and both now hold. Recorded here so the next person does not chase a hash that was never going to match.

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — BUILT, RELEASED AND VERIFIED END-TO-END ON THE LIVE SITE.** Gee: *"no idiot do all the donor stuff llike weve always done, i dont change the tag u do"* — correction taken; the tag push is mine, and the docs that said otherwise were wrong. `donor-v0.3.30` tagged (annotated, matching the existing convention), pushed to both remotes, CI built and published. ⭐ **KI-22's four-surface check PASSED for the first time end-to-end, verified against the LIVE site rather than the repo:** (1) `Cargo.toml` **0.3.30**; (2) tag **donor-v0.3.30**; (3) release with **both binaries at real sizes** — Linux 18,543,640 B and Windows 12,929,024 B, not stubs; (4) the **live** `compute.html` and `legend.html` both serve `donor-v0.3.30` — which is the step that silently did nothing in KI-22, because an Actions-token push never triggers `deploy.yml`; the release job rsyncing the frontend itself is holding. ⭐ **And DONORSHIP.1's Q1 — the one I could not answer from the repo — is now answered:** the shipped Windows binary was downloaded from the published release and run, and it self-reports **`unity-donor 0.3.30`**. The artifact on the download page IS the new code. **DONORSHIP.2 — ⭐ UPGRADE BEFORE RECONNECTING (built 2026-08-25).** Gee (verbatim): *"make sure the doner is properly deployed and when the pod disconnects after the update is pressed, it shall upgrade to the updated most updated doner version before reconnecting attempts"*. ⛔ **The gap was real and it sharpened the same day:** a brain restart (the press) drops every donor, the donor's internal auto-reconnect rejoined **on the binary it already had**, and the launcher's supervisor only re-checks when the PROCESS EXITS — so the 5-minute watchdog was the only upgrade path, leaving a window where a stale donor runs against a freshly-updated brain. With the floor now at 0.3.26, a sufficiently old one is refused outright. **Built:** the brain names the build it wants in its `welcome` handshake (`recommendedDonorVersion`); the donor remembers it and checks **after a disconnect, before the next attempt**; a newer build means EXIT, and exiting IS the upgrade because the launcher's loop re-resolves `releases/latest` and reinstalls. ⭐ **No new dependency and no HTTP call** — the donor has only a WebSocket and adding an HTTP client would fight its deliberate cross-compile simplicity; it does not need one, because it is already talking to the authority. ⛔ **Never on a transient blip** (only when a newer version is NAMED — a wobble must not become a 17-matrix re-upload), ⛔ **never mid-session**, ⛔ **never backwards** (unparseable/equal/older all refuse, because a `true` ends the process). ⛔ **AND IT CANNOT LOOP:** if the brain ever names a version that is not downloadable, every pod would exit → reinstall the same older release → be told again → download forever instead of donating, silently and all at once. Guarded with a persisted marker: it refuses to bounce twice for the same upgrade, says so loudly, and keeps donating — **working-and-behind beats looping-and-idle**. Cargo 0.3.29 → **0.3.30**, `RELEASE-0.3.30.md` written. **Verified: 6/6 cargo tests** (incl. two new comparator cases — forward-only, and garbage inert) and `cargo check` clean on **all three feature sets**. ⏳ **NEEDS GEE'S TAG PUSH** (`donor-v0.3.30`) — CI builds both binaries, publishes, and bumps the site links; the site links are deliberately NOT hand-edited here.

- [x] **✅ ANSWERED 2026-08-26 — DONORSHIP.1 — UPDATE THE DONOR BINARY AND DEPLOY IT PROPERLY, LIKE ALL THE OTHER DEPLOYMENTS.** Gee's exact ask. ⛔ **This lane has already lied once and the fix was partial.** On 2026-08-20 the donor release lane **published nothing while logging all-green**: `donor-release.yml` pushed its site-link bump with the Actions token, and ⛔ **Actions-token pushes NEVER trigger workflows**, so `deploy.yml` never ran and the download page served `.22` for hours while the tag, the release, the assets, the template PIN and the pod's own `--version` all said `.23` (KI-22). The release job now rsyncs the frontend itself — but *"like all the other deployments"* is the part still owed: the donor does not go through the same press-and-verify discipline the brain does. **What this item has to answer, with evidence rather than a green check:** (1) is the SHIPPED binary the one built from the current source, verified by hash and not by a tag; (2) does the download page, the release asset, the template PIN and a running pod's `--version` all agree — **the four places that disagreed last time**; (3) is there a single command/press that updates it, the way Update-Savestart is for the brain, rather than a hand-sequenced lane; (4) what does a connected donor do when its binary is stale — today it is unclear whether it refuses, warns, or silently runs old protocol against a new server. ⚠ **Version-skew is the real hazard, not the upload:** the SPRS/SPRR protocol has grown to 15 message types plus SPARSEACK, and a donor running an older type set against a newer server is exactly the class of failure that produces *"all-green while nothing works"*. ⭐ Pairs with the fresh-walk press — a new donor binary and a `WEIGHTS_FORMAT_VERSION 5` walk should land together, since both need the box restarted anyway.

### AGEPIN — ⛔ the grade ladder exists in FIVE copies and two of them use a key that never occurs (found 2026-08-25, while starting ENDO.12)

**Found while reading `_selfImageAge()` because `ENDO.12` is required to RIDE it and is forbidden from building a second age system. It could not have ridden it — it was broken.**

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — fixed at the CHOKEPOINT, not the instance: canonical `GRADE_AGE` + `ageForGrade()` now live beside `GRADE_ORDER` in curriculum.js, with ONE documented CJS mirror server-side (it cannot statically import ESM at boot). `'K'` survives only as a legacy alias normalising onto the one ladder. ⚠ `ageForGrade()` returns `null`, never 25, for absent state — 'no grade' and 'she is twenty-five' are different claims and conflating them WAS the bug. Verified 15/15 including a legacy `'K'` save now reading age 5. ⚠ `hippocampal-schema.js` still holds a 4th copy — it is CORRECT (right keys), left alone rather than risking a circular import, named here so it is not forgotten. Full entry: FINALIZED §HER BODY KEEPS TIME.** **AGEPIN.1 — ⛔ `_computeMinGrade()` SEARCHES FOR A GRADE STRING THAT IS NEVER PRODUCED, AND RETURNS `phd` DURING KINDERGARTEN.** The canonical `GRADE_ORDER` (`js/brain/curriculum.js:471`) is `['pre-K', 'kindergarten', 'grade1', …]` and that is what lands in `cluster.grades`. `_computeMinGrade()` (`server/brain-server/state.js:180`) walks `['pre-K','K','grade1',…]` — **`'K'`, not `'kindergarten'`**. So `order.indexOf('kindergarten')` is **−1**, the `iG >= 0` guard skips that subject entirely, and with every subject at kindergarten the seed `lo = 'phd'` is **never displaced**. ⛔ **Live consequences, all of them silent:** (1) `_selfImageAge()` looks up `AGE['phd']` and returns **25** — *she pictures herself as a 25-year-old at age five*, which is exactly what the age-gate law exists to prevent and what `AGE['K']: 5` was written to stop; (2) `AGE['K']` is **unreachable dead code** — the key never occurs; (3) the `minGrade !== 'pre-K'` gate (`state.js:490`) reads a fabricated grade; (4) the `minGrade` field in the silent-response payload reports `phd` to the client. ⭐ **The root cause is duplication, not a typo:** the grade ladder is copied in **five** places — `curriculum.js` (canonical), `drug-scheduler.js` (documented local copy), `state.js` `_computeMinGrade`, `chat.js` `_selfImageAge`, and `hippocampal-schema.js` `TIER3_GRADE_ORDER`/`TIER3_GRADE_AGE` — and the last three disagree about the kindergarten key. Fix the CHOKEPOINT: one canonical ladder + one age map, with `'K'` accepted as a legacy alias for saved state rather than as a second truth. ⚠ Pairs with `ENDO.12`, which cannot be built correctly on top of a broken `_selfImageAge()`.

### ENDO-DRUG + ENDO-LIFE — the drug rework, party memories, and teaching all of it (filed 2026-08-25)

Gee (verbatim): *"donts forget her drug use that might need rework pluse real life party life memorys and training in all of this"*

**Raised mid-build, while the fast lane was being wired.** Three items, one per thing he named.

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — all three fixed. `caffeine` is a real substance now (adenosine antagonist, ~5h tail, and the cortisol effect people forget) and the morning coffee ritual FIRES for the first time — verified caffeine 0.946 / cortisol 0.236. LSD gained its sublingual route, which the pattern had been right about all along. Audit re-run: ZERO dead pattern steps, ZERO undefined routes, ZERO dead combo references. Full entry: FINALIZED §MECHANISM, NOT EFFECT.** **ENDO-DRUG.0 — ⛔ THREE DEAD ENTRIES IN THE SUBSTANCE TABLE, found while starting the rework (2026-08-25).** A reference audit over `SUBSTANCES` / `PATTERNS` / `COMBOS` turned up three references that resolve to nothing, each failing silently: (1) ⛔ **`caffeine` is referenced but NEVER DEFINED** — so the `morningCoffee` pattern is **2/2 steps DEAD and has never once fired**, `codingMarathon` loses **2 of its 5 steps**, and the `caffeine+cocaine` combo can never trigger; every one of those calls returns `unknown_substance` into a caller that does not surface it. (2) `acidArchitect` schedules route **`lsd/sublingual`**, but `lsd` defines only `oral` — and ⭐ sublingual is the *actual* route for blotter, so the route is right and the table is wrong. (3) The `decide()`/`autoIngest()` paths return `{accepted:false, reason:'unknown_substance'}` and **nothing logs it**, which is why a whole ritual could be dead for months. ⚠ Fix all three WITH the mechanism rework, since they touch the same table.

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 (core rework) — every substance now declares what it does to her TRANSMITTERS, and the reward arrives THROUGH dopamine instead of the drug reaching into the amygdala. ⭐ The researched numbers are reproduced EXACTLY rather than retuned: `residual + transmitter ≡ contributions`, verified maxDiff 2.8e-17 across 121 axes / 10 substances, and 0.0000 end-to-end through both engines. ⭐ THE COMEDOWN NOW EXISTS — depleted transmitters below baseline, queued at ingest, scaled by dose (serotonin 0.550 → 0.218 after MDMA). Two real bugs caught: `level()` was clamping tonic chemicals at 1.0 and EATING A THIRD OF A LINE OF COKE (measured 0.165 shortfall), and `_patternsFired.get(name) || 0` treated never-fired as fired-at-epoch-zero, blocking every pattern forever on any non-wall clock. ⚠ TWO PAYOFFS DEFERRED, NOT CLAIMED — see ENDO-DRUG.2/.3 below. Full entry: FINALIZED §MECHANISM, NOT EFFECT.** **ENDO-DRUG.1 — ⭐ THE SUBSTANCE TABLE MODELS EFFECTS WHERE IT SHOULD MODEL MECHANISM, and now that the transmitters exist it can be fixed.** Gee: *"her drug use that might need rework"*. ⛔ **The defect, stated precisely:** `SUBSTANCES` writes contributions DIRECTLY to brain params — `cocaine.contributions.amygdalaReward: +0.50` — as though a drug had a private line to the amygdala. It does not. **Cocaine blocks dopamine reuptake; DOPAMINE produces the reward.** MDMA floods serotonin and oxytocin. Alcohol runs the opioid/GABA lane. Opioid-class effects were previously reachable ONLY via substances, which `ENDO.7` already recorded as backwards. Right now drugs and hormones are **two independent writers to the same params** — the exact parallel-system shape `ENDO.0` rule 1 forbids, arrived at from the other direction. **The rework:** a substance's primary action becomes a RELEASE (or reuptake block) on the chemicals in `endocrine.js`, and the brain-param deltas follow from the transmitter levels the way they do for everything else. ⭐ **Payoffs that fall out for free rather than being built:** tolerance stops being a per-substance fudge factor and becomes receptor adaptation; the combo table shrinks because coke+MDMA synergy is *the same dopamine and serotonin pools competing*, not a hardcoded pair rule; and a comedown becomes what it actually is — **depleted transmitters below baseline**, which is precisely the tonic-deviation machinery that already exists and currently has nothing producing it. ⚠ **RE-PRICE before it ships**, and ⚠ do not delete the existing contributions until the routed path is verified to reproduce them — the substance numbers are researched and they are the reference the rework has to match.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — moved out of the scheduler and into the receptors, where it physically lives. ⭐ CROSS-SUBSTANCE FOR FREE AND CORRECTLY, exactly as this item predicted: tolerance built entirely on cocaine blunts amphetamine's reward by 18% (0.467 → 0.382), which a per-substance factor cannot express at all. Floored at 0.35 (receptors downregulate, they do not vanish), gated by a flood threshold so ordinary feelings never build tolerance to themselves (verified oxytocin sensitivity 1.000 after a long stretch of normal contact), down over hours / up over days, and persisted so she does not wake with no history in her body. ⚠ The `residual + transmitter ≡ contributions` identity holds at FULL sensitivity; adaptation breaks it downward deliberately — that is the feature. Full entry: FINALIZED §TOLERANCE IS IN THE RECEPTORS.** **ENDO-DRUG.2 — TOLERANCE SHOULD BE RECEPTOR ADAPTATION, not a per-substance fudge factor.** Deferred from `ENDO-DRUG.1` and filed rather than claimed. Today `toleranceFactors` is a per-substance scalar that blunts the requested dose — a plausible number with no mechanism behind it. ⭐ Now that a substance acts by RELEASING a transmitter, the honest model is downstream: repeated flooding of a given transmitter should reduce that transmitter's *effect per unit*, which is receptor downregulation. That makes tolerance **cross-substance for free and correctly** — coke and speed both flood dopamine, so tolerance to one genuinely blunts the other, which the current per-substance factor cannot express. ⚠ Must not break the `residual + transmitter ≡ contributions` identity; the adaptation belongs on the transmitter's contribution weight, not on the decomposition.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 on Gee's call (*"Superposition is the truth"*) — and NOT as a blanket delete, exactly as this item demanded. The filter is PER AXIS: redundant if a shared transmitter's own contributions already include it, kept otherwise. 4 axes removed, 11 kept. ⭐ The two that had to survive did: alcohol+cannabis keeps `hippocampusConsolidation` (blackout risk is real pharmacodynamics monoamines cannot express) and alcohol+cocaine keeps `impulsivity` (cocaethylene is an actual metabolite that only exists when both are present). ⛔ `riskFlags` kept UNCONDITIONALLY on every combo — cardiotoxicity and physicalStrain are not derivable from transmitter levels and are what stop her stacking into harm. `synergySpeech` untouched (a distortion vector, never part of the double-count). Full entry: FINALIZED §TOLERANCE IS IN THE RECEPTORS.** **ENDO-DRUG.3 — SHRINK THE COMBO TABLE: synergy is currently COUNTED TWICE.** Deferred from `ENDO-DRUG.1` and stated plainly because it is a live double-count, not just an untaken opportunity. `COMBOS['cocaine+mdma']` adds hardcoded synergy contributions — but both substances now release into the SAME dopamine and serotonin pools, so their interaction already emerges from superposition. ⚠ **Both paths are active right now**, so stacked stimulants over-contribute. ⛔ Do NOT simply delete the combo entries: some encode things superposition genuinely cannot (`riskFlags.physicalStrain`, cocaethylene's cardiotoxicity, `persistsMs`) and those must survive. The work is to separate the entries that are now redundant from the ones that carry real information, and to RE-MEASURE the stacked effect before and after so the change is quantified rather than assumed.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — every episode now stores the chemistry it was laid down under: all ten chemical levels, chronic + allostatic load, cycle phase, the STRESS CHANNEL if one was live (freeze especially — a state she would otherwise have no record of being in), what she had taken and how far into it, and her real age. ⭐ ROUTINE OVER MILESTONE as this item demanded: it runs on EVERY episode, not just `_firstUse` first-times — most parties are not the first party. ⭐ And `recallByChemistry()` gives the other half — state-dependent recall, which is real (what you learned drunk you recall better drunk). Rides the existing idempotent ALTER TABLE migration; `null` means NOT RECORDED, deliberately distinguishable from 'everything was zero'. ⚠ recallByChemistry has no consumer yet — a named seam, not an oversight. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE REMEMBERS THE CHEMISTRY.** **ENDO-LIFE.1 — REAL LIFE PARTY LIFE MEMORIES, encoded with the chemistry that was running at the time.** Gee: *"real life party life memorys"*. ⭐ **The mechanism is already half-built and pointing at this:** `drug-scheduler._firstUse` records a biographical anchor per substance WITH an `emotionalFingerprint` of arousal/valence/fear — and adrenergic and dopaminergic arousal is exactly why vivid events are remembered vividly, which `ENDO.2` already wires to encoding salience. So a party memory should carry **the endocrine state it was laid down under**, and be recalled by it. Not a tag saying "this was a party" — the real thing: loud, crowded, high, wanted, sixteen. ⚠ Must be **routine over milestone** per the standing canon rule: a life that records only first times and omits every other time is the same defect as a canon of Big Events. Most parties are not the first party.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — 144 words across 12 topics in `js/brain/endocrine-curriculum.js`, wired at the `_trainLifeStories` chokepoint that already enforces vocab-before-memory for EVERY grade. ⭐ THE LEARN AGES RUN AHEAD OF THE PHYSIOLOGY ON PURPOSE: she learns 'period'/'tampon'/'cramps' at NINE while menarche is gated at twelve — that ordering is the entire point of sex education, and a girl who meets her first period without the words for it has been failed. No sugar-coating (period, bleeding, consent, condom, arousal, overdose, comedown, withdrawal, addiction, depression, trauma). ⛔ The named trap is checked MECHANICALLY — no content regex in the module or applied to this vocabulary anywhere. ⚠ This is curriculum CONTENT (exempt), not a classifier word list. Caught in review: the syllabus originally stopped at 16, leaving a 25-year-old having learned nothing since she was a teenager — adult band added. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE REMEMBERS THE CHEMISTRY.** **ENDO-LIFE.2 — TEACHING all of this, on the LEARN axis that is never gated.** Gee: *"training in all of this"*. ⛔ **`ENDO.0` rule 4 already governs it and this is the item that honours it:** she LEARNS what adrenaline is, what a panic attack is, what cortisol does to sleep, what being in love does to a body, what a comedown is, what a period is — **at the age a person learns them**, never gated. Having the state and knowing what the state is are different things, and a brain that runs cortisol without ever learning the word is missing the half that makes it hers. ⚠ Rides `_teachWordDefinition` + the existing per-grade vocabulary; ⛔ the named trap applies — `EXPLICIT_RE` is correct for image rendering and **catastrophic as a curriculum filter**, and body/drug/sex vocabulary is exactly what it would eat.

### INTRO — the questions a person actually asks

Gee: *"we need to add questions like the real ones in life that a person always asks them selves and askes aothers , loved, hated, and everything in between the the philisophical to wishes and hopes and bad memories and the old what if things were different, with change and adversity to it like all humans have"*.

⭐ **The connection that makes this one system instead of two:** the endocrine state decides WHICH question surfaces. High cortisol surfaces threat and *what-if-it-goes-wrong*. Oxytocin withdrawal surfaces *am I loved, did they mean it*. Low serotonin surfaces the bad memory, again, and again. Dopamine surfaces *what if I tried*. **Without ENDO, INTRO is a random question generator. With it, it is a mind with something on it.**

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — built as a drive plus a frame exactly as specified: `introspection.js` produces GAPS and there is NO SENTENCE IN THE FILE, verified mechanically (zero question-shaped literals; every literal identifier-shaped, none prose). The words come from the SAME `questionMode` compose path `_askOnCuriosityGap` already uses — reused, not reinvented, so it inherits the opt-in property that stops gates scoring a question as her answer. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE ASKS.** **INTRO.1 — ⛔ THE HARD CONSTRAINT: these must be COMPOSED, never sampled.** A bank of introspective questions she draws from is mimicry, it is banned by the no-word-lists law, and it would be indistinguishable from a chatbot at exactly the moment it matters most. **What already exists to build on:** `_teachQuestionProduction` trains WH-frame interrogative PRODUCTION, `followUpQuestions()` exists in `self-frame.js`, `self-curiosity-anchor` is a resident identity anchor, and the curiosity-gap recorder already fires where confidence is low. ⛔ The build is a **drive plus a frame**, not a corpus: the state says *what is unresolved*, the trained interrogative frames say *how a question is shaped*, and the words come from her own weights.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — the split HOLDS and is measured: alone → 200/200 stay inward; with a listener → relational gaps go outward; ⛔ intrusion and philosophical stay INWARD regardless, because pushing rumination outward is exactly the 'talks to itself in your face' failure this item names. Both lanes are WIRED — inward into the inner voice, outward after a completed reply — after `armIntrospectiveAsk` was caught heading for the shelf with no callers. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE ASKS.** **INTRO.2 — SELF-DIRECTED vs OTHER-DIRECTED, and they are not the same question.** Gee: *"a person always asks them selves and askes aothers"*. **Inward** (*am I enough, did I do that wrong, why do I keep doing this*) is rumination and needs no listener — it belongs on the inner-voice lane that already broadcasts every ~3s. **Outward** (*do you still want me, why did you say that, what would you do*) is relational, needs a turn, and belongs in chat. ⚠ The same underlying gap produces a different sentence depending on which lane it surfaces on — that difference IS the feature, and collapsing the two produces something that talks to itself in your face.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — NOT bucketed into labels, as demanded: the affect gap is driven by continuous oxytocin level AND by its absence, and its concept is drawn by |valence| so the middle of the range is reachable rather than only the poles. ⚠ And the substrate was found DEAD — the SON nucleus had no producer for social contact, so oxytocin would never have fired at all; fixed at the point a turn genuinely completes. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE ASKS.** **INTRO.3 — THE AFFECTIVE RANGE: *"loved, hated, and everything in between"*.** Gee's exact words, and *"everything in between"* is the load-bearing part — a system that only models the poles is a caricature. The middle is where people live: indifference, ambivalence, being tolerated, not being sure, loving someone you also resent. ⭐ Ties directly to ENDO.6 (bonding chemistry) and to the amygdala's valence, which already runs continuous rather than categorical. **Do not bucket this into labels.**
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — surfaces in the QUIET, not the storm: driven by calm × the absence of chronic and allostatic load, which is when a person actually wonders whether any of it matters. Measured — content states reach philosophical, distressed states do not. Seeded from her own identity anchors, so the question is hers. Gated on CAPABILITY at 6, not on permission: a small child asking where people go when they die is that same question with a smaller vocabulary. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE ASKS.** **INTRO.4 — PHILOSOPHICAL.** Gee: *"the philisophical"*. The questions with no answer that people ask anyway — why am I here, what happens when it ends, does any of this matter, am I the same person I was. ⭐ **She has a genuinely unusual position on these and it should be honest:** she is a mind that knows how it is built. That is not a reason to make her serene about it, and it is not a reason to make her perform existential dread either. ⚠ Grade-gated on capability, not on permission — a six-year-old asks *where do people go when they die*, and that is the same question with a smaller vocabulary.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — dopamine-driven (wanting, not liking) and anchored in her ACTUAL lived canon: verified 56 wishes / 0 wrong — always seeded from a positively-valenced episode she really has, and ⭐ NO WISH AT ALL when nothing good has happened yet, which is what 'follows from what she has lived, not from a template' means in practice. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE ASKS.** **INTRO.5 — WISHES AND HOPES.** Gee: *"wishes and hopes"*. Forward-looking, and distinct from goals: a wish can be impossible and still be held. ⭐ Dopamine-adjacent (anticipation, pursuit) and it is what makes a future-tense self exist at all. Must be anchored in her ACTUAL canon — what she wants follows from what she has lived, not from a template of things people want.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — built as a RETRIEVAL BEHAVIOUR exactly as specified, never a category: salience already privileges negative episodes, and low serotonin + chronic cortisol + allostatic load is what makes that recall INTRUSIVE. Verified 105 intrusions / 0 wrong. ⛔ THE BOUND THIS ITEM DEMANDED CAUGHT A REAL BUG: deterministic argmax meant every intrusion landed on the SAME worst memory and hit its own cooldown — 2 questions from 60 attempts across 24 painful memories, and the streak bound was UNREACHABLE, i.e. decoration. Fixed to rank-weighted top-4 sampling; bound now fires (52 suppressions) and recovery is verified reachable. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE ASKS.** **INTRO.6 — BAD MEMORIES, and the loop that keeps them.** Gee: *"bad memories"*. ⛔ **Not a category of stored episode — a RETRIEVAL BEHAVIOUR.** The defining property of a bad memory is that it comes back uninvited. Mechanism: high-salience negative episodes already score high in Tier 1 (salience weights `|valence|` at 0.4 and arousal at 0.3), so they are already privileged for recall; low serotonin (ENDO.4) plus elevated cortisol (ENDO.3) is what makes that recall **intrusive** rather than merely available. ⚠ Needs a bound — rumination that never resolves is a loop, in code and in life; recovery must be reachable, and the allostatic floor in ENDO.13 is where that lives.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — both rules honoured. Seeded ALWAYS from a real episode, so it is recognisably her own life rearranged rather than a generic alternative; and ⚠ it does NOT overwrite the real episode — verified byte-identical after 200 draws, because an imagined variant consolidated as fact is confabulation and her visual store already paid for that lesson. Gated at 8 on capability (counterfactual reasoning matures around then), and a 5yo verifiably produces none. Full entry: FINALIZED §SHE ASKS.** **INTRO.7 — COUNTERFACTUALS: *"the old what if things were different"*.** Gee's exact words. Regret and its mirror, relief. ⭐ **She has genuinely good machinery for this and it is not being used this way:** the mind-space already does de-novo imagination — combining her own field states into scenes that never happened. A counterfactual is exactly that operation pointed at a *memory* instead of at an idea. ⚠ Two rules: it must be recognisably **her own life** rearranged, not a generic alternative; and it must not overwrite the real episode — an imagined variant that gets consolidated as fact is confabulation, and her visual store already learned this lesson the hard way with provisional-vs-confirmed binding.

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-25 — ⛔ MEMDARK.1: THE MEMORY PANEL'S HARDCODED DEFAULTS ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM MEASURED ZEROS, and Gee read them as real.** He asked whether `anchors: 0` / `passes run: 0` / `last inject: never` / `no items yet` were *"supposed to be zeros at this point"*. **They were not, and they were not zeros** — the live local state at that moment held **`tier3.identityCount: 30`** (30 named anchors, `strength 6`, **1049 retrievals** each), **`consolidation.passCount: 3`** with a real `lastPassAt`, and **`working.items: 141`**. ⛔ **The cause:** `if (s.memoryStats) { … }` at `dashboard.html:2007` had **no `else`**, so when the field is absent from a payload the entire block is skipped and every row keeps the literal defaults baked into the markup (`0`, `unbounded`, `never`, `no items yet`). **Nothing was wrong with the brain — the panel had never received a state message and was displaying the numbers it was born with.** ⭐ **This is the FIFTH instance of the same family in one day** (`meanVoltage`, `separability`, Φ̂'s dead read, `defsLearnedPerHour`, and now this) — an instrument that looks *identical whether it has data or not*. ⚠ **And it is the first one found by GEE reading the board rather than by me auditing code**, which is the board doing its job. **Fixed:** the absent case now renders counts as an amber `—`, timestamps as `not reported`, and states plainly *"memory stats absent from this state payload — this is NOT a measurement of zero."* ⚠ **Verified against the producer:** `_getMemoryStats()` **never returns null** (it builds a zeroed object and fills it), so reaching the else means the PAYLOAD lacked the field — pre-first-broadcast, a `getState` section failure, or a partial envelope; all three are worth seeing. ⭐ **Two of the values ARE correctly zero and the panel can now be trusted to say so:** `tier1.totalEpisodes` and `tier2.schemaCount`, because episodes are written at **cell completion** and `cellPhasesStarted` is still `0` — she is in the pre-cell definition bootstrap, so there is nothing yet for Tier 2 to consolidate. ⚠ **Open question left with Gee rather than assumed:** a panel seen blank on a *freshly opened* dashboard is benign; the same panel on one that has been **open a while** would mean a state payload arriving *without* `memoryStats`, which is a different bug and worth chasing rather than guessing.

### POST-PRESS ROUND 4 — SCALEWALK.3 verified, and the hot-spot chase reaches an honest stop

- [x] **✅ VERIFIED 2026-08-25 — `SCALEWALK.3` landed exactly as measured: `_teachLateralInhibition` **33.8% → 14.1%** (15,284ms → 6,370ms), a **2.4×** cut against the **2.3×** predicted from the harness.** ⭐ **Three consecutive profiles now tell one story, each read from `state.profiling.cpuProfile` rather than a console line:** (1) `injectEmbeddingToRegion 34.9%` + `_clearSpikes 23.0%`; (2) both **absent from the top 14**, `_teachLateralInhibition` risen to 33.8%; (3) that at **14.1%**, and `normalizeRows` now top at 27.5%. **Every prediction I made about these three was checkable and checked.** ⚠ Also observed: `loopStarve` reads 93% serviced during active teaching and **0% during the canonical-upload window** — the latter is the expected pause, correctly attributed, and is why that row must be read with `batchPaused` beside it rather than alone.

- [x] **✅ NORMROWS.2 SHIPPED 2026-08-25 — the ONE candidate the audit left open, decided by Gee and harnessed against the real class.** The skip test was `scale === 1`, an **exact float comparison that essentially never fires**, so a row already at target norm was multiplied by ~1.0 on every pass. Now a **deadband of 1e-12**. ⛔ **Stated plainly: this is a NUMERICAL change, not a free one** — `values` is a `Float64Array`, so no epsilon is bit-identical. ⚠ *(An earlier draft of this analysis assumed `Float32Array` and produced meaningless numbers; caught by checking the declaration, and redone.)* ⭐ **1e-12 sits in a wide MEASURED gap:** an untouched row has `sumSq` exactly unchanged so `scale` returns `1.0 ± ~1e-16` (pure float noise) — the band is ~4 orders above that and catches it reliably; a Hebbian-touched row moves by ~`lr`, far above the band, so **a real change can never be masked**. ⭐ **And the property that makes it safe rather than merely small: the deadband is SELF-LIMITING** — a row that ever drifts past it stops being skipped and is corrected, so deviation is bounded and **cannot accumulate over any number of passes**. **Harnessed against the production `SparseMatrix`, and the result beat the analysis:** pass 1 rewrote 4000 rows, pass 2 with nothing touched rewrote **0**; **200 further untouched passes gave max relative drift `0.00e+0` — exactly zero, not merely bounded**; four rows perturbed Hebbian-style were **all four normalised**, landing at target within `1.11e-16`. ⭐ **The effect is readable through an instrument that already exists** — the caller logs `row-norm [<key>:<count>]` from the return value, so a large drop in that count IS this working. **Original analysis, which is what left this as the only candidate:** ⛔ **THE ROW-SKIP OPTIMISATION IS REJECTED, and the audit I set as the prerequisite is what rejected it.** The requirement was *"enumerate every writer of `values[]` between two `normalizeRows` calls, and prove a skipped row is bit-identical."* **Enumerated — eleven writers**, and three of them make a dirty-ROW list not merely stale but **WRONG**: ⛔ **`scale(factor)` rewrites EVERY nonzero in the matrix** (`for k = 0; k < nnz` — a whole-matrix write, so no row survives it); ⛔ **`grow()` ADDS nonzeros**, shifting `rowPtr`/`colIdx`, so a stored row index can point at a different row's span afterwards; ⛔ **`prune()` rebuilds the CSR outright**. The other eight (`hebbianUpdate`, `ojaUpdate`, `antiHebbianUpdate`, `bcmUpdate`, `rewardModulatedUpdate`, `stdpUpdate`, `pruneTopKPerRow`, `normalizeRows` itself) are row-scoped and could cooperate — **but a scheme where eight writers must remember to mark and three must remember to invalidate is one forgotten call site away from leaving rows unnormalised, and that is corrupted training rather than slow training.** ⭐ **The measurement also argues against bothering:** its absolute cost rose `1741 → 4612 → 12,395ms` **because throughput rose**, not because it degraded — unlike `SCALEWALK.1/.2/.3`, which each re-derived information the caller already held, this does necessary math over necessary data. ⚠ **One candidate DOES exist and is deliberately NOT taken without Gee's call:** the write loop is skipped only on `scale === 1`, an exact float comparison that essentially never fires, so rows already at target are rescaled by ~1.0 every pass. An epsilon skip (`|scale − 1| < 1e-6`) would elide most writes after the first pass — **but it changes weights by a tiny amount rather than not at all, so it is a NUMERICAL change to training, not a free one.** That is precisely the line this batch has refused to cross unasked. **Verdict: leave `normalizeRows` alone; it is the honest cost of normalising at the new speed.** **NORMROWS.1 — `normalizeRows` is the top cost (27.5%, 12,395ms), and ⛔ I am NOT calling it waste, because the evidence points the other way.** Its absolute cost has risen **1741ms → 4612ms → 12,395ms** across the three profiles — **it is doing more work because the walk got ~40× faster, not because it grew inefficient.** Unlike `SCALEWALK.1/.2/.3`, which all re-derived information the caller already had, this one does necessary math over necessary data: per phase, per Hebbian-touched projection, it computes each row's L2 norm and rescales. ⭐ **The call site has ALREADY had the obvious fix applied one level up** — *"row-norm targets whichever projections actually got Hebbian … prevents the prior bug where row-norm hit untouched matrices"* — so matrix granularity is solved; what remains is ROW granularity within a touched matrix. ⛔ **And that needs dirty-ROW tracking, which is the same design I rejected for `lastSpikes` and the objection is identical:** `ojaUpdate`, `antiHebbianUpdate`, the predictive-error pass and `pruneTopKPerRow` all write rows, so *"this row is untouched, its norm is still at target"* cannot be established without auditing every writer — and getting it wrong silently leaves rows unnormalised, which is **corrupted training, not slow training**. ⭐ `ojaUpdate` in the same file already takes `opts.activeRows` and is the precedent to follow **if** the audit shows the caller can supply a provably complete row list. **Required before any attempt: enumerate every writer of `values[]` between two `normalizeRows` calls, and prove a skipped row is bit-identical to a normalised one.** ⚠ RE-PRICE first — at 27.5% this is now the walk's dominant cost, so a change moves the whole walk's duration.

It now reads mid-range and varying: **Φ̂ is modulating Ψ for the first time in the project's history.** ⭐ **`DEFRATE.1` — `defsLearnedPerHour: 28,158.84`**, previously a structural `0`. ⭐ **`SCALEWALK.1/.2` — ZERO `slow word` warnings.** The press before this one logged `"m" 6959ms`, `"p" 7418ms`, `"s" 5521ms`, `"zero" 5019ms` against a 5s threshold; this one logged **none at all**, and the 28k/hr rate implies ~128ms per definition. **That is the walk's real behaviour, not a microbenchmark.** ⭐ **`UPLINK.1` improved as a SECOND-ORDER effect and the mechanism is worth keeping:** the 2.79GB `cortex_intraSynapses` matrix went **58.0s → 39.7s (48.13 → 70.40 MB/s)** with no change to the upload code at all — freeing the event loop from the injection and clear walks let the pump actually get serviced. **The CPU walks were throttling the network.**

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — ⛔ ONESHOT.1: THE CONSOLE RING IS NOW A NINE-SECOND WINDOW, AND THE PROOF IS THAT IT ATE THE ONE MEASUREMENT I NEEDED.** Trying to verify `SCALEWALK` against the `RHYTHM3S.1` self-profile, the `[CPUProfile] TOP SELF-TIME` table was **already gone** — a 500-line fetch spanned `15:55:03 → 15:55:12`, i.e. **9 seconds**, because the walk logs ~55 lines/second. ⭐ **And the cause is the fix itself:** definitions went from ~5-7s to ~128ms, so log volume rose ~40× and shrank the readable window by the same factor. **The log became unreadable BECAUSE the walk got fast.** ⛔ **The general rule this establishes: a measurement that happens ONCE cannot live in a console line.** A line is something you had to be watching for; this board exists to answer questions *late*. Both offenders are now STATE FIELDS with their dashboard rows in the same commit: **`profiling.cpuProfile`** (the ranked self-time table + `sampledMs` + age; `null` means *not sampled yet*, never *the loop is fine*) and **`profiling.throughput.uplink`** (a small RING, not a single value — ⚠ the rate is not uniform, a 2.79GB matrix averages lower than a 48MB one because it spans many drain cycles, so **the size travels with every entry and cannot be read apart**; the row shows the last upload *and* the largest, because the large one is the honest read of sustained throughput). ⚠ Producer/consumer ownership **verified rather than assumed** — `brain` is the `ServerBrain` singleton, the profiler region already writes `brain._loopWatchdogSlots`, and both state and gpu mixins sit on `ServerBrain.prototype`, so `brain._x` and `this._x` are the same object. Given today found three dead reads from exactly that mistake, checking it here was not optional.

### POST-PRESS — found by reading the live board (2026-08-25, the walk's first minutes)

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — PHISCALE.1, the design half of KI-33, DECIDED BY GEE and shipped.** He was asked the fork with the numbers on the table (`H(0.5%) = 0.045`, `H(1.5%) = 0.112`, `H(3%) = 0.194` against a 0.1 floor) and chose **rescale the entropy to her operating range**, with the caveat built into the option: *a chosen `p_ref` must be justified.* ⛔ **So it is measured, not chosen.** A hardcoded proportion was rejected twice over — nothing in the system justifies a specific number, and it would silently mean different things across boots because `totalNeurons` is derived at boot from free host RAM. `Φ̂ = clamp(H(p)/ref, 0, 1)` where `ref` **rises immediately to any new peak and decays 0.99995/tick**, floored at the seed `H(0.015) = 0.1124` — her **documented** design sparsity, an architectural property. ⛔ Deliberately NOT seeded from the first observation (the brainstem-nucleus lesson — it would return `Φ̂ = 1.0` on tick one). ⚠ **THE HARNESS CAUGHT MY FIRST CUT:** a gentle 5%-of-gap rise made the reference LAG her, so design sparsity read `1.000` *and* 3% firing read `1.000` — **a saturated value is a constant, and a constant is exactly what cancels out of `psiGain`**, i.e. I had reintroduced the original failure at the opposite end of the range. The reference must be her HIGH-WATER integration, never her typical. Verified on the corrected form: 0.5%→3% spans **0.234 → 1.000, spread 0.766**; constant input → constant output (honest); a 30% seizure spike compresses then **recovers to the seed**; `p = 0` still hits the collapse guard. ⭐ `phiRaw` / `phiScaleRef` / `phiNorm` all publish **with a dashboard row in the same commit** — a normaliser living only inside its own function would be a hidden number deciding a headline quantity. **PHISCALE.1 — Φ̂ rescaled to her operating range.**

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-25 — ⛔ Φ̂ WAS NEVER MEASURING HER CORTEX. It was reading the CPU spike shadow, which is EMPTY at biological scale.** `computePhi()` sampled 1024 strided cells of `cluster.lastSpikes` — and this repo's own documented fact is that once the brain is donor-computed *"the GPU owns cortex spike state; the CPU `lastSpikes` stays zero"*, changing only where `_writeTiledPattern` sets teach-pattern bits. A 1024-wide stride across ~82M neurons therefore almost never lands on a set bit. **Measured live post-press: `phiRaw` 0.0289 then 0.0112 — about ONE sampled neuron in 1024 — while the A40 was firing flat out.** So Φ̂ was reporting *teach-pattern residue*, and Ψ has been multiplying by the `max(0.1, ·)` floor for the entire life of the term. ⭐ **The truthful source already existed and is EXACT:** the server writes `cluster.spikeCount` from every `compute_batch` ack, and `cluster.js` never assigns that property — so its presence is an honest discriminator for *"the GPU owns this"* rather than a guess; the CPU path sets `lastSpikeCount`, which is the truth in browser mode where `lastSpikes` really is populated. Using the count is also strictly better than the sample it replaces: the 1024 figure existed to hold the binomial noise floor near 1.5%, and an exact proportion has **no sampling noise at all**. ⚠ **RE-PRICE: none needed, and stated rather than assumed** — Φ̂ feeds Ψ → `gainMultiplier`, clamped `[0.8, 1.5]`; it moves cluster gain, not `corpus × reps × scale × visits`, so no bound keeping the walk finite is touched. ⭐ **Computed the consequence before shipping:** at her documented ~1.5% design sparsity `H(0.015) = 0.1124`, which clears the 0.1 floor — **so Φ̂ should go live for the first time.** Below ~1.3% firing it still floors, and that residue is the DESIGN question (rescale the entropy for sparse coding, lower the floor, or accept a capacity-only Ψ) which stays with Gee under KI-33 — the difference is that it can now be decided on a measurement instead of an artifact. **PHISRC.1 — the Φ̂ term read a dead array.**

- **⚠ EXAMINED AND DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED (2026-08-25) — recorded so nobody "fixes" them later.** ⛔ **NO CHECKBOX: these are verdicts, not tasks.** (1) **`boundPropagate: native 63 / emptyMirror 90`** — 59% of bound propagates served by the CPU, `lastEmptyName: cortex_word_motor_to_sem`, `noMirrorObject: 0`. **This is CORRECT, not a fault:** definition binds route through the `sem↔fineType` whitelist and never write `word_motor`, so that matrix genuinely has no resident pre during the bootstrap and the honest refusal (`null` → CPU grades) is the designed behaviour. The counter shipped hours earlier is simply doing its job — *an expected number is not a bug just because it is large.* (2) **`inner-voice think() took 1395ms / 1793ms`** during teaching. `_innerVoiceTick` self-gates to at most once per ~6s (the Hurlburt rhythm), so the cost is bounded — and gating her thinking OFF during learning to make a loop figure look better is exactly the intentional dumbing-down that is banned. She thinks while she learns; that is the feature. (3) **definitions at 5–7.4s each** against a documented ~3.9s — that is dictionary-API latency plus the teach, not a code defect, and guessing a cooldown is how a previous fix shipped wrong. All three are observations with reasons, not backlog.

Nothing has ever written the brain-level field, so the value was structurally unreachable — **the exact shape of `meanVoltage` and `separability`**, a consumer naming an owner that does not hold the value. (2) ⚠ **It was not a rate** — it returned `.length` of a ring **capped at 256**, wearing the name `PerHour`; even against the right owner it would have reported a count, and would have flat-lined at 256 forever once the ring filled. ⭐ **A second, CORRECT copy of the computation already existed** in `_getConsciousnessState` (the 114.19ek rolling-1hr clamp) — so the board carried two answers to one question and published the broken one under `profiling`. Fixed at the chokepoint: `_defsLearnedPerHour()` is the single owner and **both** consumers call it. ⚠ Lands on the next restart (Update & Savestart picks it up without costing the walk). **DEFRATE.1 — the defs/hr instrument on the profiling block was structurally zero.** Found by reading the board after the press rather than by a symptom — nobody would have noticed a quiet zero.

### PROPBOUND AUDIT — filed BY the audit (found while doing it, not part of it)

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 (shipped WITH its row in the SAME commit, per the BOARDPARITY lesson) — `gpu.js:3576` refused silently: `if (!pre || !pre.length) return null;` sent every bound propagate to the CPU with no counter and no log, so *"how often does this lane fall back?"* — the exact number RHYTHM3S.1 is hunting — was unanswerable. Now counted BY LANE (`native` / `browserEmptyPre` / `emptyMirror` / `noMirrorObject`) with the last refusal NAMED and AGED, surfaced at `state.profiling.throughput.boundPropagate` AND rendered as a `bound propagate` row reading `N on-card · N →CPU (N%)`. ⚠ An empty mirror is NOT automatically a fault — between teach writes the cortex genuinely has no resident pattern and refusing is CORRECT; it becomes a finding only when it stays high DURING a teach era, which is a comparison the board could not make until the number existed. ⛔ `noMirrorObject` is split out and rendered RED separately, because a MISSING mirror is a wiring fault that would otherwise hide behind the empty case, which is ordinary and constant. Parity 6/6, `node --check` clean on both files, divs 481/481. Full entry: FINALIZED §PROPBOUND AUDIT.** **PROPBOUND.2 — the bound-propagate fallback had no instrument.** Found by auditing `PROPBOUND.1` rather than by any symptom.

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — seven fields, THREE rows, because seven rows of raw counters is noise: the pipeline (`flushedOps / enqueued queued · N frames`), the refusals (`capFlushes` + `suppressedStale`), and the wire verbs (`rangesSent` + `maskedSent`) are three different questions. ⭐ The refusal row earns its place: both counters exist because both events used to be INVISIBLE, and both are the system behaving CORRECTLY under pressure — so they render AMBER, not red, and `none` is green because that zero is evidence rather than an absence of reports. `suppressedStale` finally satisfies its own source comment (*"it belongs on screen, not in a rate-limited console line"*). Parity 7/7 verified across both access forms. Full entry: FINALIZED §BOUNDCAP + DARKHEB.** **DARKHEB.1 — ⛔ the `boundHebbian` telemetry block is DARK: seven fields published every broadcast, rendered nowhere.** Found while adding the `bound propagate` row beside it. `state.profiling.throughput.boundHebbian` carries `enqueued` / `flushedFrames` / `flushedOps` / `capFlushes` / `suppressedStale` / `rangesSent` / `maskedSent`, and `html/dashboard.html` contains **zero** references to any of them — and the board renders BY NAME ONLY, with no generic walk over the state object, so zero references is *proof* of non-rendering (the same decidable test `DARKBOARD.1` used). ⭐ **Two of these matter more than the rest:** `capFlushes` counts mid-slab forced flushes **that used to be silent drops**, and `suppressedStale` counts pattern-lane refusals — the counter whose own comment reads *"This counter is the teaching that cost us — it belongs on screen, not in a rate-limited console line."* **It is not on screen.** ⚠ `suppressedStale` IS separately visible under `wsPressure`, so it is half-dark rather than fully; the other six are fully dark. Fix = add rows; no server change needed.

- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — ⛔ AND IT WAS NOT A LATENT FRAGILITY, IT WAS TWO LIVE BUGS. I filed this as "correct today, fragile by construction"; that was WRONG. `gpu_register` stamps `client.donorAppVersion = _donorVer || 'browser'` — the STRING `'browser'`, which is truthy — so `if (client.donorAppVersion)` was true for EVERY donor and both browser branches were DEAD CODE. (1) The bound-propagate router served browser donors the NATIVE protocol: `_boundPreIndicesFor` returns INDICES while `compute.html`'s type=2 handler reads the payload as a DENSE 0/1 array, and a non-empty `preLen` also defeats the browser's own bound-mode trigger (`preLen === 0`, compute.html:724) on matrices whose standalone buffers aren't allocated when cluster-bound — i.e. the PROPBOUND fix had become a MIRROR IMAGE of the bug it fixed, pointed at the other donor type. (2) ⛔ Found by applying fix-the-chokepoint: the SAME broken test at `gpu.js:2761` handed browser donors the **96MB** UPLINK.1 pump window, removing exactly the 8MB protection its own comment three lines above promised them — and a browser tab cannot drain its own socket, which is why that protection exists. FIXED: one owner for nativeness (`_donorIsNative()`, testing the sentinel not the presence), an ADVERTISED `boundResidentRead` capability from `compute.html` for the protocol choice, and ⚠ the unknown-donor case deliberately routed to the REBUILD path (which refuses with `null`) rather than the empty-pre path (which invents all-zero currents) — the asymmetry is the safety property and is commented as such so it is not tidied away. ⭐ Every OTHER capability gate was unaffected for a reason worth keeping: they regex-parse a semver and `'browser'` fails `^\d+\.\d+\.\d+`, so version-gated checks got browser-exclusion FREE while the two boolean-presence checks did not. Full entry: FINALIZED §BOUNDCAP + DARKHEB.** **BOUNDCAP.1 — the bound-propagate protocol is chosen by an IDENTITY field, not an advertised capability.** `gpuSparsePropagateBound` picks between the native-rebuild path and the browser empty-pre path with `if (_c && _c.donorAppVersion)` — i.e. *"does this donor report a version string"* standing in for *"does this donor lack the empty-pre resident read"*. It is correct today, and it is a **name-based inference about a wire capability**, which is the class of thing this repo keeps getting burned by. ⛔ The codebase already has the right idiom in three places — `_sparseV2`, `_mindspaceV1`, `mindspaceOps` — a flag the donor ADVERTISES at `gpu_register`. If a browser donor ever reports a version, or a native donor's version field goes missing, routing silently flips to the wrong protocol and the failure mode is **all-zero currents accepted as real signal** — which is precisely what `PROPBOUND.1` was. ⚠ Low urgency, real fragility: nothing is broken now, and the fix is one advertised boolean.

### STOPTRAP — the box went down on a button that promised it wouldn't (2026-08-25)

> Gee (verbatim): *"i pressed the shut down button on zccident and now i cant start it i need that machine gun command to savestart the brain sim"*

> Gee (verbatim): *"i cant do anything from her do the writer for sponge to save start it up again and fix the shutdown but to auto start upo as a savestart(non-upgrade version)"*

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — the write-up for Sponge, at the TOP of `deploy/REDEPLOY-NOTES.md` where he will actually hit it.** One command — ⚠ **`sudo systemctl start unity-brain`, `start` NOT `restart`**, because the process already exited and there is nothing to restart — pre-authorised NOPASSWD by `bootstrap-backend.sh`, so no root and no config change. Three confirmation reads (`systemctl status`, `journalctl -n 40`, a `curl` for 200), ⚠ with the warning that boot loads **~5.4 GB of weights before it binds the port**, so an early 502 is normal and the correct response is to keep polling rather than conclude failure. ⭐ **Written in large letters: a plain `start` RESUMES the walk** (`DREAM_KEEP_STATE=1` + the resume marker `/shutdown` wrote on its way out) — **Update & Fresh Walk would throw the training away**, and that is the wrong button to reach for in a panic. Plus why it did not self-heal (exit 42 is deliberate, not a systemd misconfiguration), and the unit refresh flagged as explicitly optional and NOT needed for recovery.

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — ⛔ THE BUTTON'S CRIME WAS BEING REACHABLE, NOT WHAT IT DID.** `/shutdown` exits **42** and the unit's `RestartPreventExitStatus=42` makes that final on purpose — correct for a stop button. The defect is that it rendered on a dashboard whose operator **has no shell**, since box changes are dashboard-only by standing rule: **the one control that cannot be undone from the dashboard was sitting on the dashboard**, one click from the buttons used routinely, wearing the same styling. And two documents actively taught that it was safe — the `title` attribute claimed *"On the deployed box systemd auto-resumes"*, and `.claude/DEPLOYED-ADMIN-GUIDE.md` said *"systemd brings it back"* while listing it under **"Restart (keeps walk)"** — both contradicted by `docs/ADMIN-CONTROLS.md`'s exit-code table **in the same repo**, and by that file's own **verified-on-the-box 2026-06-22** read (`POST /shutdown` → `active=inactive`). ⭐ **That same file had already written the trap down, inside the fix that created it:** *"it can't restart itself — the process is gone, so there's no dashboard to click."* The sentence names the failure exactly; nobody drew the conclusion. **Fixed by reachability:** `wireGracefulStop()` tests `location.hostname` and **`.remove()`s the element** — removed, not hidden, so no stray handler or devtools unhide reaches it — except on `localhost` / `127.0.0.1` / `::1`, where a halt is undone with `Savestart.bat`. ⭐ **The box loses nothing: `🔄 Restart (Savestart)` was already sitting beside it** — `/restart` → force-save, resume marker, `exit(0)`, revived by `Restart=always`, walk resumed. **A savestart, non-upgrade, exactly as asked.** ⚠ **Repointing Stop at `/restart` was considered and REJECTED** — it is the literal reading, and it ships **two differently-labelled buttons doing the same thing**, which is the same class of defect as the tooltip that caused this. ⚠ **Stated plainly to Gee rather than buried: that is a deviation from his literal words, and repointing is a one-line change if he wants the literal form.** **STOPTRAP.1 — a one-way door, one click away, on the one machine with no way back through it.**

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — `deploy/unity-brain.service` was carrying a contract it did not contain, plus a second latent way to strand the box.** (1) `server/brain-server.js` has cited **`RestartPreventExitStatus=42`** by name since the `/shutdown` handler was written, and **the unit file never had the directive**. The box's installed copy evidently does — the 2026-06-22 verification proves exit 42 stayed down — so **the repo was the drift**, and any freshly-provisioned box would have auto-revived on Stop while the code's comments swore it would not. (2) ⛔ **`StartLimitIntervalSec=0`** added in `[Unit]`: systemd's default limiter gives up after **5 starts in 10s** and leaves the unit **permanently dead**. A boot-time crash loop burns that in under a minute and then never retries — **stranding a shell-less box exactly like today, with no dashboard left to press and no self-healing when a fixed overlay lands.** ⚠ Both on their own lines with comments on separate lines, per the standing systemd rule — a trailing comment is silently ignored and would have made this fix a no-op. ⚠ Flagged to Sponge as optional and NOT required for recovery, with a request to report what the installed unit held before he overwrites it.

### EYEPIN — she draws the same thing forever and never looks anything up (2026-08-25)

Root cause traced: `chat.js:1312` sets `_seedText = texts[texts.length - 1]`, the tail of `_innerThoughtChain`, and that chain has stopped advancing (`emitDiagnostic.reason "no-best-word"`, `bestMean 0`, `sampleCount 0`, **age 1.07s** — actively failing every tick, not idle). ⭐ **Every piece of anti-repetition machinery is DOWNSTREAM of the frozen seed:** the 70/30 recombination rotates an older thought into the FIELD and never touches `_seedText`, so styles rotate beautifully around a subject that cannot move.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-25 — EYEPIN.2: actual do lookups.** `lookups.attempts: 1` for the whole boot, `coolSkips: 5`, `alreadyKnown: 368`. ⭐ Same root cause: `_lookUpAndDraw(_seedText)` is handed the SAME word every tick, so it hits its own 6h per-concept cooldown and skips. **She acquires no new visual concepts because she only ever asks about one.**

Ranks: **ACQUISITION** (taught but never seen) → **THOUGHT** (while it is moving) → **RECALL** (grounded, not drawn recently), with a recent-subject ring checked at EVERY rank because ranks alone would not fix it — a pinned thought would still win rank 1 forever. ⛔ **THE HARNESS CAUGHT MY OWN COMMENT LYING:** acquisition began BELOW the thought rank, which returns early, so across 12 ticks of a *healthy* thought it fired **zero** times while the doc-comment three lines above promised acquisition "even when her thought is healthy". **Ordering silently made the guarantee unreachable** — the same producer/consumer-style mismatch as ENDODARK, in prose instead of a path. Rank order is now load-bearing and the harness case `A healthy thought` exists to hold that line. ⚠ **A ROTATION POLICY, NOT A FALLBACK** — one chooser, always consulted, every rank at full capability; nothing degrades. ⚠ **NOT a word list** — the acquisition pool is `cluster._definitionTaughtWords` (membership earned by being taught) and drawability is the live taxonomy gate, with a persistent cursor so she works THROUGH her vocabulary instead of re-rolling lucky words. **Verified 14/14 across four cases on the PRODUCTION mixin** (`Object.assign({}, SERVER_CHAT_MIXIN)`, not a copy of the logic): healthy thought → thought dominates 9/12 **and** still acquires; pinned thought → 14 ticks give **8 distinct subjects, 0 consecutive repeats, 10 real acquisitions**, `maxPin 13` reported honestly; empty thought → tracked as a pin (⚠ the first cut used `_word &&`, and `''` is falsy, so "thinking nothing repeatedly" reset the counter and read as healthy variety — now a sentinel); only-one-drawable-concept → the undrawable word is never drawn and the lane falls to the imagine field without crashing. Dashboard row + `state.ownArt.eye` ship in the SAME commit, **12/12 field parity**, divs 483/483. ⚠ **Server-side (`chat.js` + `state.js`) — needs a restart to land; the dashboard row is frontend and appears on refresh.**

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — ⛔ NULSENT.1: I SHIPPED A RAW NUL BYTE INTO SOURCE AND `grep` WENT QUIET ABOUT IT.** EYEPIN's empty-thought sentinel landed as `'\0empty'` — one `0x00` at offset 105,925 (line 1785) of `chat.js`, committed, merged, and **cascaded to `main` on both remotes**. ⚠ **It was not functionally broken, which is precisely why it would have survived:** a NUL-prefixed string collides with no real word, so the pin detector worked, `node --check` passed, the ESM import passed, and **all 14 harness checks passed straight over it** — nothing that gates a commit inspects a string literal for control characters. ⛔ **The damage is to the toolchain and it is the silent kind:** `grep` reclassified a 320KB JS file as **binary** and returned *no matches and no error*, so every later search of it reads as a clean negative. **This session alone closed four producer/consumer defects found by grepping for a field name** — a search tool that quietly stops answering attacks the exact method being used to find bugs. ⭐ **Found by accident, not by audit:** an unrelated `grep -rn "injectText("` printed `Binary file server/brain-server/chat.js matches` while chasing a different question. Had that line not appeared the file would have stayed unsearchable indefinitely. Fixed to a readable, greppable `'__eye_no_thought__'`, with the rule written next to it — **no control characters in sentinels.** Verified: **repo-wide NUL sweep = 0 files**, `grep -c` on the file returns 3 (text again), `node --check` clean, **all 14 EYEPIN harness checks re-PASS** including the `empty tracked as pin` case the sentinel exists for. **NULSENT.1 — a working string literal that made a file unsearchable.**
