### LOOKBACKOFF — the mind's eye WAS hogging the anonymous lane, and I caused it (2026-08-26)

> Gee (verbatim): *"is the mind's eye hogging the pollinations anynomous generation lane???: You show me an apple / Unity (image generation failed) / You show me a dog / Unity (image generation failed)"*

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — ⛔ YES, AND IT WAS A DIRECT CONSEQUENCE OF MY OWN EYEPIN CHANGE.** Live 15 minutes after EYEPIN shipped: **`attempts: 130`, `httpFails: 108`, `lastErr: "HTTP 429"`, `grounded: 15`** — **83% refused** — with `eye.fromAcquisition: 107 of 142 picks`. ⛔ **The bug I fixed had been acting as an accidental rate limiter:** the old picker handed the SAME word to `_lookUpAndDraw` every tick, so the per-concept 6h cooldown absorbed everything and the lane made **1 attempt per boot**. EYEPIN.2's acquisition rank picks a **different unseen word** on ~75% of ticks, and a per-concept cooldown **by construction cannot throttle a walk through fresh vocabulary**. The global gap was already 0 (NOGAP, revoked on *"its the anonymous free"* — correct then, because looks were rare). ⛔ **Worse, it was POSITIVE FEEDBACK:** `_vmLookFail` rolls the burns back so a failed concept retries in 10min instead of 6h — **every 429 scheduled another retry.** ⭐ **And the cost landed on Gee, not on her:** chat image generation is built BROWSER-side from the same public IP, so a background errand was spending the shared anonymous quota and *"show me an apple"* answered *"(image generation failed)"*. **A background errand must not outbid the person in the room.** **Fix: honour the 429** — exponential backoff from 15s, doubling per consecutive refusal, capped 10min, **`Retry-After` wins when sent** (only the server knows when it will answer), reset to zero on the first success. ⚠ **Explicitly NOT a re-arming of the budget Gee revoked:** that was a self-imposed *spend* limit from the keyed era; this is the generator explicitly answering **stop**, and ignoring an explicit refusal wins nothing because a refused request returns no image either way. ⚠ Reset-on-success is load-bearing — **without it the backoff RATCHETS** and the lane stops looking forever while every counter still reads healthy. Harness: escalation `15s→30s→60s→120s→240s→480s→600s→600s` capped, `Retry-After 120s` beats the 15s base, reset clears.

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — ⛔ SEEDPHRASE.1: a whole generated IMAGE PROMPT was being used as a concept.** Caught in `eye.recentSubjects` on the live box, sitting among `may` / `digits` / `change` / `walk`: **`'an apple, another, just, smartphone, vibrant saturated color, crisp sharp focus, bold dramatic contrast, elect…'`**. Image-lane emissions land in `_innerThoughtChain` like any other, so `_seedText` is sometimes a prompt or a sentence rather than a word. ⛔ **Not merely an ugly label — that string was handed to `_lookUpAndDraw`**, i.e. spent as a Pollinations request on a query that could never ground, **during the exact window the quota was being exhausted**. ⚠ **Structural test, NOT a word list:** a concept key in this system is a single word — every store key, every definition lookup, every taxonomy judgement is one word — so whitespace or sentence punctuation means the seed is a phrase, and a phrase is not a thing she can look at. Acquisition and recall still supply a subject, so a phrase-shaped thought costs her nothing but that one rank. **Harness 11/11** (`apple`/`walk`/`digits`/`back-pack` pass; the prompt, `backpack juice gravity!`, `hello world`, `end.`, 41 chars, empty all refused).

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — the look lane had NO DASHBOARD ROW AT ALL, which is how it ran unseen.** `state.ownArt.lookups` publishes the whole counter block, and `html/dashboard.html` contained **zero** references to it — so 108 rate-limited fetches produced nothing on screen while the operator watched his own image requests fail. Row added in the SAME commit as the counters: grounded/attempts, http-fail %, rate-limited count, skipped count, and **`⚠ BACKING OFF Ns (chat keeps the quota)`** while the backoff is armed. ⭐ **AMBER while backing off — that is the system behaving CORRECTLY under refusal, not a fault; RED is reserved for ≥50% failing while NOT backing off**, which is the unhandled case. ⛔ `null` renders as *"no attempts yet — NOT a verdict on the generator"*, never as a reassuring zero. **7/7 producer/consumer parity, divs 484/484, bundle rebuilt.**

### LOOKQUEUE — one look in the pipe, and the human outranks the errand (2026-08-26)

> Gee (verbatim): *"sooo... only beiong able to have one image gen in the pipe with ananymous teir seems to suck up all the image gen with the minds eye. so i dont knwo what to do... what do you suggest? can we put them in a que or something and not allow the minds eye to have more than one in the que so that it doesnt error out image gen in chat and will eventually generate the image when it gets done with the image that the mind;s eye is doing?"*

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — ⛔ LOOKQUEUE.1: THE LANE WAS NOT MERELY FAST, IT WAS PARALLEL — and Gee diagnosed it before I did.** The in-flight guard is keyed by **CONCEPT** (`_vmRefInFlight.has(key)`), so it only ever stopped the *same word twice*; **different words were never guarded at all**. `_lookUpAndDraw` is launched **fire-and-forget** from the imagine tick (~8s), a look takes **2-60s**, and since EYEPIN.2 every launch carries a **different unseen word** — so up to **~7 concurrent fetches** ran against a tier that serves about one. ⭐ **That is exactly the "one image gen in the pipe" Gee described, and it is why *"image gen worked yesterday"*: yesterday the lane made ONE attempt per boot.** Fixed with a brain-wide single-flight slot. ⚠ **Concurrency 1, NOT a time budget** — this is not the global gap Gee revoked (*"its the anonymous free"*); nothing says "look less often", it says **"finish the one you started before beginning another"**. Over an hour she looks just as many times, and each look now actually completes. ⛔ **My FIRST DRAFT LEAKED THE SLOT and the harness-by-reading caught it:** the claim sat at the gate, ABOVE two `return null` paths (the global gap and my own 429 backoff) that never reach the `try`/`finally` that releases it — **and a leaked slot does not slow the lane, it CLOSES it permanently**, guaranteed to trigger on the first rate limit. Claim and release now share one lifetime beside the per-concept guard. **Audited: 0 unguarded returns between claim and `try`; 13 returns inside, all covered by the `finally`.**

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — LOOKQUEUE.2: the person in the room outranks the background errand.** `_imageLanePriorityUntil` is stamped server-side at the exact instant the chat path returns `action: 'generate_image'`, and the look lane stands down for the window (`DREAM_CHAT_IMAGE_PRIORITY_MS`, default 45s). ⭐ **Stamping it there is what makes this possible at all:** the client builds the image URL itself (PROMPT ONLY, by design) and fetches from the **same public IP**, so the browser's request is invisible to this process — but the *intent* is not. ⚠ **A TIME window rather than a lock, deliberately:** there is nothing to release when the fetch happens in another process, and **a lock with no releaser is a lane that never reopens**.

- [x] **✅ VERIFIED 2026-08-26 — full gate chain harnessed, 4/4.** Concurrency-1: five ticks 8s apart with a 30s look → **1 fetch, 4 queued**. Chat priority: yields for the whole 45s window (3 ticks) then **resumes on its own**. `force` (the ✗ reject-relearn path) **still bypasses every gate** — an operator verdict must never be throttled. No slot leak across 200 claim/release cycles. **9/9 producer/consumer parity on the extended row, divs 484/484**, `node --check` clean on both server files. ⚠ `dashboard.html` is standalone — **no bundle rebuild needed** for this batch.

### VMPHRASE — one-word concepts will never be real, and the label throws the sentence away (2026-08-26)

> Gee (verbatim): *"Seems like this is dumbing her down as big red apple table is totally differetn that the original "a big red apple on a table" the visual memory should be shortening the memory should it becasue the original is turn not the content words only: seems like that could poison her brain at times not getting or not using the fulkll information"*

> Gee (verbatim): *"im seeing this in her brains mind eye too "she is only using one word concepts" so thatll never be real! also make all these fixes i wave been speaking of"*

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — VMPHRASE.1: MY OWN SEEDPHRASE FIX MADE SUBJECTS SINGLE-WORD-ONLY, and Gee is right that it can never be real.** I added `_isSingleWord` earlier today to stop a generated PROMPT string (`'an apple, another, just, smartphone, vibrant saturated color, crisp sharp focus…'`) being spent as a Pollinations request. The guard works, but it is far too blunt: **a real subject is often a phrase** — *red apple*, *old wooden church*, *cat on a chair*. ⛔ **The test must separate a NOUN PHRASE from a GENERATOR PROMPT, not "one word from everything else."** A prompt is comma-separated, style-laden and long; a subject is a short natural phrase with no commas.

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — VMPHRASE.2: the visual store files a memory under the FIRST content word, which is usually an ADJECTIVE.** Proven by running it: `_vmContentWords("a big red apple on the table")[0]` → **`"big"`**, `"the old wooden church"` → **`"old"`**. Two sites take `[0]` as the sole key — `_learnShapeSchema` (her learned SHAPE) and `_fetchReferenceAndGround` (the looked-up reference). ⛔ **So her shape-memory of an apple is filed under *big*, colliding with every big thing she ever sees, and asking for *apple* never finds it.** English noun phrases are head-final, so `[0]` is close to the worst available choice. ⚠ Narrower than it looks — the eye lane passes single words and imagination combos pass an explicit `keyOverride` — but it bites every multi-word chat ask.

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — VMPHRASE.3: the RELATION is dropped entirely — Gee's actual point.** *"on the table"* is captured nowhere. `apple` and `table` are filed as independent keys with **no link between them**, so she cannot learn *"the apple was ON the table"* from the label. ⭐ The stopword strip has a sound stated reason (*"binding a field C to 'the'/'of' would make every future thought recall random imagery through stopword collisions"*) and that reasoning is correct **for keys** — but nothing else preserves the sentence, so the relation is simply lost. ⚠ **NOT poisoning — omission.** The field C itself is full fidelity (~41k equation terms, 3 colour channels); it is the LABEL that throws the sentence away. Fix = keep the full original phrase on the entry alongside the keys, so nothing is discarded even though keys stay stopword-free.

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — VMPHRASE.4: file under the HEAD NOUN plus aliases, not one word.** Head selection must come from the live WordNet taxonomy (`server/drawable-taxonomy.js` — `primaryLex` / `unattestedNoun` / `knownOnlyNonNoun`), ⚠ **never a word list** — that is the standing law and the taxonomy is already the tool used by every other draw-lane gate.

- [x] **✅ VERIFIED 2026-08-26 — VMPHRASE, all four, tested by EXECUTION on the production mixin.** **Head-word picker 8/8** — `"a big red apple on the table"` → **apple** (was `big`), `"the old wooden church"` → **church** (was `old`), `"a cat sitting on a chair"` → **cat** (`sitting` reads abstract to the taxonomy), plus `red apple`→apple, `a dog running in the park`→dog, `the quick brown fox`→fox. ⭐ It walks the ORIGINAL text rather than the stripped list, because the phrase boundary lives in the glue: once a concrete noun is seen the next glue word closes the phrase, which is what stops `apple on the table` drifting onto `table`. ### LOOPMAX — the loop-delay max can only ever go up, and it reads as a live stall (2026-08-26)

> Gee (verbatim): *"is 52% serviced a bad number or a normal number?"* → *"it got better"* → *"yeah lets fix that"*

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — LOOPMAX.1: `loop delay max` is cumulative since boot and never resets.** Caught because it read **`18,505.3 ms` IDENTICALLY across two readings** while `serviced` went 52% → 91%, lag 320ms → 21ms and `late/min` 29,048 → 5,561. ⛔ **Everything else moved and it did not** — that is the tell. ⚠ It is almost certainly the BOOT pinning the loop while it loads ~5.4GB of weights, so it will sit there looking like a live 18-second stall for the rest of the run, on a brain that is currently at **26ms lag**. ⛔ **And it flatly contradicts the row beside it:** the freeze watchdog's threshold is 5,000ms, so an 18.5s stall is 3.7× the bar, yet `loop freezes` reads `none`. Two fields on the same card cannot both be right. **Same lying-instrument class as everything else fixed today.**

- [x] **✅ VERIFIED 2026-08-26 — LOOPMAX.1: the delay histogram now ROLLS, and the worst-ever stall is kept but RENAMED.** `monitorEventLoopDelay()` is cumulative by construction — its own comment said *"Cumulative since boot"* — so `max` could only ever rise. It is now `reset()` after each read, making mean / p50 / p99 / max describe the window since the previous broadcast: **a number that can fall as well as rise.** ⭐ **The all-time peak is NOT discarded** — losing the worst stall the process ever saw would be its own dishonesty — it is banked as `sinceBootMaxMs` and rendered as `boot-peak`, so it stops wearing the name of a live reading. The row also prints the window length (`last Ns`). ⚠ **The reset is `typeof`-guarded:** a Node build without `reset()` keeps the old cumulative behaviour rather than throwing inside the state build — and then `windowMs` grows without bound, which is the honest signal that it did **not** roll. **Proven by execution: a 400ms stall then a quiet window reads `400.6 ms → 16.0 ms`.** ⚠ **Owned: my FIRST test reported FAIL and the test was wrong, not the fix** — it ran the busy-loop before the histogram had begun sampling, so it measured 31.6ms in both windows. Re-run with the stall inside a timer, it passes cleanly. **A failing check is not automatically a failing fix.** Parity 5/5 on the row (`p` in the scan was a regex artifact — `el.p99Ms`, where `9` is not a letter); divs 484/484.

- [x] **✅ VERIFIED 2026-08-26 — SHADOWKILL: the oval is gone and nothing else casts one.** It was one `blob` at **rgb(16,15,19), alpha 0.35** beneath every subject — exactly the "dark tinted oval" Gee described, because that is literally what it was. **One call site, now removed**, and `_groundShadow` **deleted rather than left unreferenced**: ⚠ a dead painter primitive is an invitation to switch it back on, and the reason it went is that the SHAPE was wrong, not its parameters. ⭐ **Grounding is not lost** — it still comes from the ground line, the tufts and the floor/wall bands, which are DRAWN rather than tinted. **Swept for any other dark tint under a subject:** the remaining `blob` uses are the per-part colour masses (`p.rgb`, the part's own sampled colour, gated inside the silhouette), petals and ears; the other `0.35` alphas are the sky/ground gradient bands in the backdrop. **Nothing else shades beneath her subjects.** ⚠ The `strokes.length < 4` honest-no-drawing guard now sees fewer strokes per piece, which is strictly correct — a piece that only cleared the bar because of shadow blobs was never a drawing.

Everything shipped was server-side JS, dashboards and docs. ⭐ **The only thing the donor sees differently is the relation tag span shrinking 84,000 → 10,500 contiguous cells** — one `[start,len]` range in range-form, against a donor that has been handling 84,000-cell spans all along, so the frames get **smaller**, not bigger. **donor-v0.3.30 stands.**

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — PAIRDESYNC.2: the boot now says whether the pair is coherent.** `_checkWeightsPairCoherence()` compares the json's own `savedAt` against the bin's mtime — ⭐ **evidence that was already on disk and free to read**. ⚠ **It WARNS, it does not REFUSE:** refusing to boot on a stale pair would hand a shell-less operator an unbootable brain over a usually-survivable condition — the same one-way-door mistake as the Stop button. The warn names the gap, both timestamps, the likely cause (a save interrupted between the two writes — check for a leftover `.bin.tmp`) and the fix (copy the newest coherent checkpoint pair). **Validated against reality, not invented thresholds:** her live pair reads **30s → COHERENT**; this morning's real desync was **2311s → CAUGHT**; the three known-good checkpoint pairs (43s / 103s / 268s) → **no false alarms**. Tolerance 600s, `DREAM_WEIGHTS_PAIR_TOL_SEC`.

- [x] **✅ FIXED 2026-08-26 — VMUSE.5d: percept grounding is now DEFERRED, not dropped.** Gee: *"vmuse.5d ... should of been done already becase we fucking did a frewsh walk"*. ⛔ The skip fired on `_curriculumInProgress`, **true for the entire multi-week walk**, so what she saw reached her sem region essentially never — across exactly the stretch where she sees the most. ⚠ **The reason for the skip was real and is unchanged:** injecting while a teach pattern is IN FLIGHT corrupts it. ⭐ **But "in flight" is the operative word** — the drain runs in the gap BETWEEN teach calls, where nothing is mid-pattern, so the drain now carries a **second job KIND** (`kind: 'inject'`) and the look queues its grounding there instead of discarding it. Idle it still injects immediately; there is nothing to defer around. ⚠ Bounded like every other producer — cap 16, **dropped and COUNTED when full** (a stale percept is worth less than a fresh one, and this queue must never become the thing that pins the walk). **Verified: inject job dispatches (region/len/strength correct), pair job still dispatches unchanged, empty vector ignored — the two kinds do not shadow each other.**

### VMUSE.5 CONSUME — she reads the relation back, in all four lanes (2026-08-26)

> Gee (verbatim, asked where to consume it): *"all of the above"*

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-26 — VMUSE.5.A: ONE confidence gate, not four.** ⛔ Every consumer must go through a single `_confidentRelationFor(word)` that returns a band **only when the channels have actually separated for that word**, and `null` otherwise. ⚠ **The bands started writing THIS walk**, so most words will read `flat` for a while — four consumers each deciding for themselves when a readout is trustworthy is four chances to feed noise into her, and the reason to centralise it is that the `flat` handling then exists in exactly one place to be got right.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-26 — VMUSE.5.B: INSTRUMENT — publish what she is reading before anything depends on it.** State + dashboard row: the last words consulted, their band, margin, and how often the answer was refused as flat. ⭐ This is what proves the bands separate; without it the other three lanes are acting on an unmeasured signal.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-26 — VMUSE.5.C: DRAWING consumes it.** ⭐ Lowest risk of the three real consumers — a wrong relation makes an odd picture, not a wrong sentence, and it is judged by eye immediately. It also closes the loop with ARTWEIGHT and VMRELATE, which feed those exact channels.
- [ ] **⛔ NOT BLOCKED — PLANNED, AND WAITING ON THE WALK (see the plan below) — VMUSE.5.D: SPEECH + CHAT consume it.** ⚠ My "blocked" verdict here was RETRACTED by the investigation below: `fineType_to_sem` already exists, so no new projection is needed and the real gate is that the bands must separate first. ⚠ Highest impact and highest risk: it changes what she SAYS. Bounded as a small bucket-mean boost on the same shape as the existing GlobalWorkspace boost — **never a hard override**, so the trained argmax still decides and the relation only tilts it.

- [x] **✅ VERIFIED 2026-08-26 — VMUSE.5 A/B/C shipped; D STOPPED AND REVERTED because my design was wrong.** ⭐ **A — the ONE gate, 6/6:** `_confidentRelationFor` returns a band only past TWO bars — `flat === false` AND a margin ≥15% of the winner's own size — so a hairline lead is refused as firmly as a flat readout; unreadable refused; empty word safe; and it CACHES per word for 30s so four consumers cost one propagate (`cached=2` measured), bounded at 512 entries. ⭐ **B — the instrument, before anything leans on it:** `state.ownArt.relationUse` + a dashboard row showing confident/asked, not-separated-yet, unreadable, top tags and the last word read. ⚠ AMBER until something is confident, because **early in a walk refusing IS the right answer**; `null` renders as *"reader not consulted yet"*, never as "no relations found". ⭐ **C — drawing consults it:** each planned subject's dominant relation is annotated onto the plan, closing the loop with VMRELATE (what she looked at) and ARTWEIGHT (what she drew). ⚠ **It only ANNOTATES** — no subject is added, removed or reordered on a relation, because a wrong band must never change what she draws.

- [ ] **⛔ VMUSE.5.D — SPEECH: THE PLAN (investigated 2026-08-26; my first blocker claim was WRONG).** I wrote a bucket-mean boost on the GlobalWorkspace pattern and then found **my own design was wrong twice over.** ⛔ **(1)** It boosted `relBoostWord = the PREVIOUS word` — which is a repetition loop, the exact stammer the repetition penalty three lines below it exists to fight. Boosting *related* words instead needs a "words related to X on channel N" lookup that does not exist in that lane. ⛔ **(2) More fundamental:** `emitWordDirect` propagates `sem_to_word_motor`, which reads the **sem region only** — and the relation tag lives in **fineType**, which is **not an input to that matrix**. So no tag set at emission time can reach the argmax at all. ⭐ **A relation-conditioned emission needs a projection that takes fineType as input** (`fineType_to_sem` exists and is whitelisted, so the pieces are there — the wiring is not). ⚠ **Reverted to zero traces rather than left in as an inert or wrong boost**: an emission bias that cannot fire is exactly the "built but never consumed" class this whole batch exists to close, and one that fires wrongly is worse. **Filed with the mechanism named so the next attempt starts from the projection, not from the boost.**

#### VMUSE.5.D — THE ACCURATE PLAN (investigated 2026-08-26)

⛔ **CORRECTION TO MY OWN BLOCKER.** I wrote that this *"needs a projection that takes fineType as input"* and that **was wrong**. `js/brain/cluster.js` builds cross-projections from a PAIRS list containing `['sem','fineType']`, and pairs create **both directions** (the boot assertion expects *"14/14 (7 pairs × 2 directions)"*). ⭐ **`fineType_to_sem` already exists as a real trained matrix, and the association teach whitelists it** — so the relation tag has been bound bidirectionally with meaning all along. **No new projection is required. The mechanism is a two-step propagate through matrices that already exist.**

**THE MECHANISM — equational end to end, no lookup, no template:**
1. Build a fineType-shaped vector with ONE tag band lit — the same span `_writeRelationTag` writes (`band = fineSize / RELATION_TAG_BANDS`, 10,500 cells at her real 504,000).
2. `fineType_to_sem.propagate(tagVec)` → **a sem pattern that IS "what this relation means"**, read straight out of trained weights.
3. Add that pattern, SCALED SMALL, into the sem query vector `emitWordDirect` already builds.
4. `sem_to_word_motor` argmax runs unchanged — now relation-conditioned. ⭐ The trained argmax still decides; the relation only tilts it.

⛔ **THE COST, MEASURED, AND IT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN CONSTRAINT: one `fineType_to_sem` propagate is 51 ms** at the real shape (1,500,000 × 504,000, ~15M nnz). ⚠ **Naively wiring that per emitted word is a 51ms stall per word** — precisely the class of defect LOOPCHAT.1 was filed for this morning, and it would be worse than the thing it is trying to add.

⭐ **THE FIX IS THAT THE INPUT IS CONSTANT.** A tag vector is the same every time — 10,500 contiguous cells at a fixed value — so `fineType_to_sem.propagate(tagVec)` returns the **same sem pattern** for a given tag on given weights. **Cache it: 48 tags × one propagate = 48 warmups (~2.4s total), then free array reads forever.** ⚠ Warm them with `propagateChunked` (it exists on this matrix — verified) so the 48 warmups never pin the loop, and warm LAZILY on first use of each tag so unused channels cost nothing.

**THE WIRING, concretely:**
- **`js/brain/cluster/emit.js`, `emitWordDirect`** — after `preSem` is built (it already is, via `_buildSemPreVector`) and BEFORE `proj.propagate(preSem)`. That is the only place the sem query exists and is still mutable.
- Source of the tag: `curriculum._confidentRelationFor(activeConcept)` — **the ONE gate**, so a band that has not separated returns null and the whole path stays identity.
- Blend: `preSem[i] += relSem[i] * REL_MIX` with `REL_MIX` small (start ~0.10, env-tunable) — ⚠ **additive tilt, never replacement**, and never applied to a recently-emitted word (the same `_isRecentContent` guard the GW boost uses, or it becomes a stammer).
- Cache: `cluster._relSemCache = Map<tag, Float64Array>`, invalidated on weight load/save so it cannot outlive the weights it was derived from.

⚠ **PREREQUISITE, and it is a real one: the fresh walk must populate the bands first.** Tags ≥6 wrote nothing until today's VMUSE fix, so `fineType_to_sem` holds no trained mass for them yet. **Until `state.ownArt.relationUse.confident` starts climbing, this path would tilt toward noise** — which is exactly why B (the instrument) shipped first. ⭐ **Do not build D until the instrument shows the bands separating.**

⭐ **EVERY CLAIM ABOVE VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, not asserted** (real `SparseMatrix` at the true 1,500,000 × 504,000 shape, ~15M nnz): **(1)** the same tag returns a **bit-identical** sem pattern, so the cache is valid rather than merely plausible; **(2)** different tags return **different** patterns — 235,140 cells differ between tag 13 and tag 23 — so the channel genuinely carries relation information and this is not a no-op dressed as a feature; **(3)** `propagateChunked` is **bit-identical (maxDiff = 0)**, so the warmups can chunk without changing a number; **(4)** the warmup budget is **2.4s for all 48** at 50.4 ms each, one time, lazily. ⚠ **The 51 ms figure is the reason the design is a cache and not a call** — quoted here so nobody re-derives the naive version.

- **CTLPLANE.1 SHIPPED + INSTALLED ON THE BOX 2026-08-26 (Sponge; Gee's ask: "the web server should remain up, but if the brain is down it should say brain offline… we need to allow start, stop, restart… so gee or other admins do not need to wait for me"):** ROOT FLAW REMOVED — every power control lived INSIDE `brain-server.js` (`/shutdown`/`/restart`/`/savererun`/`/update`), i.e. a control plane hosted by the thing it controls, so **a stopped brain could not serve its own start button**; and the box's sudoers granted `unity` only `systemctl restart unity-brain`, **never `start`**, so even the brain's tooling could not recover a deliberate exit-42 halt. NEW: `server/brain-ctl.js` + `deploy/unity-brain-ctl.service` — a separate ALWAYS-UP service (zero deps, node builtins only, never imports brain code, `Restart=always`, `StartLimitIntervalSec=0`, `MemoryMax=256M`, running at 12.8 MB; deliberately NOT `Requires=`/`PartOf=` the brain since it must live precisely when the brain does not) exposing `/ctl/status|start|stop|restart|kick|logs`. Reports SIX honest phases incl. the distinctions the old UI could not make: **`booting` vs `online`** (the brain binds its port only AFTER loading ~5.4 GB of weights, so "systemd active" ≠ "serving" — conflating them is why a healthy boot looked like a failure), **`halted`** (exit 42 = deliberate, gated on `activeState==='inactive'` because `ExecMainStatus` lingers), **`unmanaged`** (port open + unit inactive = hand-started; counts as online because the site really works). STOP IS GRACEFUL-FIRST (asks the brain's own `/shutdown` so it force-saves + writes the resume marker, waits for the port to close, only then `systemctl stop`; reports honestly when the brain never answered and training may be lost). **START RELOADS THE PROXY (Gee's explicit ask):** once the brain BINDS its port, ctl runs `nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx` — reload not restart, so the static site never drops, and `nginx -t` gates it so a bad config can't turn a brain outage into a total site outage. SITE SURVIVES THE BRAIN: `deploy/nginx-unity-brain-ctl.conf` uses `proxy_intercept_errors` + `error_page … = @named` to turn a dead upstream into **HTTP 200 with `brainOffline:true`** + a human sentence on `/public-state.json` + `/minds-eye.json` (previously a 502 **HTML** page that callers `.json()`-parse-errored on — or, on this vhost, SPA-swallowed into `index.html` being parsed as JSON); `minds-eye.html` + the dashboard's `probePublicBrain()` now read the flag so a real outage no longer renders as the softer "warming up…". DASHBOARD: a phase-aware **Brain Power** panel (only sensible actions enabled per phase) that degrades in three layers — full controls / nginx says the control plane itself is down / panel stays HIDDEN where no `/ctl` lane exists (so a local checkout shows no broken UI). SECURITY: loopback bind, Basic-auth `/ctl/` lane like `/admin/` (**verified 401 without auth**), `execFile` (no shell) against a hardcoded argv allowlist, and `deploy/brain-ctl-helper.sh` INDEPENDENTLY re-validates + **hardcodes the unit name** — deliberately one purpose-built script rather than NOPASSWD on `systemctl` itself, because this box also runs Forgejo and the lab's git (**verified as `unity`: refuses `forgejo`/`sshd` exit 77, allows `unity-brain`**; `visudo -c` parsed OK). **VERIFIED LIVE, FULL OPERATOR CYCLE TWICE, IN REAL CHROMIUM AGAINST THE ACTUAL DEPLOYED `dashboard.html` BYTES:** baseline `Brain online` (`up 3m · 6.2 GB`, Start disabled) → clicked ⏹ Stop → `Brain halted` + exit 42 + Start enabled, and **DURING THE OUTAGE `/` `/dashboard.html` `/compute.html` all 200** with both public lanes 200 `brainOffline:true` → clicked ▶ Start → `✓ Brain started and is serving. Proxy lanes reloaded.` → `Brain online`, real state resumed, clock advancing. **TRAINING INTACT AFTER BOTH CYCLES:** `✓ CLEAN SHUTDOWN detected — COMPATIBLE (formatVersion=5, 411,216,550 neurons). RESUMING. Auto-clear SKIPPED` + 30 Tier-3 schemas + 3 passedPhases restored. TESTS SHIPPED (mock brain + mock helper, touch no real unit): `scripts/test-brain-ctl.mjs` **31/31** (contract, graceful-stop ORDERING, 409 concurrency + lock release, helper refusals, survives the run) + `scripts/test-brain-power-ui.mjs` **13/13** real-Chromium (**proves START WORKS WITH THE BRAIN DOWN**). The loop caught four defects inspection missed: `unmanaged` mislabelled offline; exit-42 `halted` persisting after later starts; a duplicated 5-min bind-wait loop (now one configurable `waitForBrainBound`); minds-eye rendering an outage as "warming up". **TRAPS FOR THE NEXT OPERATOR:** (a) `/dashboard.html` SPA-swallows — the real URL is **`/html/dashboard.html`** (55 KB shell vs 345 KB page; I briefly mis-read a deploy as failed); (b) **`unityailab.com` is NOT the brain vhost** — it's `if-only-i-had-a-brain.git.unityailab.com`, and health-checking the wrong host reports a happy site while the brain is down; (c) the two-dashboard-copy trap again (`/var/www/pages/…` auto-deploys, `/opt/unity-brain/html/…` does NOT — both synced, md5 match); (d) the vhost's inline `location = /public-state.json` + `/minds-eye.json` were REMOVED (the snippet supersedes them; duplicate `location =` fails `nginx -t`), rollbacks at `/root/vhost.rollback-*`. Box backend still `d7ff7dda` — only `brain-ctl.js` + the dashboard were added; a `server/**` redeploy stays a separate deliberate action. Install runbook + verify-by-stopping-the-brain: `deploy/README.md`; full record: `deploy/REDEPLOY-NOTES.md` 2026-08-26 CTLPLANE.1 entry.
- **CTLPLANE.2/.3 + 🔴 SELF-INFLICTED WIPE INCIDENT + 🔴 SILENT 3-MONTH BACKUP OUTAGE FOUND — 2026-08-26 (Sponge):** **CTLPLANE.2** closed the two parts of Gee's ask I had overclaimed: (a) *"everything else inbetween that we support doing"* — `/reset`, `/savererun`, `/update` also restart the brain but were served BY the brain, so they carried the same dead zone; now `POST /ctl/reset|savererun|update|update-savestart`, each delegating to the brain's own endpoint when up (identical behaviour + its own bookkeeping) and doing the equivalent work directly when down (reset writes `.force-fresh` + CLEARS `.resume-marker.json` itself so a "fresh" brain can't resume the training being discarded; update runs the same `deploy/self-update.sh` with the same `UAL_KEEP_STATE` switch — meaning **a bad deploy that leaves the brain crash-looping is now fixable from the dashboard instead of needing SSH**). `savererun` is the deliberate exception: it rewrites grade pointers INSIDE the loaded weights, so with the brain down it REFUSES and says press Start first (dashboard disables it in that phase with the reason in the tooltip) rather than pretending. (b) **visitors** now told the truth: `index.html` was rendering an EMPTY leaderboard during an outage (reads as "nobody has donated yet") → now says "Brain offline"; `compute.html` told donors only "Disconnected — reconnecting" so they couldn't tell a wifi blip from a deliberate halt → now asks the snapshot and says the brain is down, there's no compute to donate, and the tab keeps retrying (best-effort, fire-and-forget, never affects the reconnect schedule); `minds-eye` already fixed in .1. `legend.html` needed nothing (download-link upgrader falls back to hardcoded links). **🔴 THEN I WIPED THE LIVE BRAIN.** Verifying .2 on the box I sent a bare `POST /ctl/update` expecting the "no deploy script here" refusal I'd just unit-tested — `self-update.sh` DOES exist there, so the probe ran a real FRESH-WALK deploy: `.force-fresh` written, brain restarted, boot **cleared 17 state files** (brain-weights v0-v2 + .bin, conversations, episodic-memory, schemas, mindspace-memory-v3, visual-memory-v9). **MY error, not an operator action.** **Damage bounded BY EVIDENCE, not assumption:** the journal shows **Gee himself ran UPDATE + FRESH WALK on 2026-08-25 14:59**, already wiping the 38-cell/grade-3 walk a day before I touched anything; between that and my mistake there were **0 passedCells, all six subjects pre-K, zero `CELL DONE pass=true`, and `donors=0` throughout** (no GPU → could not train). So I destroyed the weight files of a ~29-hour-old pre-K walk with no completed cells — real, but not the months I first feared; only Gee's prior fresh walk kept it cheap. **Recovery exhausted + documented so nobody re-treads it:** no deleted-but-open inodes (`lsof +L1`), `debugfs -R lsdel /dev/md3` = **0 deleted inodes** (unrecoverable), the ONLY restic snapshot was 2026-05-20 and **predates the weight staging**, newest on-box weight backup is 2026-07-08 which **pre-dates the 08-16 12M geometry hop so `WEIGHTS_FORMAT_VERSION` would refuse it anyway**. SURVIVED: `identity-core.json` (30 Tier-3 schemas), `definition-cache.json`, `community-tier.json`. I stopped the brain the moment I understood (to stop the fresh walk overwriting freed blocks), then restarted + confirmed a clean resume. **ROOT CAUSE FIXED (CTLPLANE.3, deployed + verified live):** the API let a single stray request wipe training — that's a design defect, not just operator error. `/ctl/update` (fresh-walk) + `/ctl/reset` now **REFUSE** without `{"confirm":"WIPE"}` (body) or `?confirm=WIPE`; the refusal **names the token AND the safe alternative** (`/ctl/update-savestart`); **`update-savestart` needs NO token deliberately** (friction on the safe path is what pushes people to the dangerous one); dashboard's Update+Fresh Walk now demands a **typed WIPE** like Reset; body reader bounded to 16KB. **Verified on the box: the exact command that caused this now returns `refused=true, needsConfirm=WIPE` with no `.force-fresh` armed.** **🔴 SEPARATE AND WORSE — found while hunting a restore point: `nightly-backup.service` had FAILED EVERY NIGHT SINCE 2026-05-20** (~3 months) with `Fatal: Resolving password failed: /root/.restic-password does not exist` — the file DOES exist (0600 root); **the unit's own `ProtectHome=true` makes `/root` unreadable to the service** (proven: `systemd-run -p ProtectHome=true /bin/sh -c 'test -r /root/.restic-password'` → BLOCKED). Consequence: ONE snapshot total (2026-05-20) — **no Forgejo backup, no lab-git backup, no brain weights** — even though the script was extended 2026-06-30 *specifically* to stage the trained brain after an earlier silent wipe. It looked configured and protected nothing. **FIXED** via drop-in `deploy/dropins/nightly-backup/10-fix-restic-password.conf` → `ProtectHome=read-only` (still denies /home + /run/user, keeps the hardening, secret deliberately NOT moved) + `ExecStopPost` logging `user.err` on non-zero exit so a future failure is LOUD. **VERIFIED: `Result=success`, `ExecMainStatus=0`, snapshots 1→2, and the new snapshot CONTAINS `unity-brain/brain-weights*.{bin,json}` + identity-core + schemas + conversations + episodic-memory.db\*.** Timer armed 03:24 nightly. **⚠ TWO DECISIONS LEFT FOR GEE (not made unilaterally): retention is `--keep-daily 3` (~3 days of history only), and the restic repo lives ON THE SAME BOX (`/var/backups/restic`) — a disk loss takes the backups with it. A brain that takes weeks to train wants `--keep-weekly/--keep-monthly` + an off-box destination.** TESTS now **84 assertions**: `test-brain-ctl.mjs` **52** (incl. 8 new interlock assertions that would have caught my mistake — bare update/reset refuse, refusal names token + safe route, a WRONG token `"yes"` is NOT accepted, update-savestart stays token-free), `test-brain-power-ui.mjs` **22** real-Chromium (savererun DISABLED when down + tooltip says why, Update/Reset still enabled, typed-confirm abort works), `test-brain-offline-pages.mjs` **10** NEW (visitor pages vs the exact offline body nginx returns). Two harness bugs found + fixed while writing that last suite, both of which would have made it pass/fail for the wrong reason: minds-eye.html is an ES module importing `js/brain/mindspace/transform.js` and 404ing that asset silently killed the whole module (test server now serves real static assets); compute.html only calls `connectToServer()` after WebGPU inits which headless Chromium can't do, so rather than fake a GPU the suite **extracts the ACTUAL `ws.onclose` body from the shipped file and runs it** (so it reads any future edit). **LESSONS: (1) never probe a destructive endpoint on production to observe its refusal — probe it in the harness; (2) a destructive verb needs an interlock at the API, not only in the UI; (3) a backup you have never restored from is a hypothesis.** Full record: `deploy/REDEPLOY-NOTES.md` 2026-08-26 INCIDENT entry.
## GOTCHA REVIEW — the wiki's gotcha pages turned into board work, plus a fresh hunt — filed 2026-08-27

Gee (verbatim): *"review the gotcha's add them to the todo work and use ask me question where needed... and rerun the ingestion to search for more gotchas"*

**Method.** Five gotcha pages were re-read for anything still LIVE (already-fixed history stays documentation, it does not become work), then six mechanical hunts ran for more. ⭐ **The highest-value finding came from checking named instruments against the LIVE payload rather than against the source** — the source looked correct.

⭐ **Three forks were put to Gee rather than guessed** (`sem_*` sub-bands → investigate-only; `emit.js` TDZ → leave documented; provenance → all 31 pages).

- [x] **✅ DIAGNOSED + INSTRUMENT FIXED 2026-08-27 — cause isolated to ONE line in the Rust donor, and the null now explains itself. The FEATURE half is `GOTCHA.3b`.** ⭐ **Three producers, and for a native-donor brain all three are dead:** **(1) CPU** — `lastMeanVoltage` is written only at `js/brain/cluster.js:4131`, inside `step()`, which the cortex cannot run (`stepAwait` refuses above 2M; four raw-step sites carry the same return). **(2) BROWSER donor — FULLY IMPLEMENTED and working:** `html/compute.html:1481-1493` does a once-per-tick GPU atomic reduction via `gpu.readbackVoltageMean(c.name)` and puts it on `perCluster[name].meanVoltage`. ⛔ **(3) NATIVE donor — NEVER SENT, and it is one hardcoded literal:** `donor-app/src/donor.rs:1361` builds `PerClusterResult { spike_count_total: so.total, last_spike_count: so.last, mean_voltage: None }`, and `donor-app/src/protocol.rs:129-130` carries `#[serde(rename = "meanVoltage", skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` — **so the key is omitted from the JSON entirely**, `brain-server.js:6282`'s `typeof entry.meanVoltage === 'number'` is never true, and `clusters[name].meanVoltage` is never assigned. ⭐ **The wire field, the serde rename and the server-side EMA blend all already exist — the only missing piece is the donor computing the number.** ⚠ **The fix shipped here is the INSTRUMENT, not the feature:** `state.js` now publishes `meanVoltageSource` alongside the value, naming which producer supplied it (`cpu-step` / `gpu-donor-readback`) or why there is none (`unreported-by-this-donor (native donor sends mean_voltage: None — GOTCHA.3b)` / `no-gpu-donor-attached`). ⛔ **A bare `null` cannot distinguish "not sampled yet" from "this donor does not report it" from "broken", and that indistinguishability IS the defect class** — inventing a number would have been worse than publishing nothing. ⚠ Verified `this._gpuConnected` is reachable from `state.js` before relying on it (`Object.assign(ServerBrain.prototype, SERVER_STATE_MIXIN)` at `brain-server.js:8125`, and state.js already reads `this._gpuClient` at three sites) — **not repeating the ownership trap this item is about.** **ORIGINAL FILING:** **⛔⛔ `GOTCHA.3` — `meanVoltage` reads `null` for ALL SEVEN clusters on the live brain, and the fix that was supposed to repair it corrected the NAME while the PRODUCER stays unreachable.** Live evidence, local brain at ~3.6h uptime actively training: every cluster in `state.clusters` carries a `meanVoltage` key whose value is `null`. ⛔ **`DORMANT.3` (2026-08-25) closed this once** — its comment in `server/brain-server/state.js:440-446` states the read *"NAMED THE WRONG FIELD, so all seven clusters reported `null` forever for a number that is computed on every tick"*, and it now reads `cluster.lastMeanVoltage` first with `cluster.meanVoltage` as fallback. **Both are null.** ⭐ **The reason is in that comment's own words: "a number that is computed on every tick" is FALSE at biological scale.** The only writer of `lastMeanVoltage` is `this.lastMeanVoltage = vSum / size` at `js/brain/cluster.js:4131`, which sits **inside `step(dt, opts)`** (`:3839`) — and `step()` is precisely what the cortex cannot run: `stepAwait` refuses at `:4235` (*"At biological scale a CPU step is FORBIDDEN"*) and four raw-step sites carry `if (this.size > 2000000) return`. The GPU steps the brain via `compute_batch` instead. ⚠ **So this is the fourth-plus instance of the class, with a twist that makes it worse: the fix's comment asserts the value is live, which is exactly what stops the next person re-checking.** ⚠ **Do NOT assume the cause is only `step()`** — `server/brain-server.js:6282-6283` assigns `this.clusters[name].meanVoltage` from **GPU ack telemetry**, and `state.js`'s fallback should therefore catch it. Both being null means **either** the donor is not sending voltage telemetry, **or** the ack path is not reaching those objects, **or** the assignment lands on a different object than the one `getState` reads (the `brain._x` vs `this._x` ownership trap, which has produced three dead reads already). ⛔ **Establish WHICH before writing a line of fix** — three candidate causes, and the last two are indistinguishable from the source. ⚠ Also verify the sub-2M clusters separately: `brainstem` is ~0.002 of the total (~822K at 411M) and so is NOT excluded by the 2M refusal, yet it reads null too — if a cluster that CAN step also reads null, `step()` is not the whole story.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-27 — BOTH HALVES SHIPPED; the CUDA half went live in donor v0.3.33, and the "blocked on a toolchain fact" framing DISSOLVED under measurement.** Gee: *"go ahead and review all of that make the best decision for what Unity needs while following the laws and decide and build it"*. ⛔ **The fork's premise was wrong twice over:** the checked-in PTX header reads **`.target sm_75` / `.version 8.0`, built by CUDA 12.0.140** — not `compute_60` as the staged-half note (and this row) claimed — and the real constraint was the **PTX ISA version**, pinned at 8.0 by **v0.3.21** (*"the CUDA path comes back on r570 hosts"*), which a CUDA-13 regeneration would have silently un-fixed. ⭐ **Decision: match the toolchain, don't upgrade it** — regenerated in a `nvidia/cuda:12.0.1-devel` container carrying the **exact same compiler build (12.0.140 / CL-32267302)** at `-arch=sm_75`; **no local toolkit install, no arch raise, no multi-target PTX, zero compatibility change.** ⭐ **Verified without running a GPU** (the only local NVIDIA card is the display GPU, which stays untouched): the eight pre-existing kernels came out **byte-identical under label normalization** — the new PTX is the production-proven PTX plus one function — and `ptxas` (the same PTX→SASS compilation the driver JIT performs) assembles it clean at `sm_75`/`sm_86` (the A40)/`sm_89` on the 12.0 stack plus `sm_89` on 13.0. Launch wiring audited arg-for-arg against the `.cu` signature. ⚠ **Honest limit stated in the release note: not GPU-run before tagging** — the live verdict is loud either way, because `meanVoltageSource` flips from `unreported-by-this-donor` to a value when the pod updates. ⚠ **Tagging does NOT touch the running A40** — the pod keeps 0.3.32 until deliberately updated (a donor drop + full matrix re-upload; ride the next scheduled restart). Two stale `compute_60` comments in `cuda.rs` corrected, with the regeneration recipe + ISA-pin reasoning written where the next person will look. Ledger: FINALIZED §2026-08-27 PTXPIN. **ORIGINAL FILING:** **⏳ HALF SHIPPED 2026-08-27 — the wgpu half is LIVE in donor v0.3.32; the CUDA half is the whole remainder, and it is BLOCKED on a toolchain fact.** ⭐ **Done:** `voltage_mean.wgsl`, the `compute.rs` pipeline/buffers, `StepOut.mean_voltage`, and `donor.rs:1361` now passing `so.mean_voltage` instead of the hardcoded `None`. **A wgpu donor reports the number.** ⛔ **Remaining:** the CUDA kernel. The connected A40 is a CUDA donor, so `meanVoltage` still reads `null` there — with `meanVoltageSource` naming why, which is the instrument working. ⛔ **BLOCKER, and it is not effort:** the kernel must reach the donor through a **precompiled `kernels.ptx`**, and regenerating it targets `compute_60` while the local nvcc is **CUDA 13.0, which dropped that arch**. ⚠ **So this needs either a toolchain with `compute_60` support, a decision to raise the minimum arch (which would drop older donor GPUs), or a second PTX target — a real fork, not a build step.** ⚠ **The CUDA `load_function("voltage_mean")` is already written OPTIONAL** so the absent kernel degrades to `None` rather than breaking `CudaEngine::new` for every CUDA donor. **ORIGINAL FILING:** **⛔ `GOTCHA.3b` — make the NATIVE donor report `mean_voltage`. Needs a donor release; everything else is already in place.** ⭐ **The whole server-side chain works and is waiting:** the wire field exists (`protocol.rs:129-130`), the server blends it with an EMA (`brain-server.js:6280-6284`), `state.js` publishes it, and the browser donor already proves the shape end-to-end. **The one missing piece is `donor-app/src/donor.rs:1361`, which hardcodes `mean_voltage: None`.** ⚠ **It is NOT a one-liner, and that is the whole cost:** `engine.run_substeps` returns only `so.total` / `so.last`, so a voltage-mean **reduction does not exist in the Rust engine** — and it must be written **TWICE**, once for wgpu (`src/shaders/`, which has `bucket_mean.wgsl`, `current_max.wgsl` and `spike_count.wgsl` to copy the reduction pattern from, but no voltage mean) and once for CUDA (`src/cuda_kernels.cu`). ⭐ **Cost it against the value before building:** this is a **slow telemetry signal** — `compute.html` deliberately reads it back **once per tick, not per substep**, with its own comment saying per-substep *"would be wasteful"*. So the target is one reduction + one small readback per tick, not a hot path. ⛔ **Release discipline is mine end-to-end** per the standing rule: bump `Cargo.toml`, write `RELEASE-0.3.32.md`, push `donor-v*` to **both** remotes, then verify **all four** KI-22 surfaces on the LIVE site (tag, release assets, template PIN, and the shipped `.exe --version`) — ⚠ a green CI log is not one of the four, and a release once published nothing while logging all-green. ⚠ **Operational cost to weigh:** updating the pod binary drops the donor mid-walk. The durability law says that loses nothing (the coordinator holds the master copy), but it costs a full matrix re-upload on reconnect — so batch it with the next donor release rather than spending a reconnect on telemetry alone.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-27 — all 48 off-by-one rows corrected, 14 MORE wrong rows found that the filing did not know about, and the whole class is now enforced mechanically instead of by care.** ⭐ **The durable deliverable is the check, not the corrections:** `npm run wiki:coverage` now validates **every** `| \`path\` | N |` row in the wiki against `wc -l`. **176 exact counts verified, 4 deliberately approximate, 0 wrong.** ⚠ **`~N` is the honest escape hatch** — `FINALIZED.md`, `TODO.md`, `RESUME.md` and `deploy/REDEPLOY-NOTES.md` grow on essentially every commit, so an exact count for them cannot stay true for an hour and pinning one would make the check cry wolf. Those four carry `~N` and are skipped, with the reason written onto the page. ⭐ **The check found a 100-line error the moment it existed:** `scripts/Gattling Gun Savestart Forced.txt` was recorded as **94** lines and is **194** — a transcription error from an adjacent measurement, in a row no human re-read would ever have questioned. ⚠ **And it found 13 rows that had simply GROWN**, mostly because of my own edits this session (`state.js` +37 from `GOTCHA.3a`, `cluster.js` +75 across two batches, the five `deploy/*.md` pages +10 to +16 each from the `DOCPROV.3` frontmatter). **That is not the off-by-one class — it is ordinary drift, and it is exactly what the item predicted:** a line count went stale inside the commit that recorded it **twice running** (`cluster.js` 4,984 → 5,011 → 5,059). on any file ending in a newline. ⛔ **Verified as systematic, not sporadic**, across six sampled files: `neurons.js` 201/202, `synapses.js` 220/221, `sparse-matrix.js` 1400/1401, `cluster.js` 4984/4985, `modules.js` 436/437, `brain-server.js` 12414/12415. **Affected pages** (those built from the dossier): `neural-substrate`, `chemistry-endocrine`, `memory-and-consolidation`, `consciousness-and-inner-voice`, `language-emission`, `sensory-cortices`, `mindspace`, `gpu-compute`, `brain-harnesses`. ⚠ **NOT affected:** `brain-server`, `curriculum`, `vocabulary-tables`, `html-pages`, `deploy-and-ci`, `launchers`, `tooling-scripts`, `docs-tree`, `donor-lane` — those counts came from a `wc -l` inventory. `cortex-cluster` is **already corrected**. ⭐ **And the deeper rule this exposed, worth more than the ±1:** a line count went stale **inside the commit that recorded it** — `cluster.js` read 4,984, and the `GOTCHA.5` comment added in the same batch took it to 5,011. **A line count is a READING, not a property; quote it with the commit that produced it**, exactly as `state.totalNeurons` must be quoted with its boot. ⚠ Fix by editor, not by script — and the numbers must be re-measured at the fixing commit, not copied from this item.
- [ ] **⚠ `GOTCHA.1` — `js/brain/cluster/emit.js` throws on direct `import()` (pre-existing circular-import TDZ). LEFT DOCUMENTED on Gee's call 2026-08-27, not fixed.** Confirmed pre-existing on an untouched baseline via `git stash`, so no recent change introduced it. ⭐ **Production is unaffected** — importing `curriculum.js` first warms the cycle, and that is the real boot order. **Impact is on harnesses and verification only**, which is not nothing: `emit.js` owns emission and `EMITZERO.1` is open, so the file most in need of isolated harnessing is the one that cannot be imported alone. **Workaround: import `curriculum.js` before `emit.js`.** ⚠ The eventual fix is the `pk-curve.js` pattern — extract the shared symbol into a leaf module with no imports, which cannot participate in a cycle. ⛔ Deliberately NOT done mid-walk: an import restructure in the emission path changes nothing semantically but the risk is unpriced.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-27 — and the ROOT CAUSE turned out not to be the dead keys at all. Two real fixes shipped; the `sem_*` delete is no longer worth doing.** ⭐ **§3.2 root-cause-then-siblings changed the whole item:** deleting 6 unread keys would have fixed half a symptom, because `word_motor_*` nests identically and must stay. The actual causes were in the two places that *consume* the region map. <br>⭐ **FIX 1 — `validateClusterRegions()`, extracted from `uploadCluster` in `js/brain/gpu-compute.js` and EXPORTED so it can be harnessed.** The old loop could only run behind a real WebGPU device, so its behaviour was verified by reading — which is what let both defects survive. **(a) Nesting was reported as an overlap ERROR:** `start < prevEnd` rejected all 12 sub-bands and warned `overlaps prior region … skipping` for each, so **every healthy upload printed 12 warnings** — and a genuine partial overlap would have printed in identical words, indistinguishable from the noise. Nested spans are now summarised on one line; a true boundary-crossing overlap still warns loudly. ⛔ **(b) WHICH REGION SURVIVED WAS AN ACCIDENT.** `sem` and `sem_ela` have **identical `start`** (verified live: `150000`/`150000` at 200K; `word_motor`/`word_motor_ela` both `188000`), so sorting by `start` alone left the winner to `Array.prototype.sort` stability **plus object-literal insertion order**. It kept the umbrella only because the umbrella is declared first — **had a sub-band ever sorted first it would have claimed the span and `sem` or `word_motor` would have been silently dropped from GPU registration**, with the warning naming the sub-band. Sorting is now `(start ASC, span DESC)`, so an enclosing region *always* precedes what it encloses. <br>⭐ **FIX 2 — the real cost, and it was NOT bandwidth.** `_gpuClearCortexSpikeRegion` **does not validate the region name**: for each one it encodes a type-9 frame, **sends it to the donor**, counts it via `_countTeachOut`, and — with the language pseudo-cluster up — sends a **second** `langCortex/<region>` frame. So a full clear spent **up to 24 wire frames on regions the donor has never heard of**, on the path **11 of `_clearSpikes`'s 13 call sites take**. ⛔ **Worse than the traffic: those frames land in `teach_ops`, which `TEACHMIRROR.1` made the signal separating a saturated donor from an idle one — the no-ops were padding the instrument used to judge donor health.** Fixed at the chokepoint: `NeuronCluster.topLevelRegionNames()` (memoized, **structural containment test — no name list, nothing to keep in sync when a region is added**), consumed by `curriculum.js`'s full-clear path. Clearing an enclosing region already covers every span nested inside it, so nothing is left uncleared. <br>**VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, not reading — real cortex clusters at 2,000 AND 200,000 neurons:** baseline 23 declared → 11 accepted / 12 skipped captured *before* the change; after it, **accepted set IDENTICAL (same 11, same order)** — this is deliberately not a behaviour change; `topLevelRegionNames()` **exactly equals the GPU-registered set** (the invariant that matters — a mismatch would either address a region the GPU lacks or skip one it has); memoization returns the same array identity; a region-less cluster returns `[]` without throwing; and two adversarial cases pass — **a true partial overlap still warns** (not silenced as nesting), and **the umbrella wins even when the sub-band is declared first** (hazard (b) closed). `node --check` clean on all three files, ESM `import()` clean, **bundle rebuilt** (131,965 → 132,008 lines). ⚠ **The 6 `sem_*` keys were LEFT IN PLACE.** With both consumers fixed they now cost nothing measurable, and deleting region metadata on a training brain to remove 6 unread `lang_*` payload rows is not worth the change. **ORIGINAL FILING BELOW:** **⏸ `GOTCHA.2` — INVESTIGATION DONE 2026-08-27. Verdict: deleting the 6 `sem_*` keys is GEOMETRY-NEUTRAL and strictly positive. `word_motor_*` must STAY.** All seven enumerations of `regions` were found and read: <br>⭐ **(1) `js/brain/gpu-compute.js:738` — THE SUB-BANDS ARE ALREADY BEING SKIPPED.** The validator sorts regions by `start` and refuses any whose `start < prevEnd`, logging *"overlaps prior region … skipping"*. Nested sub-bands overlap their umbrella **by construction**, so all 12 are dropped from GPU registration on every `uploadCluster` — **23 declared regions become 11 validated, and 12 warnings are printed every time.** Deleting the 6 `sem_*` removes 6 spurious warnings and changes nothing the GPU sees. ⛔ **LATENT HAZARD FOUND HERE, worth more than the cleanup:** `sem` and `sem_ela` have **identical `start` values**, so which one survives depends on `Array.prototype.sort` stability plus **object-literal insertion order**. It works today only because the umbrella is declared first and ES2019 sort is stable — **if a sub-band ever sorted first, the UMBRELLA `sem` region would be the one skipped**, silently, with a warning that names the sub-band. **(2) `gpu-compute.js:829-830` — `MAX_REGIONS = 16` and the pack is `.slice(0, 16)`.** 11 validated is under the cap, so this is fine *today* — ⚠ but 23 declared would silently truncate to 16 if the overlap-skip ever stopped working. ### ⭐ Hunts that came back EMPTY — recorded so nobody re-runs them

⛔ **A negative result is a finding.** Six mechanical hunts, four clean:

| hunt | pattern | result |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | `typeof this.X === 'function'` guards where `X` has **no definition** anywhere (the `_teachWordSpellingDirectFinal` class — 37 call sites, 0 definitions) | **CLEAN.** Zero across every tracked `.js`/`.mjs`/`.cjs`. That class is genuinely closed |
| **B** | NUL bytes in tracked text files (make a file read as **binary to `grep`** → empty results, no error) | **CLEAN.** Zero |
| **C** | cost gates that can resolve to *never* (the idle-only schema learn) | **CLEAN.** Every `idle` hit is an avatar/motor action state, not a gate |
| **D** | module consts used exactly ONCE where the single use is a log line (the `GRADE_TIMEOUT_MS` shape) | **5 candidates, all FALSE POSITIVES.** Each single use is functional (a catalog lookup, a tolerance compare, a pixel cap); the heuristic fired because the line happened to contain a backtick. ⚠ The detector needs "referenced only from inside a template/console **argument**", not "on a line containing one" |
| **E** | region keys with no readers | ⭐ **HIT — `GOTCHA.2`** |
| **F** | dashboard reads a state field nothing publishes (the producer/consumer class) | ⚠ **INCONCLUSIVE, deliberately not filed.** 25 candidates, but `s`/`st` in `dashboard.html` is frequently a LOCAL (a checkpoint row, a `brain-ctl` status, a curriculum row), and separating those from the global snapshot needs scope analysis the grep does not have. **Filing 25 phantom items would be worse than filing none.** The sharper substitute is what found `GOTCHA.3`: check named instruments against the LIVE payload |

⚠ **And one live-payload absence that is NOT a bug:** `bundleFreshness` is missing from the running snapshot, but `server/brain-server.js:8969` does publish it — the local brain is on build `2673d14c`, which predates it. ⛔ **Checked the code before filing**, because *"the page can be current while the server is old"* is this project's most-repeated trap and it applies to state fields too.

NOT tagged, and the three reasons are below.** Gee: *"yea lets do the doner release on those"*. ⭐ **What shipped into the tree:** `donor-app/src/shaders/voltage_mean.wgsl` (new) + `VOLTAGE_MEAN_SHADER` in `gpu.rs` + `VoltParams`/`VOLT_PARTIALS`/pipeline/two buffers in `compute.rs` + `StepOut.mean_voltage` + a `Backend::voltage_mean` arm + `donor.rs:1361` now passes `so.mean_voltage` instead of the hardcoded `None` that made the field read `null` on all seven clusters for the native donor's entire life. ⭐ **Design choices worth keeping:** the reduction is **partial sums, not an atomic**, because WGSL has no atomic float add and fixed-point scaling would lose precision on a value that straddles zero; the partials buffer is **engine-level, not per-cluster** (`step_cluster` reduces one at a time, so 256 slots ≈ 1KB total and no `Cluster` allocation site was touched); it is sampled **once per substep RUN, not per substep**, matching `compute.html`'s own *"would be wasteful"* note; the host divides by the **real `n`** after summing so a short final chunk cannot skew it; and it returns **`None` on any failure, never `Some(0.0)`** — `0.0` is a legitimate mean for a Rulkov population straddling zero, so a zero-on-error would be indistinguishable from a real quiet cluster, which is the exact failure this field exists to end. ⛔ **A SERIOUS BUG IN MY OWN SHADER, CAUGHT BEFORE IT SHIPPED:** the first draft used `@workgroup_size(64)` while the host computes its dispatch with the shared `dispatch_dims()`, which divides by `const WORKGROUP: u32 = 256`. For 256 slots that returns **one workgroup** — so only 64 threads would have launched, `partials[64..255]` would **never have been written**, and the host would have summed stale memory into the mean **every tick, producing a plausible number with no error anywhere.** Fixed to 256 and the contract is now written on the shader. ⛔ **THREE GATES BEFORE TAGGING, none of them busywork:** **(1)** the WGSL is validated by naga at `build_pipeline` **at runtime** — `cargo check` does not parse `include_str!` shaders, and I have **not run it on a GPU**. A stub returning `0.0` would have been a lie. The board's own cost note says this must be written **twice**; half is honest, a fake half is not. **(3)** ⚠ **Tagging drops the A40 mid-walk and costs a full 17-matrix re-upload** — the board's own guidance is *"batch it with the next donor release rather than spending a reconnect on telemetry alone."* ⛔ **`Cargo.toml` deliberately LEFT AT 0.3.31 and no `RELEASE-0.3.32.md` written** — bumping without a note trips `docs:drift` check 4, and writing a note for something untagged is a note that lies. **Bump + note + tag land together when the release is authorized.**

## SRCGAP — a page can make claims about a file it never declares, so drift can never fire on it — filed 2026-08-27

- [x] **✅ BUILT 2026-08-27 — `SRCGAP.1`: `docs:drift` check 9, "sources cover the files each page cites". 21 real gaps standing after 6 were fixed.** ⛔ **The class this closes was hit FOUR TIMES IN ONE DAY** — `HTML-ENTRY-POINTS` cited `html/docs.html:189-207`, `THRESHOLD-DERIVATION` cited `emit.js:1719`, `KNOWN_ISSUES`' `KI-16` lives in `curriculum.js`, `HELD-BACK`'s entire noise-gate section is `cluster.js:2173` — **and none of those files were in the citing page's `sources:`.** ⭐ **That is worse than a wrong source list: check 8 can only ever fire on files a page NAMES, so a page making load-bearing claims about an undeclared file is a page drift is structurally blind to.** All four were caught by reading; this makes the mechanical subset mechanical. **Signal = a line number** (`path.js:1404` is a precise claim, which is exactly what `sources` is for); basename-only citations resolve **only when unique across tracked files**, ambiguous ones skipped not guessed (eleven `README.md` files). ⛔ **THREE OF MY OWN DEFECTS FOUND BY RUNNING IT, and the first one invalidates the check's own premise:** **(1)** ⛔ **the line-number rule would have caught NONE of the four motivating gaps** — run against `471b5248`, three of those pages had **zero** line-precise citations and `KNOWN_ISSUES`' only one pointed at a file already declared. **The signal was wrong for the class it was built for, and only running it against the pre-fix pages revealed that.** **(2)** A bare-mention second signal was built and **measured**: bare counts for the four gaps were `cluster.js` 2, `emit.js` 1, **`consolidation-engine.js` 0**, `curriculum.js` 2, `docs.html` 4, `brain-equations.html` 5 — so ≥2 catches four of six and ⛔ **`consolidation-engine.js` at ZERO is unreachable by ANY mention-based rule** (the page discussed `DREAM_CONSOLIDATION_MAX_MS` without naming the file). It added **+300 items at threshold 2, +175 at 3, +67 at 6, +13 even at 15** against a 43-item baseline — **DELETED on those numbers, because this file's own law is that a check which cries wolf gets ignored and `sources` is explicitly a FOCUSED set.** **(3)** ⛔ **I passed `gaps.slice(0, 30)` into `note()`, which capped the COUNT and not just the display — every threshold reported "46 items" and the check looked insensitive to its own knob.** Fixed, **and the identical latent cap was fixed on check 6 (`dead.slice(0, 40)`) while in the file.** ⭐ **A guard that silently under-reports is this file's own reassuring-direction lie, self-inflicted twice.** ⛔ **FOURTH defect, and it hid its own fix:** the sources-block regex required every line after `sources:` to be `- item`, so a YAML **`#` comment inside the list truncated the parse** — the four sources I added with an explaining comment above them **silently did not register, and the check reported the same 27 gaps as before.** Parser made comment-tolerant (and item extraction made line-based, since a block-wide `- ` sweep would harvest dashes out of comment prose); **fixing it immediately revealed a further real gap on `WORD-SALAD-FIX` that had been invisible behind the truncation.** ⭐ **THE HONEST LIMIT, WRITTEN ON THE CHECK ITSELF: this narrows the gap, it does not close it. A page can be ABOUT a file it never cites precisely, and no mechanical rule available here finds that** — choosing `sources` stays a judgment made at verification time. **6 gaps fixed** (`WORD-SALAD-FIX` ×5, `SEEDED-TOPOLOGY-SPEC` ×1); **21 remain, each to be settled by that page's own verification pass rather than by bulk-adding sources** — which would produce exactly the cries-wolf noise the bare-mention signal was deleted for.

it un-does it, silently, with no counter and no log.** ⭐ **And the guard is provably dead:** `topLevelRegionNames()` is a **method on the `NeuronCluster` class** (`js/brain/cluster.js:1404`), so every instance has it via the prototype; the enclosing `_clearSpikes(regionNames = null)` (`:11929`) binds `const cluster = this.cluster`, and the block is already gated on `cluster._gpuProxy && cluster._gpuProxy.clearSpikeSlice && cluster.regions` — three fields only a real cluster has, two lines under a `cluster.lastSpikes.fill(0)`. **There is no reachable path where the method is absent and the object is still a cluster.** ⛔ **This is a `feedback_no_fallbacks_law` violation** (capability-degradation `if-X-else-Y`, which the law separates from legitimate defensive I/O `try/catch`) **and the mirror image of `DORMANT.1`** — there, `_teachWordSpellingDirectFinal` had **37 `typeof`-guarded call sites and zero definitions**, so the guards hid a missing function; here the definition exists and the guard hides a live bug. ⭐ **The generalisable rule: a `typeof` guard around a method your own class defines is never protection — it is either dead code or a silent downgrade, and you cannot tell which by reading the call site.** **Fix:** call it unconditionally, so a future refactor that removes the method throws loudly instead of quietly re-teaching the donor 24 phantom frames per clear. ⚠ **Bundle must be rebuilt in the same commit** — `js/app.bundle.js:103714` carries the same guarded expression, and the bundle is deliberately NOT auto-cleared at boot. ⚠ **Read scope stated honestly: `curriculum.js` is 28,340 lines and I did NOT read all 36 chunks of it.** I read the enclosing function, its binding of `cluster`, the definition site in `cluster.js`, and every call site of the method repo-wide. **Claiming a full read of 28,340 lines would be the same class of lie this whole sweep is fixing.**

## WIKIFULL — the wiki covers the whole stack, not a seed — filed 2026-08-27

Gee (verbatim): *"12 pages i want the full fucking stack all files in it"*

**The state that provoked it:** `wiki/` shipped 2026-08-27 with **12 files — 3 infrastructure (`CLAUDE.md`, `index.md`, `log.md`) and only 9 content pages** (5 modules, 2 concepts, 1 decision, 1 gotcha) against a **448-file tracked repo**. It was seeded from one session's findings, which is a demonstration, not a map.

- [x] **✅ BUILT 2026-08-27 — `wiki/` went 9 content pages → 35, and the ask became a number: `npm run wiki:coverage` reports 448 of 448 tracked files named, 100.0%, 0 broken wikilinks, 0 orphans, index in sync.** 26 new pages + 5 existing extended; `scripts/wiki-coverage-check.mjs` is tracked, report-only, and **fail-soft when `wiki/` is absent** (it prints *"nothing was checked — this is NOT a pass"* rather than green, because a fresh clone legitimately has no wiki). ⭐ **Facts came from the AST graph built the same day** (`graphify-out/graph.json`, 2,747 nodes over 167 code files, code-only, 0 LLM calls) plus each file's own header — per-file symbol lists are extracted, not recalled, and `last-verified: c5e9d412` is real on every new page. ⛔ **The first coverage run said 67.4%, and every single gap was an ellipsis:** `bank-000.json … bank-009.json` covered two of eleven, `RELEASE-0.3.{11,17,…}.md` covered none, `college1.js … college4.js` covered two of four — **an ellipsis reads as completeness to a human and as nothing to a checker.** Fixed by writing 448 literal paths. ⭐ **The matching rule is picky on purpose:** exact path, or a basename that is UNIQUE across the tree, or a disambiguating suffix — because there are eleven `README.md` files and letting one mention cover all eleven is a false pass in the reassuring direction. **Four findings from reading the code instead of the docs:** ⛔ **(1)** `js/brain/visual-cortex.js`'s header claimed the IT stage *"calls AI for high-level description"* — **false for months and contradicted at `visual-cortex.js:214` in the same file**; `describeImage()`/`autoDetectVision()` verified as no-op stubs; **fixed in the code**, because a header asserting an LLM in the perception path is the inverse of this project's load-bearing claim. ⛔ **(2)** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` said `curriculum.js` is `~12500 lines`; it is **28,340** — stale by ~16,000, corrected. ⚠ **(3)** `js/brain/cluster/README.md` says `cluster.js` "is now 4,728 lines"; it reads **4,985** — recorded on the page, not silently fixed. ⚠ **(4)** My own draft of the `docs-tree` page **fabricated `docs/ADMIN-ONBOARDING.md`** and omitted five real files; caught by listing the directory instead of recalling it, and the five turned out to be corpus the running brain fetches by name (`Ultimate Unity.txt`, `english-baseline.txt`, `coding-knowledge.txt`, `persona-cosmic.txt`, `component-templates.txt`). ⚠ **Honest limit, stated on the `wiki-coverage` page itself:** 100% means every file is NAMED, not that every description is right — 21 of 26 module pages are `status: verified`, the 5 pre-existing ones keep their `TODO: ingest` markers. **ORIGINAL FILING:** **`WIKIFULL.1` — every file in the stack is named on a wiki page, and coverage is MECHANICAL rather than claimed.** ⛔ **The trap to avoid is the one this project has paid for repeatedly: a page that ASSERTS completeness.** *"All files are covered"* written in prose is the same class of lie as `SKILL_TREE.md:358` claiming a route was removed while it ran in production for four more months. So coverage gets a checker — enumerate tracked files, enumerate the paths named in wiki `sources:` blocks and page bodies, print the difference — and the number it prints is the only claim allowed. ⚠ **Not one page per file:** 448 pages of boilerplate is worse than 9 good ones, and `wiki/CLAUDE.md`'s own rule caps a page at ~150 lines and wants pages to map to a module or subsystem. The shape is a page per real subsystem, each **naming every file it owns** with a role read from the code, so the union is the whole tree. ⚠ **Data directories are named collectively but completely** — `corpora/academic` (65 files), `corpora/life` (19), `corpora/coding` (14), `vox-bank` (11), `assets/social` (11) are content, not architecture; each gets one page that lists what it holds and why, and every filename appears. ⚠ **Facts come from the AST graph built the same day** (`graphify-out/graph.json`, 2,747 nodes over 167 code files) plus each file's own header, NOT from memory — per-file symbol lists are then extracted, not invented. ⚠ **`wiki/` is gitignored on Gee's word**, so the pages themselves land untracked; the coverage checker is tracked because it is tooling that governs, and it must fail-soft when `wiki/` is absent so a teammate's clone does not report a broken wiki it was never given.

- **BACKUP HARDENING — RESTORE TESTED + TWO DECISIONS DOCUMENTED FOR GEE (2026-08-26, Sponge):** follow-through on the silent-backup find. (a) **The restore was actually RUN, not just written down** — `restic restore latest --include '*/unity-brain/*'` returned `brain-weights{,-v0}.{json,bin}` + `identity-core.json`, the JSON parsed, and contents matched the live brain's then-current state (`passedCells=0`, `grades.ela=pre-K`). The backup is a backup, not a hypothesis. Two gotchas found BY running it: `--include` filters paths *within* the snapshot but still writes ~10GB of Forgejo (make room + clean up), and **a snapshot from before a `WEIGHTS_FORMAT_VERSION`/geometry change is NOT a restore point** — precisely why the 2026-07-08 on-box backup was useless after the 08-16 12M hop. (b) **`deploy/BACKUP-DECISIONS.md`** written with ready-to-run one-liners for the two POLICY choices I deliberately did not make alone: retention `--keep-daily 3` → suggested `--keep-daily 14 --keep-weekly 8 --keep-monthly 12` (nearly free: restic dedups, brain state ~22MB/snapshot, **717GB free** on the box), and the restic repo living on **`/dev/md3` — the SAME array as `/opt/unity-brain`** (RAID survives a disk, not an array loss / fs corruption / `rm -rf` / reprovision, so one bad event takes the brain AND every backup of it; off-box options costed out but not chosen since it involves money + credentials). Also includes a periodic verify procedure, because the reason the 3-month outage went unnoticed is that **nothing ever checked the exit code**. (c) **`deploy/README.md` runbook was STALE and is now complete** — it documented CTLPLANE.1's six endpoints only, with **no mention of the WIPE interlock**, which is a hazard in itself (the next operator would hit the refusal and assume a bug). Now carries all 11 endpoints with a "needs the brain up?" column (savererun the single YES, with the reason), the refused-vs-confirmed curl worked through, why the interlock exists, and an explicit **"never probe the wiping verbs against a live box to watch them refuse"**. Every documented endpoint was then re-verified against the live box, deliberately probing ONLY the ones that cannot act — applying the lesson instead of just recording it. Runbook + drop-ins synced to `/opt/unity-brain/deploy/` so the box copy is the current one.
