Surfaced by the `ARTHOG.1` verdict: the rate limit engaged exactly as specified, and the phase kept dragging. That is the row's own fallback answer — *"art was not the cause and that is equally useful to know"* — arriving as a finding.

- [~] **⏳ INSTRUMENT BUILT 2026-08-27 — the per-caller tally this row named as the honest first move now exists; the lever decision waits on its first live read.** Every `_teachAssociationPairs` call tallies `calls / pairs / ms` under its caller label (`cluster._assocCallers`; ms added at both real exits — clean finish AND budget stop — using the existing `startMs`), published bounded at `state.curriculum.liveness.assocCallers` (top 12 by ms, the `teachProfile` pattern). Diagnostic only: rebuilt from zero each boot, never persisted, never gates anything. ⛔ **The lever stays UNPULLED on purpose:** the curriculum caller IS the teaching, so any bound on it is the `STRUCTURE_DOSE` class and needs a RE-PRICE written down first — and pricing it before the tally names WHERE the 16 s/call goes would repeat the exact inference-not-measurement this row exists to end. **Lands on the next press; read `assocCallers` there, then decide.** **ORIGINAL FILING:** **⛔ `ASSOCBOUND.1` — `_teachAssociationPairs` is the most expensive lane per call in the walk, and the curriculum's own caller has no bound at all.** Measured live on `51da0c23`, 11.2 h into one `ela/kindergarten` cell: **874 calls / 14,031,365 ms.** ⭐ **Second only to `_teachHebbian` (15,786,751 ms) but across 238× FEWER CALLS** — so on a per-call basis it is far and away the costliest thing she does. One in-flight invocation, nested `_teachSentenceStructure → _teachConcreteSentences → _teachAssociationPairs`, had run **2.24 h** while `phaseWork` read `done 1 / total 5`. ⛔ **`DREAM_ART_WEIGHT_MIN_GAP_MS` (`chat.js:2307`) bounds ONLY the art caller** — confirmed working, `skippedRate` 2,262 and climbing. **The curriculum's own call is unbounded, and that is where the hours are now.** ⚠ **DO NOT simply add a gap limit to the curriculum caller.** The art caller is a *side effect* of drawing and is safely paced; the curriculum caller **is the teaching** — rate-limiting it does not save work, it deletes curriculum, which is the `STRUCTURE_DOSE` class of change and needs a **RE-PRICE (`corpus × reps × scale × visits`) written down first** per the standing law. ⭐ **Establish WHERE the 16 s/call goes before choosing a lever** — the honest first move is a per-caller and per-phase tally on that method, since the whole `ARTHOG` investigation was slowed by there being **no per-caller counter** and having to infer attribution from two correlated samples. ⚠ **A slow phase is NOT a latched phase and this row must not be re-read as a hang:** `curriculum.activePhase` changing between two state samples proves the stack is live (it moved `_teachHebbian` → `_teachAssociationPairs`), and `cellSubPhases` advanced **+2,592 in 11.5 min**. ⚠ **Related and still climbing: `lateral.activeSum` 979,773,384 at ~550k/min**, with `KI-29`'s 1.06 B line ~2.4 h out — **re-measure it rather than quoting this figure**, and note an earlier estimate of 3.2 h came from too short a window. ⭐ **The generalisable rule this earned: bounding one caller of a shared hot method is fixing an INSTANCE. Ask who else calls it before claiming the class is closed.**

## SUBJRETIRE — subjects retire at the correct grade, but only once they have actually been trained — filed 2026-08-27

Gee (verbatim): *"you have the word!those subject can be retired at the correct grade once they have been trainined"*

Answering the `cs`-at-college fork. ⭐ **The ruling carries a CONDITION, and the condition is the whole design:** *"once they have been trainined"* — retirement is gated on the **ledger**, not on a grade number alone. A subject that never actually ran must NOT be silently dropped.

- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-27 — shipped: `SUBJECTS_RETIRED_AT` (nine tracks at `grade12`), `subjectsOwedAt(grade, passedCells)`, and `ledgerFloorIdx()` extracted so the boot resolver and the retirement test share ONE floor rule. College 19 offered → **10 owed against 10 runners**; grad/phd 20 → 11. Verified 11/11 edge cases — an empty/null/undefined ledger retires NOTHING (a fresh brain has trained nothing), and with `pe/grade12` missing `pe` stays rostered at college1 AND phd while trained `music` retires. Bundle rebuilt same commit; a `GOTCHA.9`-class dead `typeof` guard removed from `_heldBack`. ⚠ **NOT closed by this row: `genered`/`cstheory`/`cssystems` enter at college1 with no grad/phd runner (11 owed vs 8) — same class of decision, and not retired on my own authority.** Full entry: FINALIZED §2026-08-27 SUBJRETIRE. **ORIGINAL FILING:** **⏳ `SUBJRETIRE.1` — `SUBJECTS_RETIRED_AT` + a ledger-gated roster, so the nine K-12 tracks stop being rostered at college while remaining impossible to skip untaught.** ⛔ **The problem:** `subjectsForGrade` is **purely additive with no retirement mechanism** — every track ever introduced stays rostered forever, so the college roster is **19 subjects against 10 runners** and grad/PhD is **20 against 8**. The nine with no college runner: `pe · music · health · language · cs · civics · economics · psychology · ap`. ⭐ **All nine retire at `grade12`** — they are K-12 courses, and `GRADE_ORDER` puts `college1` directly after `grade12`, where a separate roster (`major`/`genered`/`cstheory`/`cssystems`, then `research`) takes over. That matches real school: high school ends and college has different classes. ⛔ **THE TRAP THIS MUST NOT REPEAT — `WALKORDER.1`:** Grade 1 finished with PE/Music/Health **never taught**, because a subject was skipped without clearing `allPassedThisGrade`. **A retirement that fires on grade number alone re-creates that bug by design** — an untrained subject would vanish from the roster and its debt would disappear with it. **So retirement requires a ledger hit on `<subject>/grade12`; without it the subject stays rostered and still owed.** ⚠ **And it must inherit the ledger-floor discipline from `BOOTORDER`:** `passedCells` recording postdates the pre-K era, so a terminal grade **below the ledger's own floor** counts as trained (unrecorded history is done, not owed) — otherwise a long-trained brain keeps all nine rostered forever, which is the same class of bug pointing the other way. ⚠ **`subjectsForGrade(grade)` stays PURE and unchanged** — it answers *"what subjects exist at this grade"*, which is a different question from *"what still owes work"*; a second explicitly-named function answers the second. ⛔ **Not one function with an optional ledger argument** — that is a capability-degradation branch (`feedback_no_fallbacks_law`) where the same call silently means two things. ⚠ **RE-PRICE: this REMOVES cells from the walk, so it shortens it and weakens no gate** — `corpus × reps × scale × visits` is untouched per subject; the roster simply stops growing past `grade12`.
